# Native Linux isolation v1.81.0 work breakdown (Personal bindings + all connectors)

## Status 2026-11-09

States: **done** = merged on `feat/iso181-w12-20261108`, tests written; **done-security-reviewed** = merged after an independent review gate (NO-GO findings fixed and re-reviewed); **gated-off** = merged but hidden by default; **needs real host** = code/scripts exist, acceptance needs root on Ubuntu 22.04/24.04 or Debian 12 or real provider accounts; **open** = in progress. `docs/architecture/native-linux-isolation-connectors-plan.md` and the code. [SEC] = needs independent
security review. Most code is `#[cfg(target_os="linux")]`: compile/test on Linux only.

## Decisions
- D-1 Personal employee creation: no path creates one today (`duduclaw-migration/src/import_manifest.rs:173`
  stages proposals only from import; importer maps legacy bindings to `shared:` at
  `duduclaw-import/src/authority.rs:207`; `binding_plan` accepts only `shared:` at
  `handlers/migration_authority_bindings.rs:17`; `agents_create.rs` has no allocation path).
  Proposed: admin-only `isolation.employee_propose` with `binding_kind` + `owner_id` through the existing
  approval → allocation flow (A4).
- D-2 One connector account store: `ConnectorCatalog` (`isolation_connectors.rs:14-55`) becomes the only
  production store with binding + generation; standalone `BindingStore` stays a test double.
- D-3 Google sources: bridge supports Personal + Home OAuth; delegate / service-account refused at
  `google_identity/isolation_bridge.rs:76-78`; Apps Script has no connector tool.
- D-4 29 non-connector tools without production selectors (`mcp_isolation_parity_tests.rs:97-105`:
  file_read, execute_program, web_extract, …) are outside A–G.
- D-5 (E4, new rule) Google tenant-source subject in protected mode. Tenant sources are the existing admin
  config only: domain-wide delegation = sealed `protected/google-service-account.sealed` + the employee's
  `agent.toml [google] identity = "delegate:<S>"`; service account = `config.toml
  [integrations.google_service_account]` (`key_file` + `subject = S`) on the `home` identity. Both are served
  only through the Shared employee's own SharedTenant Google route of its pinned tenant. The subject S comes
  only from that config (never employee arguments, env or run metadata; argument keys that name an identity
  are refused) and is admitted only if, comparing normalized emails by exact equality with users.db:
  (a) S ∈ `[integrations.google_identity] service_mailboxes` AND no users.db account has email S ⇒ Shared
  action, any requester the Shared employee's authority admitted (Human or System); or (b) delegate only:
  exactly one users.db account has email S, it is Active, and the authenticated requester (capability
  principal + verified run association) is that account by exact user id ⇒ Personal-like action. Everything
  else refuses (undesignated non-person S, a person's S for anyone else or for System, ambiguous S, a person
  listed as a service mailbox, a service account acting as a person). Personal employees never use tenant
  sources. Tools are limited to `[integrations.google_identity] tenant_scopes` (each must be a minted
  scope; empty ⇒ status only). No home fallback: `fallback = "home"` on a delegate, or an Apps Script bridge
  beside the service account, refuses.

## Owner decisions (2026-11-08)
- D-1: Personal protected employee is requested by the staff member (own account page) and approved by an Admin through the existing approval → allocation flow. A4 becomes `me.isolation.request_personal_employee` (self, owner = caller) + admin approval; dashboard employee creation in protected mode goes through the same proposal path.
- D-2: ConnectorCatalog is the single production store.
- D-3: delegate and service-account Google sources are in scope (E4).
- D-4: the 29 non-connector tools are in scope; a separate breakdown (package H) is required.
- Owner decision 2026-11-08: Personal→Shared delegation requires an owner approval card. A delegation from a Personal employee's live run to a Shared employee is not queued until the Personal employee's owner approves an `ApprovalBroker` card (kind `isolation_personal_delegation`, decided on the dashboard by that owner only); denial or expiry means it is not sent; the approval is single-use and bound to (source employee id, target employee id, task digest). Shared→anything is unchanged.

- P1 [SEC] Personal data visibility for team grants, admin reads and cross-agent management tools (SR2 finding 5, deferred from the SR2 fix round). A Personal employee's data (sessions, memory, wiki, task text, files, approval payloads) can still be read through paths that were built for Shared employees: dashboard team grants (Manager/Viewer bindings and team rosters), Admin read RPCs, and cross-agent management MCP tools (`list_agents`, `agent_status`, `task_status`, inspect/update/remove tools). Fix direction: one shared predicate in the gateway, `personal_reader_allowed(home, agent, caller)` backed by the A2 lifecycle, that allows only the lifecycle owner (fresh users.db read) for a Personal employee; apply it at every read site that today checks `has_agent_access`/role, including list/get filtering (hide, not just refuse) so listing ⇔ access holds; Admins get metadata only (label, owner, status) unless an explicit, audited break-glass with step-up; team grants and `reports_to`/department rosters skip Personal employees; cross-agent MCP tools refuse a Personal target unless the caller is that employee. Tests: one per read surface (team grant, admin read, each management tool) for owner allowed / non-owner refused / admin metadata only.

## Infrastructure and acceptance
- Native root on Ubuntu 22.04 (192.168.0.45), 24.04, Debian 12:
  `DUDUCLAW_ISO_DISPOSABLE=1 cargo test -p duduclaw-spawnd -- --ignored iso_root_ --test-threads=1`;
  `DUDUCLAW_ISO_DISPOSABLE=1 cargo test -p duduclaw-gateway --test iso_personal_chain -- --ignored iso_root_e2e_ --test-threads=1 --nocapture`.
- Provider test accounts: Google Workspace test tenant (2 users Personal, Home, delegate/SA), Notion,
  GitHub org/repo, disposable Odoo, Postgres/MySQL read-only user, Discord/LINE/Telegram bots, search key.
- Matrix (fixture alice owner, bob; employees alice-personal = Personal(alice), team = Shared):
  1 owner gmail_search ok†; 2 bob binding refused / forced ⇒ personal_binding_conflict†; 3 team Personal
  tool only via explicit home fallback; 4 member removed mid-run ⇒ next call refused ≤1 s†; 5 tenant uuid
  replaced ⇒ all refused; 6 delete/recreate same label ⇒ old capability + receipts refused†; 7 revoke
  between selection and send ⇒ no request, uncertain; 8 revoke during Google refresh ⇒ uncertain, next page
  refused; 9 revoke between Notion pages; 10 gateway+spawnd restart ⇒ old capability refused†; 11 same-name
  relink ⇒ new id; 12 missing factory/provider ⇒ connector_setup_required†; 13 background after password
  reset ⇒ refused; 14 Odoo forbidden / DB non-SELECT / unbound channel recipient refused before credential;
  15 live smoke per provider family. († also on 24.04 and Debian 12.)

## Plan-doc corrections
Package A files are `duduclaw-migration/src/allocation.rs` + spawnd allocation (not capability.rs); tenant
pin lives only in gateway `apply.db`; no Personal creation path exists; "principal generation" is the global
`acl_generation` (`duduclaw-auth/src/isolation.rs:75`); `install_boot_sources` has no caller and
BootSources holds per-employee providers as singletons (API change needed); Odoo/channel executors exist
only in the CLI crate; no production catalog file; Notion/GitHub are home-wide only; RecordAuthority has
`deny_unknown_fields`; old acceptance.sh is obsolete; 29 non-connector production-selector gaps unlisted.

## Estimate
Coding 4–5 working days (2 waves/day); security reviews +3–5; infrastructure acceptance 3–5.
Optimistic 10, expected 14, pessimistic 20 working days.

## Follow-ups found during implementation
- From B2: `boot::record_authorization` pins the A2 lifecycle before policy/catalog I/O and passes that same lifecycle into capability construction; `human_authorization` also reads the lifecycle instead of inventing Shared. Both paths issue `lifecycle.binding`, recheck it after issuance I/O, and re-read users.db with B1's `personal_facts` (including mirror/direct binding/member count); lifecycle Unavailable stays Unavailable, Denied/Stale/Tombstoned become Revoked. The B2 assignment explicitly refuses Personal record-backed work (`HostTurn.background.is_some()`), even when it is represented as Human(owner), overriding the earlier B1 follow-up's background assumption; B3/C2/F1 must preserve that refusal unless the owner changes the contract. C2/F1: keep the lifecycle pinned across any added provider/catalog I/O, with final lifecycle and principal rechecks before returning the authorization. `authority::lifecycle_refusal` is now `pub(super)` to share the error mapping. Seven issuer tests were added in `boot_personal_tests.rs` using real stores and a fake root allocator; build/tests/fmt were not run per delegation. Existing `authorization_tests.rs` and CLI `mcp_isolation_parity_tests.rs` use synthetic snapshots without confirmed lifecycle stores/root ReadBack, so their successful-issuer fixtures need migration before the builder suite can pass; those files are outside B2's authorized edit scope. The record-backed test fixture also needs the new receipt fields when B3 lands.
- From D2: `mcp_oauth::refresh_token` (`mcp_oauth.rs:673`) uses `reqwest::Client::new()` which follows redirects; build it with `redirect(Policy::none())` and make `get_client_config` distinguish read errors from absence (owner: C4/E3, whichever edits `mcp_oauth` first). Notion has no enable flag: missing flag = allowed, explicit `[integrations] notion = false` = kill switch.
- From A1: `Specification.binding` is `Option<EmployeeBinding>` (legacy proposals have none); Shared carries `tenant_id` from the proposal — A2 must check it equals the tenant pinned in `apply.db`. Import validator now rejects Employee proposals with binding fields.
- From A3: `remove_personal_agent_in_transaction` also deletes all bindings for the agent and bumps the ACL generation. Triggers make two existing paths fail for Personal agents: offboarding with transfer (`org_write.rs` `move_bindings`) and `create_member_at_epoch` binding to a Personal agent. A2/A4 must define offboarding of a Personal owner (disable/tombstone the employee through the lifecycle path, then remove the mirror) and give these paths a clear refusal message. Mirror has no FK to `users`.
- From A2: `crate::isolation_lifecycle` is the read model (`read_lifecycle`, `recheck_with`; `resolve_confirmed_employee` delegates to it). The Personal mirror is written by the Employee apply path (`save_confirmed_receipt`) in its own users.db transaction, committed while the apply.db writer lock is held and before the confirmed row; a mirror failure refuses the step. `tombstone_personal_employee` (admin handover) marks the apply.db row disabled and then removes the mirror. Open: (1) no root action disables an Applied allocation, so the tombstone is gateway-side only; spawnd keeps the UID/storage and refuses a new live allocation with the same label until it gets one (owner: spawnd/A1). (2) Applying a Personal employee records the mirror but does not bind the owner in `user_agent_bindings`; B1 requires member count 1, so A4 must bind the owner. (3) The tombstone has no RPC yet: A4 adds it with admin + step-up. (4) Offboarding a Personal owner without a transfer is still allowed; the lifecycle then reads Denied (owner not Active) until an admin tombstones it. (5) `readback_shared_employee` now refuses Personal employees, so the UserAgentBinding/DataGrant/Schedule/Channel migration categories never target one.
- From C3: F1 must call `isolation_connector_lifecycle::install_credential_material(vault)` with the same `AccountCredentialVault` the executors use; without it revoke still fences the route but drops no in-process material. Admin step-up reuses the migration dashboard proof (`UserDb::migration_step_up`; `isolation.connector_step_up` is a fourth method writing the same proof). Shared routes always bind the users.db installation tenant (`duduclaw_auth::isolation::read_isolation_tenant`); Personal owners must be Active, logged in, no forced password change. No dashboard page yet (F4 or follow-up).
- From D1: account lists come from `plan_connector_accounts` (own Personal/SharedTenant routes + SharedHome routes of the factory's `home_binding` for Personal employees and same-tenant Shared employees). Database/computer/channel routes also need per-account provisioning details (`ConnectorAccountDetails`: source name / website credential id / channel route + token); no production store exists yet — C3 must write one alongside `isolation.connector_provision`, and F1 must supply it plus `home_binding` (tenant from `apply.db`). The CLI Odoo/channel executors are bound per employee provider, so D3 supplies `OdooExecutorFactory`/`ChannelExecutorFactory` (with declared tool coverage), not fixed executors. A provider with no route is registered as a refusing adapter (`Unavailable`); F3 can surface it as `connector_setup_required`. `InstalledConnectorProviders` does not pin the binding (selection still checks it per call); F1 should rebuild on binding change.
- From the post-merge fix (5c485cf1a+): duduclaw-import's pinned `session/tasks/cron-v1793.sql` catalogs now describe v1.80 stores, so a v1.79.3 home cannot import those three stores. Required before release: accept a set of known schema versions (v1.79.3 and v1.80.x), each with its own pinned catalog and column mapping; A2A-owned goals stay excluded; cron actor columns never read. (Task M1, owner: migration.)
- From B1: `GatewayAuthority` takes the employee principal only from `isolation_lifecycle` (fresh `read_lifecycle_with` at prepare, `recheck_with` against the lifecycle pinned in `PreparedAuthority` on every recheck; Unavailable ⇒ `Unavailable`, Denied/Stale/Tombstoned ⇒ `Revoked`). The grant binding must equal the lifecycle binding (else `BindingMismatch`), so until B2 replaces the hard-coded Shared at `boot.rs:353` every Personal-employee run is refused at prepare. B2 must issue `lifecycle.binding` and apply `authority::personal_facts` (now `pub(super)`) to its ingress snapshot before and after I/O. Personal contract (exact comparisons, also on every `check_epoch` users.db re-read): `Principal::Human` user == lifecycle owner == snapshot human, `personal_owner == Some(owner)`, `agent_member_count == 1` and the owner has a direct binding; `Principal::Background` is refused before any I/O. A4 must bind the owner on apply (unbound owner ⇒ refused). A non-admin principal without access still fails in `read_isolation_authority` and surfaces as `Unavailable` from `identity` (pre-existing). Shared is unchanged except that `check_epoch` now also refuses when a Personal mirror appears under the label. C2: `authorize_connector` should start from `self.identity(.., None)` (it enforces all of the above) before the catalog route check. Background records keep running as `Human(owner)` via `record_authorization`, so B3 needs no Background principal for Personal employees. Tests use an injected root (`GatewayAuthority.root`, production `RootAllocator`); F1 needs no change.
- From B3: `RecordAuthority` gains `binding: Option<EmployeeBinding>` + `allocation: Option<ProposalBinding>` (both `#[serde(default)]`, so pre-v1.81 receipts still parse under `deny_unknown_fields`). Both `None` = legacy: `store::load` returns it, but `recheck_receipt` refuses it (Revoked) before any store read, `store::save` refuses it, and the next scheduler tick files one new card (text says re-approval after upgrade) whose receipt replaces it; only one of the two set, or a Personal binding, is `Invalid`. Capture and `record_delegation` read `isolation_lifecycle::read_lifecycle_with` (Unavailable ⇒ Unavailable, Denied/Stale/Tombstoned ⇒ Revoked) before the reuse short-circuit; a Personal target files no card (`RequestError::PersonalEmployee`, `PERSONAL_REFUSAL`). Recheck compares employee, label, allocation and binding with the live lifecycle, requires Shared, and checks the owner snapshot tenant = recorded tenant. `request_record_from_settings` now returns `RequestError` (`user_message()`); `refusal_reason()` (task-local, never a grant) is shown by cron (skip note) and reminders (Personal ⇒ failed, legacy ⇒ stays Pending); the dispatcher queue path (`pending_isolation_approval`) does not read it yet. A delegation *from* a Personal employee's live run to a Shared target is still allowed (only the target runs in background); B1/SR2 should confirm. Not tested here: full `recheck_receipt` happy path (needs users.db snapshot + policy + material + approvals) and the request/card flow (needs `ApprovalBroker`); G2 should cover legacy re-approval and matrix 6/13 end to end.
- From A4: approvals use the existing Employee proposal path in `migration-confirmations.db` (approval card = `migration.confirmations.approve`, Admin step-up), not `approval::ApprovalBroker` (agent HITL with TTL auto-deny). `me.isolation.request_personal_employee` stages rev 1 = Pending `Personal(caller)` without `settings`, which `EmployeeAuthority::validate_current` refuses; the Admin's `isolation.employee_propose {request_id}` (step-up) writes the product config through `create_agent_files` (inert until allocated) and stages rev 2 with `settings` (supersedes rev 1), then the Admin approves it on the card. Display names live in `apply.db` `personal_employee_requests` (presentation only; owner/status always from the proposal). The confirmation store is created on first use if a protected home never migrated. `record_personal_mirror` now binds the owner (Owner access) in the same users.db transaction via `duduclaw_auth::db::bind_personal_owner_in_transaction` (A2 (2)). `isolation.employee_tombstone {label, employee_id}` + `isolation.employee_list` added (A2 (3)); A2 had already made the offboarding/`create_member_at_epoch` refusals coded (`personal_employee_handover_required`, `member_agent_personal`). `agents.create` refuses in protected mode. Open: other creators that bypass `agents.create` (MCP `create_agent` in the CLI, onboarding/template/import paths, if any write `agents/<id>` directly) are not yet refused in protected mode; disk quota is a fixed 1 GiB; the dashboard page (request form, admin prepare/tombstone) is still to do.
- New task C6 [SEC] (from D1): a per-account connector settings store for database source names, website/computer-login credential ids and channel routes, written by C3 provision (admin) and read by `ConnectorAccountDetails`; F1 passes it with `home_binding` (tenant pinned in apply.db). Owner-facing admin page for isolation connectors (C3 RPCs incl. `isolation.connector_step_up`) is a new surface: flow → design canvas → implementation (task F5).
- From SR1 (security review, two High findings in `isolation_lifecycle.rs`, fixed; lifecycle API signatures unchanged): (1) Every lifecycle read/recheck of a Personal employee now applies the full `owner_eligible` rule (Active, non-empty `last_login`, `must_change_password = 0`), not just `status`. Tenant, Personal mirror and owner row come from ONE users.db read transaction via the new `duduclaw_auth::read_isolation_authority_in(&tx, ..)`, which refuses autocommit connections; `read_isolation_authority` wraps it. `validate_proposed_binding` uses one transaction as well. (2) The three authority stores (`users.db`, `migration-authority/apply.db`, `migration-confirmations.db`) are opened on all lifecycle paths (read, recheck, validate, mirror write/remove, tombstone) only through `isolation_lifecycle::open_store` → `crate::fs_safe::sqlite::open_existing(dir, name, Access::{ReadOnly,ReadWrite})`. This extends the gateway's existing private-SQLite opener (`fs_safe/sqlite.rs`, already used by approval/workflow/discovery stores) rather than adding a second one, so it stays in the gateway (no other crate needs it). Checks: home canonicalized (only the macOS root aliases `/var` `/tmp` `/etc` are allowed); descriptor walk from `/` with lstat-at + `openat(O_NOFOLLOW)` through the DuDuClaw home to the store directory, with every ancestor owned by the effective uid or root and not group/other writable (a root-owned sticky dir is allowed only if the next entry is ours); the store directory owned by the euid with no group/other write; the database and any present `-journal`/`-wal`/`-shm` a regular file with nlink 1, owned by the euid, no group/other write; the directory and database dev/inode compared before and after the open (lstat, because SQLite owns its descriptors and opening a second fd would drop SQLite's POSIX locks on close); `SQLITE_OPEN_NOFOLLOW`; never created (no `CREATE` flag, no create-new step). Any violation is `Unavailable`. Non-Unix platforms refuse. The confirmation store is built from the hardened read-only connection with the new `ConfirmationStore::from_read_only_connection` (refuses a writable connection). Remaining (not in SR1 scope; owner: A4 or whoever next edits `migration_authority_employee.rs`): `save_confirmed_receipt` and `current_tenant` there still open `apply.db`/`users.db` by pathname; they should move to `open_existing`.

- From D3: CLI installs `CliOdooExecutorFactory` / `CliChannelExecutorFactory` before `start_gateway` via one-shot `isolation_connector_boot::install_connector_executor_factories`. F1 must read `connector_executor_factories()` and pass `.odoo` / `.channel` into `GatewayConnectorFactoryParts::{odoo_executors,channel_executors}`. Missing factories report `available() == false` and leave refusing adapters without reading provider credentials. Executors retain only the supplied employee provider (channel may contain several employee-authorized routes). Added the three requested tests; Claude must run them on Linux.

- From E2: `google_identity::isolation_routes::GoogleAccountRoutes::read(home, agent, employee, binding, catalog)` derives Google routes only from the live A2 lifecycle and active C1 catalog rows of that exact binding. Personal maps contain only the lifecycle owner and never a home fallback; Shared accepts only same-tenant SharedHome/SharedTenant. Missing or ambiguous Google routes return `GoogleRoutesError::SetupRequired` (`connector_setup_required: google`); unreadable catalog/lifecycle returns `Unavailable`. F3 can consume the typed error/code; the current MCP `VerificationError` has no setup-required variant, so the bridge maps setup-required to `Unavailable`. The bridge pins the full route (including generation) with its source proof, and C1 independently rechecks its SelectedConnector generation at use time. E3 must advance/revoke the same catalog before changed consent/source material can be used; refresh of the same source must preserve the logical proof. E4 must retain exact lifecycle-binding and requester checks when adding delegate/service-account adapters (still refused here), and must not restore Personal-to-home fallback. F1: `GoogleIsolationBridge::new` and `GoogleBridgeFactory::bridge` now take `Arc<ConnectorCatalog>` instead of a caller-built account map; `isolation_connector_boot.rs` passes the factory's same catalog. Route/lifecycle reads are live and blocking (SQLite/root IPC), so preserve the authority blocking boundary; rebuild the bridge after a legitimate route/source-generation change. Multiple Google accounts per binding need an explicit catalog-to-source mapping before they can be enabled; E2 refuses ambiguity. Added the six requested route tests plus lifecycle-change/unreadable, ambiguity and bridge-generation/relink coverage; not run by Codex (Linux builder validation belongs to Claude).

- From C6: per-account connector details live in a second table `isolation_connector_details` in the catalog file `isolation-connectors.db` (not a sibling store), so route + details commit in one transaction at provision, revoke/`advance` delete them in the transaction that moves the generation, and the reader takes route + details from one read snapshot (`ConnectorCatalog::route_details`). The table is opened only through `ConnectorCatalog::open` (private-SQLite opener + 0600 checks) and is schema-verified exactly like the routes table; a pre-C6 catalog gets the empty table on open. Rows are keyed by the opaque account id, pinned by trigger to the route's active generation, `binding_id` and provider, never updatable, deletable only after revoke/generation move, and also pinned to one protected employee (`EmployeeId` hex + agent id from `isolation_lifecycle::read_lifecycle`); another employee seeing the same route skips it. References only: database `source`, computer `credential` (website credential id), channel `channel` + `scope` + `status_label` + `extra_id` + `token_ref` ∈ {`none` (self-configuring channel, SharedHome only), `home` (`config.toml [channels] <channel>_bot_token`, shared routes only), `agent` (the pinned agent's own `agent.toml [channels.<ch>] bot_token`, no `reports_to` walk)}; tokens are resolved in memory at build. D1 trait changed: `ConnectorAccountDetails::detail(&Route) -> Option<ProvisionedDetail{detail, pin}>` + `current_pin(&AccountId)`; the factory wraps database/computer/channel selection in a fence that re-reads the pin (generation, binding, employee, references) at every selection recheck. Missing details for any route of a provider (or a token reference that no longer resolves) ⇒ refusing adapter, `InstalledConnectorProviders::refusals()` = `ProviderRefusal::SetupRequired` (`reason()` = `connector_setup_required`; `setup_required()` lists providers); no route ⇒ `NoRoute`; details store unreadable ⇒ whole build `Unavailable`, use-time recheck `Unavailable`. **F1** must pass `details: Arc::new(CatalogAccountDetails::new(home, catalog.clone()))` built over the SAME `Arc<ConnectorCatalog>` it passes to `build`, plus `home_binding`; F1 should rebuild providers after any provision/revoke/advance (a build never picks up new details). **F3** reads `refusals()`/`setup_required()` of the installed providers for `connector_setup_required{provider}`. **F5** (admin page) must collect for `isolation.connector_provision` with provider database/computer/channel a `details` object: `employee` (agent id of a confirmed protected employee; RPC resolves it through the lifecycle), and per provider exactly — database: `source` (pick from `[db_sources]` names granted in that agent's `capabilities.db_sources`), computer: `credential` (pick from website credentials whose allowed agents include the agent), channel: `channel`, `scope` (exact chat / settings scope / pairing subject, no `*`), `token_ref` (`agent` for Personal routes; `home`/`agent` for shared; `none` only for wecom/dingtalk/googlechat/teams SharedHome), optional `status_label` (default = channel) and `extra_id`. Never a token field. `details` on other providers is refused. Refusals are specific (`details_required`, `reference_unknown`, `reference_foreign`, `employee_mismatch`, …) in the audit row and the zh-TW message. Open: (1) one employee per detail-requiring route (Shared employees each need their own route); (2) no way to re-attach details to an existing account after `advance` — admin revokes and provisions again (C4 only advances Google/Notion/GitHub routes today); (3) network-backed `secret://` channel tokens cannot be resolved by the synchronous build and read as setup-required; (4) channel `scope` is validated by shape + `ChannelAccount::new`, not against a registry of known chats (none exists); (5) `connector_list` does not yet report per-route detail state for F5. Merged with D3: when `channel_executors.available()` is false the factory never reads channel details, builds no channel provider and reports `NoRoute` (not `SetupRequired`); Odoo keeps D3's `available()` gate on `plan.single`.

- From SR2 (security review NO-GO; fixes for findings 1–4 and 6–8, finding 5 deferred to new task P1): (1a) MCP `create_agent` is an intentional isolation refusal (`Refusal::ProposalRequired`, hidden and refused in the isolated adapter) and `handle_create_agent` refuses on every transport when `runtime_readiness::is_protected_linux()`, both pointing to the proposal path. (1b) `isolation.employee_propose` adopts an existing `agents/<label>` for a Personal proposal only with this flow's creation marker: apply.db `employee_dir_markers(label, owner_user_id, digest, created_at)`, written right after `create_agent_files` creates the directory, digest = SHA-256 over SOUL.md, CONTRACT.toml, agent.toml (absent/file, never followed through a symlink), the SKILLS tree listing + contents (bounded, no symlinks) and the label's org.toml entry; the retry must name the same owner and the digest must still match, re-checked again under the apply.db lock in `stage_prepared`; a live (not tombstoned) confirmed employee's directory is never re-proposed into (`LABEL_TAKEN`). A marker-write failure removes the just-created directory. Shared proposals keep the old "existing registry entry is proposed as is" rule. (1c) Every other writer of a new `agents/<id>` refuses in protected mode with `DIRECT_CREATE_REFUSED`: `create_template_with_skills` (covers `templates.create_agent`, company/bundled/library templates) and `experts.install` / `experts.install_builtin` / `experts.install_draft` (dispatch guard). Verified not to create agent directories: MCP `spawn_agent`/`spawn_ephemeral`, `agents.archive` (moves wiki pages), `device_backup`, `fork_migrate_rpc`, onboarding RPCs. Not covered: the operator terminal `duduclaw pack/expert install` run directly by the service account (no protected-mode signal in a plain shell) and whole-home `backup_restore` (operator restore, not an employee creation path). (2) Owner decision above: `isolation_background::personal_delegation` — `DelegationSource` carries the run's lifecycle binding; a Personal source builds a `Gate` (owner, source/target agent + employee id, task digest of the material `payload`); `admit` files one `ApprovalBroker` card (kind `isolation_personal_delegation`, 24 h TTL, ≤ 20 pending, self-notifying and dashboard-only, text names source, target and the task truncated with `truncate_chars(…, 200)`) and refuses with `OwnerApprovalPending`; the employee re-sends after approval and the approval is consumed in the same `isolation_background.db` transaction that stores the delegation receipt (`personal_delegation_consumed`), so it is single-use; only `decided_by = dashboard:<owner>` inside the card window counts; an owner denial within the window refuses (`OwnerDenied`) without filing a new card. The owner decides through the new `me.isolation.list_personal_delegations` / `me.isolation.decide_personal_delegation` (approve re-reads the lifecycle: caller must still own exactly the source employee id); `approvals.decide` refuses the kind for everyone. `record_delegation`/`record_bus_delegation` now return `DelegationError` (CLI shows `user_message()`). Not tested end to end: the live `record_delegation` path (needs the boot OWNER/live run association; G2 should cover it) and the owner RPC (needs a root ReadBack). (3) `ConfirmationStore` counts only open Employee proposals (latest revision pending/approved/applying/rejecting) toward `DEFAULT_EMPLOYEE_PROPOSAL_LIMIT`; staff requests are limited to 10 per user per 24 h (apply.db `personal_employee_request_log`, not reset by purge); new `isolation.employee_purge_requests` (Admin + step-up) calls the new `ConfirmationStore::purge_closed_employee` (Admin/MFA/step-up/CSRF session; only Employee proposals whose latest revision is Rejected/ExpiredRejected and that have no apply/effect/rotation rows; one `purged` audit event kept) for indexed staff requests and drops their index rows. Migration import limits are unchanged. (4) `tombstone_personal_employee` renames `agents/<label>` to `<home>/isolation-quarantine/<employee id hex>-<label>` (root 0700, owned by the euid; the moved directory chmod 0700) inside the apply.db writer transaction before the row is disabled; an existing target, a cross-filesystem move or any rename error is `Unavailable` and nothing is disabled; the marker row is deleted in the same transaction; a replay of an already-disabled row never moves a directory that appeared later. The RPC rescans the registry afterwards. (6) `prepare_propose` and `stage_prepared` hold the apply.db `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` lock (same as `request_personal`) across the open-label check; `stage_prepared` re-lists the store and refuses `LABEL_PENDING` before staging. (7) `fs_safe::sqlite::open_existing` now opens users.db in `boot::human_authorization_with`, `isolation_background.db` (read) and approvals.db (`check_approval`, `approval_pending`) in `isolation_background/store.rs`, and apply.db/users.db in `save_confirmed_receipt`/`current_tenant`; the receipt store write path uses the private create-or-open `fs_safe::sqlite::open` (0600). (8) `isolation_mcp::human_authorization` is `#[cfg(any(test, feature = "test-support"))]` (no non-test caller existed). Tests added (not run here; Linux builder): protected_mode_refuses_mcp_create_agent, propose_refuses_foreign_existing_dir_for_personal, propose_retry_accepts_own_marker, templates_create_refused_in_protected_mode (the RPC half only runs where `is_protected_linux()`), personal_to_shared_delegation_needs_owner_approval, shared_delegation_unchanged, employee_quota_counts_open_only + purge_closed_employee_removes_only_untouched_rejected_proposals (duduclaw-migration), staff_request_rate_limited (also covers the purge RPC), tombstone_quarantines_directory (also the fail-closed move), propose_label_lock_prevents_duplicates. Open: dashboard surfaces for the owner delegation cards and the purge button (F4/F5); the quarantine has no retention/cleanup tool yet. SR2 round 2 → round 3 (gate NO-GO on N1; N1 and the Lows fixed): (N1) the Personal delegation card now binds the FULL task text: `Gate::payload` = owner, source/target agent + employee id, `task` (exact text), `task_digest`, `delivery`; the owner-only `me.isolation.list_personal_delegations` returns `task` exactly as bound (via `personal_delegation::shown_task`: digest must match, ≤ `MAX_TASK_CHARS` = 4000, showable) plus `delivery`; a card failing that check is neither listed nor approvable (`decide` refuses it). `admit` refuses before any card: > 4000 chars ⇒ `DelegationError::TaskTooLong` (`TOO_LONG_TEXT` to the employee); text that `credential_detect::mask` would change, or control/bidi/zero-width formatting (except `\n` `\r` `\t` and ZWJ/ZWNJ) ⇒ `TaskNotShowable` (`NOT_SHOWABLE_TEXT`). The generic `summary` names only the two employees (no task text). F5 dashboard rendering of `task` remains out of scope. (N2) `material::delegation_resend_binding` (`DELEGATION_RESEND_FIELDS` = target, payload, reply_channel, lane, on_behalf_of, spawned; never turn/session/hop fields) is bound in the card (`delivery` = those fields minus `payload`, which is `task`); the resend must repeat them exactly; a material target ≠ the delegation target is `BindingMismatch`. (N3) open cards per source employee ≤ 5 (`MAX_PENDING_PER_SOURCE`), per owner ≤ 10 (`MAX_PENDING_PER_OWNER`), installation ≤ 100 (`MAX_PENDING`, was 20) ⇒ `TooManyPending` (`TOO_MANY_TEXT`); an existing approval is still consumed when capped. (N4) `approval_visibility::DashboardOnlyReader::PersonalOwnerOnly` for this kind: `approvals.list`/`approvals.get` and live approval pushes never show it, not even to an Admin (it was already self-notifying and undecidable there). (N5) `quarantine_agent_dir` opens the home with `SafeDir::open_nofollow`, `agents/` and `isolation-quarantine/` as no-follow child descriptors (root created 0700 by `mkdirat`, owner/mode checked on the descriptor) and moves with the new `SafeDir::rename_noreplace` (Linux `renameat2(RENAME_NOREPLACE)` via `SYS_renameat2`, macOS `renameatx_np(RENAME_EXCL)`, other Unix refuse); the moved directory is chmod 0700 through its descriptor (`SafeDir::set_mode`). No separate existence check remains; an existing target (even an empty directory), a symlinked `agents/` or quarantine root is `Unavailable` and nothing is disabled. Behaviour change: a home whose `agents/` is a symlink can no longer tombstone (fail closed). (7-residual) `isolation_employee_rpc.rs`: users.db (`resolve_binding`) and apply.db (`request_rows`, `confirmed_employees` read-only; `open_requests` read-write after `GatewayAuthority::new` created it) go through `fs_safe::sqlite::open_existing` (`open_apply`). (1b-residual) `employee_dirs::snapshot`: the adopted folder may hold only SOUL.md, CONTRACT.toml, agent.toml (now required), SKILLS/ and `.claude/` with only `settings.json` (now in the digest, tag `duduclaw-employee-dir-v2`; markers recorded with v1 no longer match, retry must recreate); anything else (IDENTITY.md, MEMORY.md, instructions, `.mcp.json`, …) ⇒ `FOREIGN_DIR`. A Personal proposal's `settings` are parsed from exactly the digested agent.toml bytes (`Snapshot::settings`: parse + the registry's `proactive`/`sticker` clamps), the live registry entry must be that directory with identical settings, and `stage_prepared` re-derives them from the snapshot checked under the apply.db lock (mismatch ⇒ `STALE`). (1c-u2) `backup_restore::stage_restore` refuses in protected mode (`is_protected_linux()`) with `PROTECTED_RESTORE_REFUSED` before any lock/read/write, and the boot swap of a restore staged earlier returns `RestoreSwapError::ProtectedMode` without moving anything (staging + marker kept). Documented: a whole-home restore could undo tombstones (confirmed row live again, mirror/binding back, directory back under `agents/`) and revive revoked proposals/approvals; protected installs use the bounded migration import. (1a-i) `mcp/agents.rs` comment: the env check is defence in depth; the disposition table is the gate. Tests added (not run here; Linux builder): personal_delegation_shown_text_equals_bound_text (handler, owner RPC), personal_delegation_oversized_task_refused, personal_delegation_resend_must_match_bound_fields, personal_delegation_cap_per_source, personal_delegation_card_hidden_from_admin_list (`approval_visibility`), tombstone_quarantine_rename_noreplace, adopt_folder_with_unlisted_entry_refused, backup_restore_refused_in_protected_mode, delegation_resend_binding_ignores_turn_and_session_only; updated personal_to_shared_delegation_needs_owner_approval and propose_retry_accepts_own_marker. SR2 round 3 → round 4 (NO-GO on N1/N4; fixed): (N1) `personal_delegation::showable` is now an allowlist rule per character (`hidden_at`): refused are every control except `\n` and `\t` (so `\r` is now refused too — behaviour change from round 3), every char of general category Cf/Co/Cn (via the existing `regex` dependency, `\p{Cf}\p{Co}\p{Cn}`; a failed table build refuses everything; Cs cannot occur in a `char`), and explicitly the tag characters U+E0000–E007F (all of plane 14), variation selectors U+FE00–FE0F, U+E0100–E01EF and Mongolian U+180B–180F, U+00AD, U+034F, U+061C, the Hangul fillers U+115F U+1160 U+3164 U+FFA0, U+17B4/17B5, U+180E, U+FFF9–FFFB, U+2028/2029, all bidi controls and BMP/plane-15/16 private use. Exceptions, single and anchored on both sides: ZWNJ/ZWJ between two letters of Arabic/Syriac/Indic (U+0600–077F, U+0870–08FF, U+0900–0DFF); ZWJ between two emoji (the emoji before may carry one VS16 — `🏳️‍🌈`, the only allowed adjacent pair); VS15/VS16 directly after an emoji, or after `0-9 # *` when followed by U+20E3. No new dependency. (N4) `approval::no_reminder` returns true for `isolation_personal_delegation`, so `maybe_remind` (poll and `expire_stale` paths) never claims a reminder slot for it; `approval_notify::push` also returns `None` for the kind (defence in depth). (Optional) `DELEGATION_RESEND_FIELDS` binds `upstream_unknown` too (7 fields; the card's `delivery` gains it). Tests added (not run here; Linux builder): tag_characters_refused, variation_selectors_refused_except_after_emoji, arabic_letter_mark_refused, soft_hyphen_and_fillers_refused, zwj_emoji_sequence_allowed, private_use_refused (personal_delegation_tests.rs), personal_delegation_card_never_reminded (approval/tests/simulation_cases.rs); updated personal_delegation_oversized_task_refused (`\r\n` → `\n`), personal_delegation_resend_must_match_bound_fields and delegation_resend_binding_ignores_turn_and_session_only (upstream_unknown).
- From C2: `GatewayAuthority` now implements both `BindingAuthority::authorize_connector` (own binding) and `authorize_product_home_connector` (explicit home fallback) through `authority::connector_with`, in this order: (a) `identity_with(.., None)` (fresh lifecycle read; all B1/B2 rules; Unavailable/Revoked/BindingMismatch as before); (b)+(c) one `ConnectorCatalog::route` read: inactive or generation ≠ the selection's pinned generation ⇒ `Revoked`; binding/kind ⇒ `BindingMismatch` unless Personal employee ↔ Personal route of the same owner, Shared employee ↔ SharedTenant route of its tenant or SharedHome route of this installation, and (home fallback) SharedHome route whose binding is the claimed home binding, which must be `Shared(users.db installation tenant)` (the lifecycle read has just required that tenant to equal the apply.db pin); a Shared employee may only name its own tenant as home; (d) SharedHome routes only: `ProductHome::connected(provider)`; production `ConfiguredProductHome` reads only `config.toml [integrations]` (google `google_workspace = true`, github `github = true`, notion not `false`; unreadable/malformed ⇒ `Unavailable`; other providers have no separate product connection, so the active SharedHome route of this installation is it; unknown provider ⇒ not connected). It deliberately opens no token/credential store or source lock, because `recheck_binding` can run under the Google source owner's locks; token presence stays with the credential providers (D2/E2) and disconnect must move the catalog (C4); (e) `needs_details` providers: `ConnectorAccountDetails::current_pin` must exist with the same account, the pinned generation, this employee and agent, else `Revoked`; then the route is re-read (must be the identical row) and users.db re-read (`current_facts`, same tenant/epochs/member rules). Malformed account id ⇒ `Invalid`; unknown id ⇒ `Revoked`; unreadable catalog/details/config/users.db/root ⇒ `Unavailable`. At the trait, `Unavailable`/`Invalid` map to `BindingError::Unavailable`/`Invalid`, everything else to `Denied`; `SelectedConnector` still reports every authority refusal as `BindingMismatch` (pre-existing, unchanged). **Trait change:** both connector methods of `duduclaw_mcp_boundary::bindings::BindingAuthority` take `generation: u64` (the route generation selection pinned); `SelectedConnector::recheck_binding` passes its pinned generation, and the standalone `Mutex<BindingStore>` test double ignores it (it has no generations; the catalog re-read after it still compares). Personal employees can now use SharedHome routes through the explicit home fallback (owner decision C, D1 plan); before C2 the default `Denied` refused them. **F1:** `GatewayAuthority::new` takes a 5th argument `catalog: Option<Arc<ConnectorCatalog>>`; `boot_protected` opens `ConnectorCatalog::open(home)` into `sources.connector_catalog` when none was installed and passes that same `Arc` (no catalog ⇒ every connector use `Unavailable`). F1 must install its catalog in `BootSources.connector_catalog` *before* `boot_protected` builds the authority, and pass that same `Arc` to the factory, `CatalogAccountDetails` and selection; the authority builds its own `CatalogAccountDetails` over the same `Arc`. Each check does one root ReadBack (identity) plus catalog/users.db reads, on top of `verify_live`; keep it on the blocking pool. Tests (`isolation_mcp/authority_connector_tests.rs`, a child of the B1 test module, real stores + root fixture): c2_personal_route_of_owner_authorized, c2_personal_route_of_other_owner_refused, c2_shared_tenant_route_wrong_tenant_refused, c2_shared_home_route_requires_connected_home, c2_revoked_route_refused, c2_generation_moved_refused, c2_detail_pin_changed_refused, c2_unreadable_catalog_unavailable; not compiled or run here (Linux builder). Open: (1) whether the product-home check must also prove token presence for google/notion/github (needs a lock-free presence read from `mcp_oauth`/Google home source, owner C4/E3); (2) whether odoo/search/model SharedHome routes need a product-connected read beyond their providers' own use-time config checks; (3) no end-to-end `SelectedConnector` + `GatewayAuthority` test yet (needs a constructible `SpawndBackend`; G1/G2).
- From C4: product credential events now move the catalog through new `crate::isolation_connector_events` (`Target` personal/personal_all/home, `Hint` Record/Leave/Cancel, `Reconnect` Same/Switch, `revoke_then` = fence → drop → fence again, `rotate`/`begin_rotation`+`Rotation::finish` = advance → publish → advance + relink). Catalog side is a child module of `isolation_connectors` (`isolation_connector_relink.rs`): scoped one-transaction `revoke_scope`/`advance_scope`/`relink_scope`/`cancel_relink_scope`/`active_in_scope`, plus a third catalog table `isolation_connector_relink` (account PK = revoked predecessor, `subject` = domain-separated SHA-256 of the provider subject — Google `sub`, or "home-installation" for home OAuth — state pending/relinked/cancelled, successor; schema-verified like C1/C6, created on open of an older catalog; triggers: insert only for an already revoked google/notion/github personal/shared_home route, pending → relinked/cancelled once, successor must be a different ACTIVE route with the predecessor's provider/kind/binding and succeed one hint, never deleted). A reconnect never reactivates an old id: it registers a NEW route (fresh id, generation above all, predecessor label) and marks the hint relinked. `ConnectorCatalog::revoke` (C3 admin revoke) of an already revoked id now cancels its pending hint, so an owner's reconnect never undoes an admin decision; route rows are unchanged by that. Mapping: Personal Google own disconnect / `invalid_grant` consent revoke (`google_personal/access.rs`, `meeting_access.rs` call `lifecycle::consent_revoked` before `mark_needs_reconnect`) ⇒ revoke `Personal(owner)` google routes + hint; admin-initiated disconnect (`actor != user`) ⇒ revoke, cancel hints; repeated own disconnect with nothing connected ⇒ Leave; `flow::finish` publishes through `lifecycle::publish_connection`: same `sub` or first connect ⇒ advance, publish, advance, relink hints of that `sub` and cancel hints of other subs; different `sub` while connected (account switch) ⇒ revoke + cancel first, new account stays `connector_setup_required` (C3 provisioning is admin-only, so no auto-provision); a disconnect that raced the publication ⇒ re-fence. Suspension/offboarding (`users.remove`/`users.offboard` → `lifecycle::disconnect(.., offboard=true)` → `owner_change::revoke_owner_routes_then`) ⇒ revoke EVERY `Personal(owner)` route of all providers and cancel hints; a restored owner needs a new provision. Owner reassignment of an employee needs no route change (routes are per person; Personal binding is fixed, handover = A2/A4 tombstone). Home OAuth (`mcp_oauth::remove_token` → `remove_home_credential`; `exchange_and_store` → `publish_home_credential`; Google home callback via `begin_home_rotation`) ⇒ same pattern on SharedHome routes of google/notion/github (any tenant); Slack/custom untouched. A fence failure refuses the event before material is touched (zh-TW / English message); a home without `isolation-connectors.db` changes nothing and the file is never created; an existing unsafe/unreadable catalog refuses. In-process material is dropped through new `isolation_connector_lifecycle::installed_material()` after the fence (needs F1's `install_credential_material`). D2 follow-up done: `refresh_token` uses `Client::builder().redirect(Policy::none())` and treats any 3xx as a failed refresh before parsing; new `mcp_oauth::read_client_config` → `Result<Option<_>>` (strict `home_clients::load_one`: duplicate rows, undecodable secret, gated Google row, changed file ⇒ `Err`); `get_client_config` keeps its `Option` signature (now = strict read `.ok().flatten()`, documented as "None = refuse") because ~30 callers all already refuse on `None`; the one permissive caller (`isolation_connector_native_home` GitHub refresh-endpoint pin) now uses `read_client_config` and refuses on `Err`. Tests (`isolation_connector_events_tests.rs`, `mcp_oauth/c4_tests.rs`): the eight requested c4_* tests; not run here (Linux builder). Open: (1) E3 must call `lifecycle::consent_revoked` from the other Personal refresh paths (`google_personal/oauth.rs`, `google_identity` effect/execute authorities) and fence SharedHome google (`events::fence(home, &Target::home("google"), Hint::Record(&RelinkSubject::home("google")), ..)`) on a Google home `invalid_grant` in `home_source/oauth_refresh_io.rs`; (2) home OAuth has no stable account subject, so a home reconnect with a DIFFERENT Notion/GitHub/Google account relinks too (admin action; revoke the successor via C3 if unwanted); (3) `exchange_code` still follows redirects (not in D2's ask; recommend `Policy::none()`); (4) `save_tokens` (whole-file save, no production caller) can drop notion/github rows without a fence; (5) F1/E2 must rebuild providers/bridge after a relink (new id); (6) F5's connector list may want to show pending relinks (`pending_relinks`).
- From SR3 (security gate passed; non-blocking findings fixed): (1) `isolation_connector_database::select_only_before_connect` (protected free-SQL path) now refuses any `\` inside a `'`/`"`/`` ` `` literal (MySQL `SELECT 'a\'' INTO OUTFILE …` bypass), PostgreSQL `E'…'` and `U&'…'` literals, and `$$…$$` / `$tag$…$tag$` dollar quoting, all as `Invalid` before connect; `''` escapes still pass. (2) Until E4 adds tenant credential sources, `GoogleAccountRoutes` admits only SharedHome (not SharedTenant) Google routes for Shared employees (a tenant-only catalog is `SetupRequired`), and C6 provision refuses channel `token_ref = home` on SharedTenant routes (`reference_foreign`); `home` is SharedHome-only. (3) D1/C6 trait changed again: `ConnectorAccountDetails::detail(&Route, EmployeeId, &str) -> DetailLookup::{Missing, Foreign, Ready(ProvisionedDetail)}`; `CatalogAccountDetails` compares the stored pin's employee/agent BEFORE resolving any token, so another employee's bot token is never decrypted into this build and its missing token cannot mark this employee's provider setup-required; the factory skips `Foreign` and treats a `Ready` detail pinned to another employee as `Invalid`. Remaining Info items for **F1**: (a) `install_credential_material` is never called on the production path yet; (b) vault material is keyed by account only, so it survives a catalog `advance` of that account — F1 must key it by account + generation (or drop it on advance) when wiring the install.
- From C5: `ConfirmationCategory::Connector` is now a built-in migration category (`handlers/migration_authority_connectors.rs`, `Plan::Connector`); the old "Connector refused" test now uses Role. This is the in-place case (a pre-isolation home switched to protected mode): the new admin RPC `isolation.connector_import_legacy` (Admin + migration step-up + CSRF, Enterprise-only gate) calls `connectors::import_legacy(home)`, which reads every legacy store FIRST (any unreadable/malformed one ⇒ `Storage`, nothing staged) and then stages one Pending Connector proposal per account into `migration-confirmations.db` (created like A4 if absent). Proposal id = first 16 bytes of domain-separated SHA-256 of the legacy source key, so a re-import is `unchanged`, a changed account supersedes only a still-Pending proposal, and a decided one (approved/applied/rejected/expired) is never re-staged (`closed`). Fields: `name` (label), `binding_kind` (`personal`|`shared`), `owner_id` (Personal only), `settings` = canonical JSON `{schema:1, source, provider, kind, details}`; no `credential`, no fingerprint, no URL/email/tenant/token. Mapping: `person_credentials/store.json` record whose `owner_id` is a users.db user ⇒ google `Personal(owner)` (`google_personal:<owner>`; an owner not in users.db is NOT proposed, counted `unproven_personal`, never downgraded to shared); visible home OAuth token for google/notion/github (new presence-only `mcp_oauth::stored_home_providers`, no decryption) ⇒ SharedHome (`home_oauth:<p>`); non-empty `config.toml [odoo]` ⇒ odoo SharedHome; `[db_sources.<name>]` granted BY NAME in a confirmed protected employee's `capabilities.db_sources` ⇒ database SharedHome + details `{employee, source}` (wildcards skipped); website credential allowed for a confirmed employee ⇒ computer + `{employee, credential}`, `Personal(owner)` only if `created_by` is user `owner` and the employee is that owner's Personal employee, else SharedHome; `agent_bindings.json` channel user → confirmed employee ⇒ channel + `{employee, channel, scope = bound user, status_label = channel, token_ref}` with `agent` (employee's own `[channels.<ch>]` bot; Personal employee ⇒ `Personal(owner)`, Shared ⇒ SharedTenant) else `none` (self-configuring channel with home marker) or `home` (`[channels] <ch>_…token`), both SharedHome; no bot ⇒ nothing. Employees come from `isolation_lifecycle::read_lifecycle_with` (no apply.db ⇒ none; Unavailable ⇒ refuse; Denied/Stale/Tombstoned ⇒ skipped). Approval (`validate_fresh`) and every apply step re-derive the proposal from the live legacy stores (must be exactly what the import would stage, else `Stale`), resolve the binding via new `isolation_lifecycle::connector_route_binding` (one users.db read tx; Personal owner eligible = Active + logged in + no forced change; shared = installation tenant) and the employee via the lifecycle, then run C6 `validate_request`. Publish goes through new `isolation_connector_lifecycle::provision_migrated` = C3 `provision` (same detail validation, same catalog transaction, same readback, audit `origin: migration`) plus a provenance row in a fourth catalog table `isolation_connector_migrations` (new child module `isolation_connector_origin.rs`: operation PK + digest + UNIQUE account, insert only for the route inserted in the same tx (active, newest generation), never updated/deleted; schema-verified, created on open of an older catalog). Re-apply of the same operation returns the route it created (no second id); a revoked migrated route stays revoked (readback `Revoked`, nothing minted). `MigratedRoute::Conflict` (new `LifecycleError::Conflict`): any route (active or revoked) of the same provider/kind/binding for a non-detail provider (a revoked one's successor only via C4 relink or admin C3), or an active route with an identical details record. Compensation/reject after approval revokes exactly the provenance route via C3 `revoke` (fence then material) and reads the fence back; no provenance ⇒ nothing to undo; reject/expire before apply creates nothing (staging never opens the catalog). `migration.confirmations.list` items gain `connector` = `{provider, label, kind, binding, owner_user_id, details, legacy_source}` for the card. Tests (`handlers/migration_authority_connectors_tests.rs`, `#![cfg(unix)]`, real stores, fixture root ReadBack): c5_import_stages_connector_proposals, c5_apply_provisions_route_and_details_via_c3, c5_reject_creates_nothing, c5_personal_requires_proven_owner, c5_reapply_idempotent, c5_no_secrets_in_proposal, c5_unreadable_legacy_config_refuses; not compiled or run here (Linux builder). Open: (1) the cross-install `--migrate-from` path still stages only the duduclaw-import worker's `requires_reentry` Connector notices (old secrets are not migrated); after re-entry the admin runs `isolation.connector_import_legacy` on the new home — whether the import worker should emit C5-shaped proposals is an owner decision (old user ids ≠ new ids, so ownership proof would need an email map); (2) channel `scope` is the legacy bound external user id (a DM chat for Telegram/LINE); Discord/Slack user ids are not channel ids and will fail C6 validation at approval — group-chat scopes have no legacy source; (3) search/model accounts and remote MCP servers are not mapped; (4) a Personal Google proposal only creates the route — the owner's re-consent publishes through C4 (`publish_connection` advances it); (5) dashboard UI for the import button and the connector card (F4/F5); (6) a Personal employee's database/home-bot routes are SharedHome (home-wide sources), reachable only via the explicit home fallback (C2).
- From E3: refresh / consent-revoke recheck. (1) Personal Google: every refresh path now fences on `invalid_grant` through C4's `lifecycle::consent_revoked` (catalog first): legacy `access::resolve` and `meeting_access` (already, then `mark_needs_reconnect`), and now the pinned path `access::resolve_pinned_core` used by the google_identity effect/host-claim/remote-claim authorities — called right after the provider result and BEFORE any scope/Remote check can end the call (the fence is a product event, not an effect of the request); the pinned path still never marks/reselects (revision unchanged) and fails `needs_reconnect` (hard). `consent_revoked` now skips the fence only when the vault holds a DIFFERENT `credential_id` (a reconnect published while the refresh was in flight); unreadable vault/missing record still fences. (2) Google home source (`oauth_refresh_io`): `invalid_grant` ⇒ new `mcp_oauth::home_consent_revoked(home, "google")` (= C4 `events::fence(Target::home, Hint::Record(RelinkSubject::home))`, reason `home_oauth_consent_revoked`) BEFORE `record_google_invalid_grant`, gated on the capture still being the stored grant (receipt + capture identity, without the request-scope barrier); a home reconnect relinks under new ids. (3) GitHub home (`github_workspace::get_valid_github_token`): new `mcp_oauth::refresh_token_checked` → `RefreshFailure::{InvalidGrant, Failed}` (`invalid_grant`, and GitHub's `bad_refresh_token` even with HTTP 200); InvalidGrant ⇒ `home_consent_revoked(home, "github")` unless the stored refresh token already changed; `refresh_token` keeps its `String` API. Notion has no refresh path (`get_valid_notion_token` only reads). Stored home tokens are not deleted on a dead grant (Google marks reconnect; GitHub keeps the expired row) — only routes + in-process material are dropped by the fence. (4) Matrix 8 (revoke during refresh): the recheck is `io_authority::verify_before_io()` = the selection's live verifier (bridge: `binding_io_context` → `recheck_binding`, i.e. active + generation + binding). Already present after the exchange and before publish in `resolve_pinned_core` and `oauth_material`/`oauth_refresh_io`; added in legacy `access::resolve` (new token zeroized, `identity_changed` hard) and in `get_valid_github_token` (before send and after the exchange, before `upsert_token`). For the GitHub check to be live, `NativeConnectorExecutor::execute` now runs the credential acquisition inside `io_authority::scope(selected.io_context(), …)` (`isolation_connector_clients.rs`); no product policy reached from that recheck calls `verify_before_io`, so there is no re-entrancy. (5) Never another account: in protected mode or inside an isolated scope the legacy consent-revoke refusal is hard, and `resolve_person_for_tool` never converts a fallback-eligible Personal refusal into `UseHome` inside an isolated scope (the only home fallback there is the C2 catalog-backed SharedHome route); unscoped non-protected behaviour (configured `fallback = "home"` with notice) is unchanged. (6) C4 open question 3: `mcp_oauth::exchange_code` uses the shared non-redirecting `token_endpoint_client()` and refuses any 3xx before parsing; `google_personal::oauth::response_json` also refuses 3xx before reading (it already had `Policy::none()`), so a redirect body can never read as tokens or as `invalid_grant`. A normal refresh never moves the catalog, so the E2 bridge's pinned route proof survives it. Tests (shared fixture `isolation_connector_events::e3_test_support::selection` — a real catalog-backed `SelectedConnector` whose only fence is the catalog): `google_personal/e3_tests.rs` e3_personal_invalid_grant_revokes_routes, e3_refresh_never_switches_account; `google_identity/effect_authority_e3_tests.rs` e3_pinned_invalid_grant_revokes_routes_without_fallback, e3_revoke_during_refresh_discards_new_token; `home_source/oauth_material_e3_tests.rs` e3_home_invalid_grant_revokes_shared_home, e3_revoke_during_home_refresh_discards_new_token; `mcp_oauth/e3_tests.rs` e3_exchange_code_does_not_follow_redirects, e3_github_home_invalid_grant_revokes_shared_home, e3_revoke_during_github_refresh_discards_new_token. Not compiled or run here (Linux builder). Open: (a) the fence runs only while the dead grant is still current; if the request scope was ALREADY revoked when Google answers (Remote/Closed refresh policy and the home barrier check the scope first), that request does not fence — the next unrevoked refresh will; (b) a GitHub/Google home dead grant leaves the stored token row (dashboard shows reconnect) — decide whether to drop it; (c) `remote_mcp/oauth.rs` (generic remote MCP servers) was not reviewed for redirects/consent revoke (not a catalog provider); (d) whether non-protected installs should also stop the configured home fallback on a consent revoke (left unchanged).
- From E4: delegate (DWD) and service-account Google sources are SharedTenant sources under the new rule D-5 (Decisions). New `google_identity/tenant_source.rs`: `read(home, agent)` derives the source from existing config only (no new secret store) and fails closed — unreadable/malformed `config.toml`, agent `[google]`, sealed delegation record (must parse via `delegation_key::info`, i.e. sealed under a protected key id) or SA key file (regular file, bounded, `parse_key`), or a `tenant_scopes` entry outside `google_workspace::REQUIRED_SCOPES` ⇒ `Unavailable`; `fallback = "home"`, a delegate outside `allowed_subject_domains`, or `[integrations.google_apps_script]` beside the SA ⇒ `Refused` (`BindingMismatch`). Key material is represented only by a domain-separated SHA-256 of its bytes; the source digest (kind, subject, mailboxes, scopes, material) is the bridge's pinned per-tool proof, so a key re-import, subject or scope change refuses until the bridge is rebuilt. `admit_subject` implements D-5; `subject_accounts` reads users.db `list_users` and compares `normalize_email(email) == S`. `isolation_routes::from_lifecycle` now takes the tenant-source kind and admits the unique SharedTenant Google route of the lifecycle binding (Shared(pinned tenant)) only when a tenant source exists (still none ⇒ ignored, SR3 rule kept); `GoogleAccountRoutes` gains `tenant`, separate from `home`; Personal employees never get one; ambiguity stays `SetupRequired`. Bridge (`isolation_bridge.rs`): `source` reads the tenant source before routes, `Effective::Delegate` ⇒ tenant route (subject must equal the selector), `Effective::Home` + SA ⇒ tenant route (never SharedHome); `tenant_route` checks Shared binding, route kind/binding/tenant equal to the capability's, configured scope, then D-5; `tenant_selection` re-reads the source (must be identical) and rechecks the catalog row before returning; `candidates_for_call` (also used by every C1 recheck) and `owned` refuse arguments carrying identity-selector keys (`delegate`, `impersonate`, `as_user`, `on_behalf_of`, `sub`, … exact keys; `subject` stays, it is a mail subject). C3 `provision` refuses a Google SharedTenant route while no tenant source is installed (`reference_unknown`) or its material is unreadable (`registry_unavailable`). Core: `[integrations.google_identity]` gains strict `service_mailboxes` (normalized emails) and `tenant_scopes`; `google.delegation.settings_set` preserves them (no dashboard editor yet — F5). Tests added (not run here; Linux builder): e4_delegate_impersonates_requesting_user_only, e4_delegate_subject_from_args_refused, e4_service_account_shared_only, e4_tenant_mismatch_refused (+ _at_route_read), e4_shared_tenant_route_admitted_only_for_tenant_source, e4_unreadable_key_unavailable (+ _at_bridge), e4_tenant_source_refuses_home_fallback_paths, e4_subject_accounts_compare_normalized_email_exactly, e4_subject_rule_is_exact, e4_google_shared_tenant_provision_requires_tenant_source, core e4_tenant_source_keys_parse_strictly. **Open (owner E3/effect_authority, not edited here):** (1) the sealed host's dedicated lane runs with `RequesterIdentity::Unknown`, so `Prepared::immediate` → `requester_gate::check` refuses a delegate unless the employee has `requesters = "anyone"`; delegate execution therefore fails closed end to end until the bridge-authenticated requester (or a bridge-issued D-5 admission) is carried into `GoogleCallContext::dedicated`. The service-account path has no requester gate and works. (2) The mint still requests all `REQUIRED_SCOPES`; `tenant_scopes` is enforced per tool at the bridge, not in the token — narrowing the assertion `scope` needs the assertion owner. (3) Small TOCTOU: the host re-reads `agent.toml`/`config.toml` itself after the bridge's last recheck, so a fallback/bridge setting added in that window could reach the host's home fallback; closing it needs the host to accept the bridge's pinned source proof. (4) Subject/source is pinned by the bridge's first use, not stored with the route; pinning it in C6 details at provision needs a details-schema v2 (`provider='google'` rows) and `needs_details` handling for Google tenant routes only. (5) Personal employees cannot use DWD as their owner (they bind Personal(owner) routes only); if wanted, it needs a Personal-route variant served by DWD with requester == owner == subject account. (6) C5 (migration) must not import Google SharedTenant routes without the same tenant-source gate. (7) F3 can report `connector_setup_required: google` for a Shared employee whose tenant source exists but has no SharedTenant route.
- From F3: readiness reasons live in new `isolation_readiness/reasons.rs`; `isolation_readiness::report(home, admin)` (blocking; `system.status` runs it through `spawn_blocking`, `report_failed()` on a join error) adds `status` (`ready` | `action_required` | `service_unavailable`), `reasons` (distinct `{code, provider?}` over all employees) and, for Admin callers only (`acl::require_role(ctx, Admin)` in the `system.status` arm; the `system.status_changed` broadcast never carries them), `employees` [{label, ready, reasons}]. `ready` stays the admission truth (components only); `status()` is unchanged for its boot/CLI callers. Per employee (listed by new `isolation_lifecycle::confirmed_entries`, hardened opener, missing apply.db = none, >256 rows or a corrupt row = whole listing `service_unavailable`): disabled row or lifecycle Tombstoned ⇒ `revoked`; lifecycle Unavailable ⇒ `service_unavailable`; Stale ⇒ `personal_binding_conflict` (Personal receipt) else `binding_evidence_missing`; Denied ⇒ `binding_evidence_missing`; Personal: new `isolation_lifecycle::personal_snapshot` + B1 `personal_facts` (now `pub(crate)`, re-exported from `isolation_mcp`, Linux only; non-Linux reads `service_unavailable`) ⇒ `personal_binding_conflict`; factory: new `isolation_readiness::record_connector_build(home, agent, employee, Result<&InstalledConnectorProviders, VerificationError>)` stores `setup_required()` per label + employee id ⇒ `connector_setup_required{provider}`; no report for this employee id (no factory, recreated label) ⇒ `connector_setup_required` without provider (matrix 12); build Err Unavailable ⇒ `service_unavailable`, Revoked ⇒ `revoked`, BindingMismatch ⇒ `binding_evidence_missing`, Invalid ⇒ setup required; catalog (opened only if `isolation-connectors.db` exists; never created by a status read; unsafe ⇒ `service_unavailable`): C4 `pending_relinks` (Personal / SharedHome of the binding) > 0 ⇒ `revoked`; employees with an active Google route: E2 resolution via new `GoogleAccountRoutes::readiness(home, catalog, lifecycle)` (same `from_lifecycle`; merged with E4: reads `tenant_source::read(home, lifecycle.label)` like the bridge, unreadable ⇒ `Unavailable`/`service_unavailable`, refused ⇒ `BindingMismatch`/`binding_evidence_missing`, then passes its kind so SharedTenant routes count only with a real tenant source) ⇒ SetupRequired `connector_setup_required{google}`. `launch_service` Unavailable (spawnd unreachable) adds `service_unavailable` overall. **F1** must call `record_connector_build` after every `ConnectorProviderFactory::build` (success or error), including rebuilds after provision/revoke/advance/relink; until then every protected employee reads `connector_setup_required`. Dashboard: banner shows per-reason messages, admin-only next-step links (`/app/system/migration`, `/manage/integrations`, `/manage/users`, `/app/system/security`), the admin per-employee list, and a non-paused "needs attention" title when admission is open but reasons remain. Open: (1) each status read does one root ReadBack per protected employee (≤256); if that is too slow on large homes, cache per acl/policy epoch; (2) Personal owner offboarded (lifecycle Denied) reads `binding_evidence_missing`, not a dedicated reason; (3) `/manage/integrations` is the closest existing page until the F5 connector admin page exists — repoint the hint then. Tests (not run here; Linux builder / local vitest): `isolation_lifecycle::tests::readiness_reasons::{readiness_reports_each_reason, readiness_unreadable_store_is_service_unavailable}`, `isolation_readiness::tests::readiness_overall_status_and_admin_only_employee_labels`, banner vitest cases per reason.
- From H1: protocol for D-H1 only, no broker. `duduclaw-isolation/src/ipc.rs` adds `SpawnRequest::OpenWorkFile { run, employee_id, path: WorkPath, mode: WorkOpenMode::ReadOnly }` (no fds) → `SpawnResponse::WorkFile { run, file: WorkFileMetadata { size, inode, device } }` + exactly one `FdRole::WorkFileRead`, and `SpawnRequest::WorkAnchor { employee_id }` → `SpawnResponse::WorkAnchor { employee_id, anchor: WorkAnchorIdentity { device, inode } }` (no fds); `ErrorCode` += `WorkFileNotFound`, `WorkFileRefused`, `WorkFileTooLarge`, `WorkspaceBrokerUnavailable`; `check_work_reply(request, response, received_fds)` pairs replies and fd counts. `WorkPath` (validated on construction and deserialization, so the gateway cannot even build an unsafe path): non-empty, ≤ 4096 bytes, ≤ 64 components, each ≤ 255 bytes, no `.`/`..`/empty component, no leading `/`, NUL, `\` or ASCII control byte, and first component not `.forks` (fork workspaces keep their own `fork-<id>` handle; owner can relax this). Reply metadata: `size` ≤ `MAX_WORK_FILE_BYTES` (16 MiB), `inode`/`device` ≠ 0. **Opcode registry (coordination with HX):** the wire still carries `op` names; new `ipc::Opcode` (u16, exhaustive `SpawnRequest::opcode`) assigns core 0x0001–0x0008 (`reserve_run` … `take_io`), Package H range 0x0100–0x01FF (`open_work_file` 0x0101, `work_anchor` 0x0102), and reserves the HX range 0x0200–0x02FF: `HX_RESERVED_OPS` = `helper_call` 0x0201, `helper_wait` 0x0202, `helper_stop` 0x0203, `helper_cancel` 0x0204; `HX_RESERVED_RESULTS` = `helper_started`, `helper_ended`; `HX_RESERVED_ERROR_CODES` = `helper_busy`, `helper_unavailable`, `run_time_exhausted`, `helper_cgroup_unavailable`. **HX4** must add its variants with exactly those numbers/names; `h1_opcode_space_reserved_for_hx` fails on any helper-range opcode not in the reservation table or with a different name. Out-of-file edits needed to compile: `spawnd/src/server.rs` dispatch answers both ops `WorkspaceBrokerUnavailable` (fail closed until H4), `duduclaw-execution/src/spawnd/transport.rs::map_error` maps the new codes (NotFound / Denied / Unavailable). For **H2**: open with `O_RDONLY|O_CLOEXEC|O_NOCTTY|O_NONBLOCK` through `openat2` as planned, `fstat` the opened fd for the reply metadata, and map refusals to the four codes (ENOENT/ENOTDIR → `WorkFileNotFound`; ELOOP/EXDEV/not S_ISREG/nlink ≠ 1/owner/device → `WorkFileRefused`). For **H4**: check `owned(run, connection)`, `entry.employee == employee_id`, state `Running` and an attached storage lease before opening; `WorkAnchor` needs an allocated employee. For **H5**: `validate_response` (`transport.rs:346`) and `validate_fds` (`:446`, `WorkFileRead` = S_IFREG + O_RDONLY + FD_CLOEXEC) still refuse the new pairs/role (`_ => false`); use `check_work_reply`, then `fstat` the fd against `WorkFileMetadata`, and map the four codes to typed file-tool refusals instead of `map_error`'s generic `BackendError`. For **H16/H4**: an old spawnd decodes an unknown `op` as a malformed frame and closes the whole control connection (`server.rs:167`), so the gateway must not send these ops without a spawnd readiness feature bit (suggest `work_broker`, next to HX's `helper_calls` [F17]) and should report `workspace_broker_unavailable` instead. Tests added: `h1_open_work_file_codec_roundtrip`, `h1_rejects_unsafe_paths`, `h1_response_carries_exactly_one_fd`, `h1_opcode_space_reserved_for_hx`, `h1_bounds_enforced`; not compiled or run here (Linux builder).
- From H6: brokered `employee-work` root in `duduclaw-mcp-boundary/src/resources.rs`. **Trait** `WorkBroker: Send + Sync { fn open_work_file(&self, &WorkFileRequest<'_>) -> Result<BrokeredWorkFile, WorkBrokerError> }` (blocking; call from the blocking pool). `WorkFileRequest` has private fields and getters `run()`, `employee()`, `path() -> &WorkRelativePath`, `max_bytes()` (= `BROKERED_FILE_MAX_BYTES`, 16 MiB); only the boundary builds it, from the verified capability record (never from tool arguments; `check_arguments` already refuses `run_id`/`employee_id`/`root`/`cwd`). `BrokeredWorkFile { file: File, metadata: WorkFileMetadata { regular, size, device, inode, nlink } }`; `WorkBrokerError::{NotLive, NotFound, Refused, TooLarge, Unavailable}` is diagnostic only: the boundary maps every broker error to `VerificationError::Unavailable`. **Root** `ResourceRoots::insert_brokered(handle, employee, Arc<dyn WorkBroker>)` accepts only `EMPLOYEE_WORK_ROOT` ("employee-work"), once, and is mutually exclusive with a dirfd `insert` of that handle (both directions refuse), so there is never a host-path alternative. `authorize_file` (file_read/csv_read/xlsx_read) routes a brokered handle to the new `authorize_brokered_file(call, handle, relative)`: work-path validation → the same workspace-selection rule as `authorize_workspace` (now the shared `selected_workspace_grant`) → owner employee + capability `Workspace{employee-work}` grant → selected input path must equal the call's `path`/`file_path`/`input_path`/`source_path` argument (shared `require_selected_input`) → `verify_live` → broker → re-check of the received fd → `verify_live` again (the run may end during the root call) → `AuthorizedFile` bound to the call, grant and inode (whose `Read` keeps re-checking liveness). The fd check refuses (`Invalid`) a broker claim of non-regular/nlink≠1/size>16 MiB and an fstat that is non-regular, nlink≠1 or >16 MiB, or (Linux) opened WRONLY/RDWR/O_PATH; a dev/inode different from the broker's claim is `Unavailable`. `open_read`/`open_directory`/`authorize_workspace`/`authorize_helper_workspace` on `employee-work` still refuse (no dirfd); helper/execution directory delivery for `employee-work` needs H3's anchor, not this file path. **Path rules (shared validator `WorkRelativePath::parse`)**: superset of `relative_path`: 1..=4096 bytes UTF-8; no NUL, `\`, or `char::is_control` (C0, DEL, C1); `/`-separated, each component non-empty, not `.`/`..`, ≤255 bytes; ≤64 components; no leading/trailing `/`; never rewritten (no NFC/case normalization: root opens the exact argument bytes). Constants `WORK_PATH_MAX_BYTES/MAX_COMPONENT_BYTES/MAX_DEPTH` and conformance vectors `WORK_PATH_CONFORMANCE` are public. **H1** (`ipc.rs`, `duduclaw-isolation`, which cannot depend on mcp-boundary) must apply exactly these rules when it decodes `OpenWorkFile` and should copy `WORK_PATH_CONFORMANCE` into its tests; **H2** must pass the same vectors (spawnd can depend on mcp-boundary or copy them). If the owner prefers a single implementation, move `WorkRelativePath` down to `duduclaw-isolation` and re-export it here (no behaviour change). **H5** implements `WorkBroker` over spawnd `OpenWorkFile` (run must be live, owned by the employee, storage lease attached; openat2 BENEATH|NO_SYMLINKS|NO_MAGICLINKS|NO_XDEV; O_RDONLY|O_CLOEXEC|O_NOCTTY|O_NONBLOCK; S_ISREG, nlink 1, employee uid, nodev mount, ≤ `max_bytes`) and returns honest fstat metadata. The broker must also pass the request's run id to root unchanged; the boundary does not cache run liveness. Open: the 16 MiB limit is checked at open, and an employee process in the same run can still append to the inode afterwards; consumers already bound reads (file_read `max_bytes`, worker limits), but H2/H5 may hand back a sealed memfd snapshot instead to pin the content. **H10/F1** wire `insert_brokered` at boot with the H5 bridge (no production caller yet). **H11** needs no new resolution call: `ResourceRoots::authorize_file` already returns the brokered `AuthorizedFile`; its tests can use `resources::test_broker::TestWorkBroker` (`cfg(unix)`, new feature `test-support`; `new` emulates H2's refusals, `unchecked` hands back any fd; `end_run`, `fail_with`, `misreport`, `calls`). Tests in `resources_tests.rs` (mod `h6`, `cfg(unix)`): h6_brokered_root_requires_live_run, h6_path_from_args_normalized_and_bounded, h6_no_host_path_fallback, h6_broker_error_fails_closed, h6_non_regular_refused (also covers the 16 MiB limit); not compiled or run here (Linux builder).
- From HX2 (signed helper policy + spawnd cgroup plumbing; D8 revision §2.3/§2.4/§5): (1) Policy v2: the `isolation-policy-v1` artifact now parses as v1 `{version:1,runtimes,native}` (exact, `deny_unknown_fields`; a v1 record naming a launcher is refused; v1 ⇒ no launcher, so every helper is absent from the table) or v2 `{domain:"duduclaw-isolation-policy-v2",version:2,…}`; both fields sit inside the artifact bytes the signed release manifest binds by sha256, and every deployed v1 reader refuses v2 because of `domain`. `NativeRecord.launcher: Option<LauncherRecord{reserve:{memory_bytes,tasks,cpu_quota_percent}, helpers:[HelperBinding], helper_tmpfs_bytes}>` (omitted when `None`, so launcher-less records keep their MCP `launch_policy_digest`; a launcher-bearing record changes it ⇒ re-run MCP acceptance for that runtime). `HelperBinding = {profile, slot: foreground|recording, seccomp (flat F_H) + seccomp_digest, self_only: [prlimit64_self_only | prctl{options}], memory_bytes, tasks, cpu_quota_percent, open_files, file_size_bytes, timeout_ms, filesystem: [work|work_recordings|system_ro], egress}`. Shared vocabulary (`HelperFs`, `HelperSlot`, `SelfOnlyCall`, `RUNTIME_LANDLOCK`, forbidden paths, constants) is the new pure `duduclaw_run::helper_policy`; `runner::restrict_filesystem` now installs exactly `RUNTIME_LANDLOCK`, so check 6 is computed on the enforced list (union of enclosing rules, Landlock rights subset, never inside or above `/home/employee` `/config` `/run` `/tmp` `/dev/shm` `/runtime`). Checks 1–7 are `duduclaw_spawnd::helper_policy::validate_launcher`, run in `installed::bundle` (plus `helper_tmpfs_bytes ≤ native.tmpfs_bytes`) and again in `RootPolicy::with_launchers` (any failure fails the whole publication).
