Check 1's entitlement half is per employee (`RootPolicy::helper_table(entry.employee, parent)`, `derive_helper`): entitlements are host data, so an unentitled helper is absent from that run's table rather than failing every employee's publication. The `valid_helper` protocol-argv half stays in the gateway (spawnd checks kind/id grammar, `!mcp && !pty_allowed`, network shape). Check 3 uses `RESULT_GRACE_MS = 5_000` duplicated from `isolation_execution_program.rs`; keep them equal. Check 5: ≤ 16 bindings and launcher fds (control + 1 deduplicated helper registry mount + exec/script + 4 cgroup) ≤ `HELPER_FD_BUDGET = 40`; a run whose attachments push the total over 80 is refused by `TrustedRun::validate`. (2) Trampoline contract (`runner.rs`, minimal so HX3b can build on it): `TrustedRun.launcher: Option<LauncherPlan{helpers:[HelperProgram], mounts, tmpfs_bytes}>` (serde default), fds after the forwarder in the order control socket (SEQPACKET), helper mounts (RO/nosuid/nodev), per helper exec (+script), then `rt-runtime/cgroup.procs`, `rt-runtime/cgroup.kill`, `hx/fg/cgroup.procs`, `hx/rec/cgroup.procs` (each checked: regular, write-only, cgroup2 magic, uid 0, distinct). `take_inputs` validates them; `launch` still REFUSES any launcher and `runner::LAUNCHER_SUPPORTED = false`, so spawnd plans none: **HX3b flips the constant in the same change that forks the launcher.** Helper argv is `/helpers/<entry>` (+ `/helpers/<script>`) + signed fixed args, env empty (HX3b/HX5 set scratch env). (3) spawnd: `bootstrap_mounts::prepare_helpers` (one RO clone of the generation registry + `open_verified` per helper, ≤ 32 hashes, 30 s), `host.rs` builds the table from `entry.employee`, `native_trampoline` creates the launcher socketpair (spawnd end kept per run) and, in `bind_unit` after the trampoline's challenge and before the plan is sent, calls `helper_cgroup::linux::prepare_run`: requires `cgroup.kill`, memory/pids/cpu, exactly one process in the unit (the waiting trampoline), mkdirs `rt-init` `rt-runtime` `hx` `hx/fg` `hx/rec`, moves that pid into `rt-init`, enables controllers in the unit and `hx` subtree, `memory.oom.group=1` on both slots, open-time-credentials gate, then CLOSED slot limits (`memory.max 0`, `pids.max 0`) until HX4 writes a binding (`write_slot_limits`, readback), and opens the four fds. Any failure fails closed for helpers only: the launcher is stripped from the plan and the run's `HelperState` is `Unavailable("helper_cgroup_unavailable")`. Also provided for HX4: `kill_and_wait` (`cgroup.kill` + recursive `populated 0`), `oom_kills(memory.events)`, `NativeTrampoline::{helper_state, helper_position, helper_channel}`, `readiness::helper_readiness`. `ErrorCode::HelperCgroupUnavailable` is NOT added (ipc.rs belongs to HX4/H1); map the reason there. (4) CVE-2021-4197 detection is behavioural, not a version list (distribution backports make `uname -r` unreliable): on the first helper-enabled run spawnd opens `hx/fg/cgroup.procs` as root and asks PID 1 to run `/usr/bin/printf 0` with that fd as stdout in a transient `duduclaw-cgprobe-<nonce>.service` (`DynamicUser=yes`, no capabilities, `NoNewPrivileges`, private network, 2 s). With open-time credentials the unprivileged write moves the probe and printf exits 0; without the fix the kernel checks the writer's credentials, the write fails `EACCES`, printf exits 1 and all helpers on the host are refused. Answers 0/1 are cached per spawnd process; other outcomes are not cached and refuse that run's helpers. spawnd's bounding set has no CAP_SETUID, hence PID 1 rather than an in-process setuid fork. (5) Units: run units get `OOMPolicy=continue` and `Delegate=yes` (with `ProtectControlGroups=yes` kept), all read back via `UnitPolicy::readback`. spawnd's own unit gains `ReadWritePaths=-/sys/fs/cgroup/duduclaw.slice/duduclaw-employees.slice`, because spawnd itself runs with `ProtectControlGroups=yes`. (6) Signer: `sign-isolation-manifest.py::validate_policy_document` (v1/v2 shape, domain, launcher bindings, timeout ≤ 300 s, fs/slot/egress rules, self-only list, tmpfs bound), called by `package-linux-release.py` on the policy artifact before its digest is bound; the release-packaging fixture policy is now a minimal v2 document. **Needs root on a real host (H15):** `iso_root_helpers_refused_without_open_time_credentials` on Ubuntu 22.04 5.15 / 24.04 / Debian 12 (record the probe answer per kernel); that `ReadWritePaths=` beneath a `ProtectControlGroups=yes` tree really yields a writable cgroupfs bind for spawnd; that `Delegate=yes` + `OOMPolicy` are accepted and read back on systemd 249/252/255; that the unit cgroup holds only the trampoline at challenge time; that systemd reports `ExecMainCode/Status` for a main process that moved itself out of its unit cgroup; and the first-probe latency against the trampoline's 5 s handshake timeout. **Findings for HX3b:** (a) helper runtimes live in the same generation registry that is mounted at `/runtime` for the runtime (RX in its Landlock list), so `runtime_cannot_exec_helpers` will fail unless `/runtime` becomes a per-runtime narrowed view or helpers move to a separate registry anchor; (b) `prepare_helpers` re-hashes shared interpreters per helper on every run (cost, not authority; dedupe by digest later).
- From HX3a (launcher wire codec, spawnd IPC ops, F_L/F_H builders; D8 revision §2.2/§2.4/§5): (1) **Wire** `duduclaw_run::helper_wire` (pure, host-tested; `helper_wire::linux::{send, recv}` is the only socket I/O). Frame = `DCH1` ‖ kind u8 ‖ fixed big-endian body, exact length per kind, `MAX_FRAME_BYTES` 4096: spawnd → launcher `Start` 0x01 {call[16], helper u8 (< 16, index into `LauncherPlan.helpers`), slot u8 (0 fg / 1 rec), timeout_ms u64 (1..=300 000, the effective value)} + exactly `START_FDS` = 3 fds [input memfd, stdout w, stderr w]; `Stop` 0x02 {call} (recording only), `Kill` 0x03 {call}, both fd-free. Launcher → spawnd, never any fd or cmsg [F14]: `Started` 0x81 {call}, `Ended` 0x82 {call, kind u8, code i32: exited 0..=255 / signalled 1..=64 / timed_out 0 / start_failed `LauncherError` code}, `Draining` 0x83, `Error` 0x84 {code u16}. `Error` is channel-fatal; per-call refusals are `Ended(StartFailed(reason))` with `LauncherError::{SlotBusy, Draining, Descriptors, Sandbox, Exec, Internal}` (`Protocol` only in `Error`). `Receipt {fds, foreign_cmsgs, truncated, control_truncated}` is what `recv` observed; `decode_from_launcher` refuses any fd (`Ancillary`), any other cmsg, `MSG_TRUNC`, `MSG_CTRUNC`; `decode_to_launcher` requires exactly 3 fds on `Start` and 0 otherwise. `recv(socket, max_fds, nonblocking)` uses `MSG_CMSG_CLOEXEC`, a `CMSG_SPACE(1 fd)` control buffer when `max_fds` = 0 (spawnd: stray fds that fit — two on 64-bit because of padding — are delivered, owned and closed; more ⇒ `MSG_CTRUNC`, never installed), `MAX_FRAME_FDS` = 4 on the launcher side (a surplus fd is counted, not silently dropped), and owns every delivered fd before returning. State tables: `SpawndChannel` (spawnd; `receive(now_ms, bytes, receipt)` — any error closes it: rate > `MAX_FRAMES_PER_SECOND` 64 in any 1000 ms sliding window, unknown call, Started twice, Ended(exit) before Started, StartFailed after Started, second Draining, launcher `Error`; `start` checks fresh call id (never reused in the run, ≤ 65 536), helper index's signed slot, slot free, not draining; `stop` recording only, `stop`/`kill` idempotent; `abandon(call)` frees a slot that spawnd ended root-side and accepts that call's late frames in order (≤ 8 outstanding, else the channel closes)) and `LauncherChannel` (launcher; Start for a busy slot or after SIGTERM ⇒ `Command::Refuse` (race, not a violation; answer with `refused()`), Stop/Kill for an ended call ⇒ `Ignore`, id reuse / wrong slot for the index / index outside the table / Stop on fg / never-seen id / fd surplus ⇒ protocol violation). **HX3b** drives `LauncherChannel` from `launcher.rs::serve`; **HX4** owns one `SpawndChannel` per run (`new(plan.helpers.map(slot))`), calls `abandon` after its own `cgroup.kill` + `populated 0` when the launcher has not sent `Ended`, and treats every `receive` error and EOF as `helper_unavailable` for later calls. (2) **IPC** (`ipc.rs`): `Opcode::{HelperCall 0x0201, HelperWait 0x0202, HelperStop 0x0203, HelperCancel 0x0204}` exactly as H1 reserved (`h1_opcode_space_reserved_for_hx` updated only in `every_request`). `SpawnRequest::HelperCall { run, employee_id, helper: RuntimeKind (Registered only), slot: HelperSlot, timeout_ms 1..=300 000, input_bytes ≤ 16 MiB }` + `[PromptSealed]`; `HelperWait/HelperStop/HelperCancel { run, call: HelperCallId }`. Replies `HelperStarted { run, call, effective_timeout_ms }` + `[StdoutRead, StderrRead]`, `HelperEnded { run, call, outcome: HelperOutcome }` (`Exited(0..=255) | Signalled(1..=64) | TimedOut | OomKilled | RunEnded | StartFailed`), `Ack` for stop/cancel; `check_helper_reply(request, response, fds)` pairs run/call, `effective ≤ requested`, fd counts. Deviation from §5 text: replies also carry `run` (pairing, as `WorkFile` does), and the request carries `input_bytes` (spawnd needs the size to revalidate the memfd, as `prompt_bytes`); the request names the helper as `RuntimeKind` per §2.3/§5 — the helper *index* exists only on the launcher wire. `HelperCallId` (16 bytes, all-zero refused on construction and decode, `hex()` = recording job id). `ErrorCode` += `HelperBusy`, `HelperUnavailable`, `RunTimeExhausted`, `HelperCgroupUnavailable` (names = `HX_RESERVED_ERROR_CODES`); consts `MAX_HELPER_TIMEOUT_MS` (now also the source of `helper_policy::HELPER_TIMEOUT_MAX_MS`), `MIN_HELPER_RUN_REMAINDER_MS` 5 000, `MAX_HELPER_INPUT_BYTES`. `HelperSlot` moved into `ipc.rs` and is re-exported by `duduclaw_run::helper_policy` (same serde names). **Feature bit [F17]**: there is no hello op, so `SpawnResponse::Reserved` gains `features: SpawndFeatures` (u32 bits, `#[serde(default)]`, omitted when empty, unknown bits ignored); bit 0 = `HELPER_CALLS` (`helper_calls`); `HELPER_FEATURE_MISSING_REASON` = `spawnd_feature_missing`. Out-of-file compile edits: `spawnd/src/admission.rs` reserve sends `SpawndFeatures::NONE` (**HX4** sets `HELPER_CALLS` once it dispatches), `spawnd/src/server.rs` answers all four ops `HelperUnavailable` (input memfd dropped), `execution/src/spawnd.rs` destructures `Reserved { .., }`, `execution/src/spawnd/transport.rs::map_error` maps busy/unavailable/cgroup ⇒ `Unavailable`, `RunTimeExhausted` ⇒ `Timeout` (**HX5** maps them to typed results first; `validate_response`/`validate_fds` still refuse the new pairs, use `check_helper_reply`), and its test fixture. (3) **seccomp** (`seccomp.rs`): `launcher(arch, parent, parent_digest)` = F_L = parent ∪ L_restrict: `Architecture::launcher_restrict_calls` (landlock ×3, `setsid`, `wait4`, `waitid`, `timerfd_*` ×3, `close_range`) unconstrained, plus `prctl` ∈ `PRCTL_SELF_ONLY` (`PR_SET_DUMPABLE` only with arg2 == 0), `prlimit64` pid == 0, `setpgid(0, 0)`, `seccomp(SET_MODE_FILTER, flags 0)` as argument checks unless the parent already allows the call outright (union never narrows). `helper(arch, parent, parent_digest, profile, digest, self_only)` = F_H: refuses unless `seccomp_within_parent` (flat ⊆ parent, closed self-only set), compiles `Prlimit64SelfOnly` (pid 0) and `Prctl{options}` (exactly the binding's options) as argument checks; both verify digests and embed `common_rules` first. `int`/`pid_t` args are compared on the low 32 bits (all the kernel reads), `unsigned long` on both halves. `init` now allows `setitimer` (both arches) and `alarm` (x86_64 only; asm-generic has none). **HX3b** must install `launcher(..)` in the launcher child after its Landlock, and `helper(..)` in the helper child immediately before `execveat`; note F_L adds no `kill`/`dup3`/`openat`/`unlinkat`/`mkdirat`/`sendmsg`/`recvmsg`, so the launcher relies on the parent profile for those (all runtime profiles hold them today; a profile without `kill` cannot run the timerfd fast path — spawnd's watchdog still applies). Tests added (not compiled or run here; Linux builder): duduclaw-run `f_l_prlimit64_other_pid_denied`, `f_l_prctl_option_allowlist`, `init_filter_allows_alarm`, `f_l_is_parent_union_restrict_and_keeps_common_refusals`, `f_h_is_within_parent_decoded`, `seccomp_native_uapi_numbers_for_launcher_and_helpers` (Linux), `helper_wire::tests::{wire_rejects_fd_on_launcher_frame, wire_exact_fd_counts_and_layout, wire_strict_decoding, wire_spawnd_state_table, wire_abandoned_calls_accept_late_frames_in_order, wire_rate_limit_64_frames_per_second, wire_launcher_state_table, wire_linux_seqpacket_fds_cmsgs_truncation_and_eof (Linux, real socketpair)}`; duduclaw-isolation `hx3a_helper_ops_use_reserved_numbers_names_and_codes`, `hx3a_helper_call_codec_and_strict_schema`, `hx3a_helper_reply_pairing_and_fd_counts`, `hx3a_helper_calls_feature_bit_is_additive`; `h1_opcode_space_reserved_for_hx` must still pass. HX4's `control_channel_closed_on_cmsg_or_truncation` should drive `SpawndChannel` + `linux::recv` end to end (state closed, later calls `helper_unavailable`); the codec-level cases are already covered here. Too many abandoned calls is reported as `WireError::Rate` (an unresponsive launcher); rename if HX4 wants a distinct audit reason.
- From M1: duduclaw-import now accepts `sessions.db`/`tasks.db`/`cron_tasks.db` from a set of known versions, each with its own pinned catalog and column mapping: `*-v1793.sql` (v1.79.3, restored) and `*-v180.sql` (v1.80.x = the catalogs after the post-merge fix, which the gateway fresh-store regression tests now compare against; `capture_tasks_schema.py`/`capture_cron_schema.py` write the v180 files). New `src/catalog.rs::detect` requires the whole `sqlite_schema` to equal one catalog (sessions optionally + the exact channel_settings extension); otherwise `Error::Schema` plus a bounded `SchemaRefusal` (`take_schema_refusal()`, printed by the worker on stderr) naming the closest version and missing/unexpected/changed objects and missing catalog columns, never SQL text or rows. Mappings: v1.80 sessions also filter `delivery_pending=1`; tasks exclude A2A goals in both versions via a recursive CTE over `parent_task_id` seeded by `created_via='a2a'` or an `a2a:` creator (both) and `a2a_owner`/`task_access_principals` (v1.80 only, authorized only for v1.80); cron actor columns stay unauthorized (`cron_columns`). **The v1.79.3 catalogs were reconstructed without git history** from the v1.80 catalogs + CHANGELOG/docs: sessions minus `delivery_pending`/`client_msg_id`; tasks minus the A2A objects (`a2a_owner`, `idx_tasks_a2a_creator_id`, `task_access_principals` + backfill + 3 triggers, `a2a_requests`, `a2a_task_sequence` + trigger) and `idx_tasks_employee_health`; cron minus `created_by_user_id`/`scoped_by_user_id` and the `cron_creation_receipts` batch. Before release (owner: migration) diff them against `git show v1.79.3:` sources or a real v1.79.3 home; in particular the 1.79.3 `task_authority_revision_v1` column list and the dating of `idx_tasks_a2a_creator_id` were inferred, not checked. Not covered by M1: whether v1.81 changes these stores (HEAD is treated as v1.80.x; a change needs a `*-v181.sql` + mapping), and the other single-version catalogs (users, approvals, memory, workflow, autopilot, channel settings) were not reviewed for 1.80 drift — e.g. the 1.80.0 MFA e-mail/passkey and remembered-session tables are absent from `users-v1793.sql`. Tests added (not run here; Linux builder): import_accepts_v1793_session_tasks_cron, import_accepts_v180_session_tasks_cron, import_refuses_unknown_schema_with_diff, a2a_goals_excluded_in_both_versions, cron_actor_columns_never_read_in_both_versions.
- From HX3b (in-trampoline launcher; D8 revision §2.1/§2.4/§2.5, Q1 = A): `runner::LAUNCHER_SUPPORTED = true` and the `launch` refusal is gone, so spawnd now plans launchers (helpers still cannot run until HX4: slot limits stay CLOSED and spawnd answers every helper op `HelperUnavailable`). (1) **Fork point**: `child_init` forks the launcher after `PrivilegesVerified` and before `restrict_filesystem` (new `Stage::LauncherForked`, legal only between those two; `launcher_fork_stage_precedes_runtime_landlock`), as an in-process function `linux/launcher.rs::serve` (no exec, non-dumpable inherited and re-checked). Launcher, in order [F12]: blocks SIGTERM (handler + `ppoll` mask), ignores SIGPIPE, `setsid`; fd table = 0/1/2 → `/dev/null`, status pipe, ready pipe, control socket, helper exec/script fds, `hx/fg` + `hx/rec` `cgroup.procs`, everything else swept, table and identities read back; Landlock = `helper_policy::launcher_landlock` (`/helpers` RX, `/helper-tmp` Ephemeral, `/dev/null`, union of `HelperFs` paths with `WorkRecordings` mapped to `/work` in the launcher layer only, so a runtime-planted `/work/recordings` can fail one recording call but not launcher setup; never `/home/employee` `/config` `/run` `/tmp` `/dev/shm` `/runtime`, host test `launcher_landlock_excludes_home_config_run_tmp_shm`); common + F_L; then marks `FailurePage` bit 4, `munmap`s the page, closes the status fd and writes `L` to the ready pipe. Init waits for that byte (bounded by `CONTROL_TIMEOUT_MS`) after the exec check and before `all_isolated(forwarder, launcher)`, which now requires exactly bit 4 when a launcher is planned. Launcher failure before ready fails the run; afterwards its death is not fatal (init just forgets it). (2) **HX2 finding (a) fixed in the trampoline** (`mounts::PreparedRoot::attach_helpers`): Landlock rules are per inode and spawnd's helper registry clone has the SAME root directory inode as `/runtime`, so mounting it at `/helpers` would have put every helper beneath the runtime's `/runtime` rule. Instead the clone is staged at `/.helper-registry` (open_tree refuses files of foreign mounts on 5.15/6.1/6.8), each signed helper file is bound read-only from its verified fd onto a placeholder in a private RO tmpfs at `/helpers/<name>` (a file bind is its own mount root, so no path to it crosses a runtime rule), helper-only names in `/runtime` are over-mounted with an empty root-owned mode-0 file (the runtime's own exec/script and the forwarder are never masked; a masked helper file must have nlink 1, else the run fails), and the stage is detached before pivot. `/helper-tmp` is a separate tmpfs (`LauncherPlan.tmpfs_bytes`, employee 0700, noexec like `/tmp`). Consequences: helper names must be flat registry names (`helper_policy::helper_file_name`; `LauncherPlan::validate` refuses nested scripts and one name with two identities) and a helper sees only its own signed entry/script under `/helpers` — **HX5/HX6** helpers that need sibling registry files (node_modules, python stdlib) must get them from `/usr` or have them added as signed table files. `LauncherPlan` now requires exactly one registry mount. (3) **Helper child** (`Child::prepare`, under F_L): reset SIGTERM/SIGPIPE and the mask, `setsid`, `PR_SET_PDEATHSIG` + parent check, `write("0")` to the slot `cgroup.procs`, reopen `/helpers/<entry>` (and script) with no symlinks and require the signed inode/device on the read-back bind mount id (the root-verified exec fd is re-verified with `verify_executable` on every Start), `chdir` to `/helper-tmp/<call>`, stacked Landlock (`/helpers/<file>` RX as file rights, `/helper-tmp/<call>`, `helper_policy::helper_landlock`), exact `FdPlan` 0 = `/dev/null` or the recording stdin pipe, 1/2 = call pipes, 3 = `/dev/null`, 4 = input memfd (`reorder_for_exec` reads the table back), `prlimit64(0, …)` hard = soft for CORE 0, NOFILE, FSIZE (capped at the inherited hard limit), env exactly `HOME`/`TMPDIR` = `/helper-tmp/<call>`, F_H, `execveat`. Failures before exec return a `LauncherError` byte on a CLOEXEC status pipe ⇒ `Ended(StartFailed)`; EOF ⇒ `Started`. Start fds are checked (sealed tmpfs memfd ≤ `MAX_HELPER_INPUT_BYTES`, two distinct write-only FIFOs). Timeout: timerfd at min(Start, binding) ⇒ `kill(-pgid)` + `kill(pid)` ⇒ `Ended(TimedOut)`; reaping via `CLONE_PIDFD`; scratch removed with `openat(O_NOFOLLOW|O_DIRECTORY)` + `unlinkat` + raw `getdents64`, bounded. SIGTERM ⇒ `Draining`, Start refused (`LauncherChannel`), fg killed, recording gets `stop\n`, SIGKILL at F; idle + draining or control EOF ⇒ exit. (4) **Landlock under F_L**: the pinned crate `fstat`s each rule fd, which F_L does not guarantee, so launcher and helper layers use the new raw `landlock::restrict_raw` (+ `Policy::rights_on` file-rights masking). (5) **F_L sufficiency** (HX3a note): `Architecture::launcher_parent_calls` lists the 29 calls the launcher/helper child issue beyond L_restrict (read, write, close, lseek, mmap, munmap, rt_sig{action,procmask,return}, kill, fcntl, chdir, getppid, fstatfs, getdents64, openat, mkdirat, unlinkat, ppoll, dup3, pipe2, sendmsg, recvmsg, clone, statx, openat2, pread64, execveat, exit_group); `parent_supports_launcher` is enforced in `LauncherPlan::validate` (trampoline) and at publication (`helper_policy::validate_launcher` ⇒ new `HelperPolicyError::ParentLacksLauncherCalls`, reason `helper_launcher_unavailable`; per HX2 convention this fails the whole publication). glibc `malloc` falls back to `mmap` when `brk` is denied; anything else missing surfaces as EPERM ⇒ StartFailed/launcher failure, never a wider filter. `scripts/sign-isolation-manifest.py` does NOT check this yet (follow-up). (6) **F2**: the trampoline writes `oom_score_adj = -1000` for itself before pivot (host procfs), so only the supervisor keeps it; init writes 0 right after `mount_proc` while still root, which also resets the inherited `oom_score_adj_min`; both read back (not a receipt field). Needs CAP_SYS_RESOURCE: added to `REQUIRED_BOUNDING_CAPABILITIES` and spawnd `TRAMPOLINE_CAPABILITIES` (dropped with the bounding set before any employee process). (7) **F4**: the runtime child writes `0` to `rt-runtime/cgroup.procs` first thing; init keeps only the status and `rt-runtime/cgroup.kill` fds (`seccomp::init_with_kill` allows `write` to exactly those two), and on reaping the runtime writes `1` to `cgroup.kill`, SIGTERMs the launcher, arms `setitimer` at F + 2 s (re-arming every 200 ms; a PID-namespace init needs the SIGALRM handler, SIG_DFL would be ignored), then SIGKILLs the launcher group and exits with the stored runtime status; the forward handler's runtime pid is cleared on reap. F comes from the new signed-plan field `LauncherPlan.drain_ms` = parent `termination_grace_ms` − 2 s (≥ 1 s), set by spawnd (`native_trampoline::launcher_drain_ms`, `host.rs`). Tests added (not compiled or run here: macOS worktree, no shell; Linux builder): duduclaw-run `init_filter_allows_cgroup_kill_write_only`, `launcher_refused_for_parent_without_launcher_calls`, `seccomp_native_uapi_numbers_for_launcher_parent_calls` (Linux), `landlock_file_rules_carry_only_file_rights`, `launcher_landlock_excludes_home_config_run_tmp_shm`, `helper_layer_rules_stay_inside_runtime_list`, `helper_file_names_are_flat_registry_names`, `launcher_fork_stage_precedes_runtime_landlock`, `launcher_ready_before_all_isolated`, `launcher_plan_requires_parent_launcher_calls_and_flat_helper_files`, `launcher::tests::{scratch_listing_parses_getdents_and_cleanup_is_no_follow, helper_child_rlimits_are_hard_equal_soft}`; spawnd `publication_rejects_parent_without_launcher_calls`, `launcher_drain_window_is_grace_minus_two_seconds_bounded` (+ CAP_SYS_RESOURCE assertion in the unit-policy test, fixtures updated to launcher-capable parents); root (`tests/root.rs`, fixture `root-fixture/helpers.rs`, coordinator plays spawnd with real `SpawndChannel` frames and its own cgroup under `/sys/fs/cgroup`): `iso_root_helper_{launcher_forked_before_runtime_landlock, runtime_cannot_access_proc_of_helper, launcher_fd_table_exact, launcher_ready_before_all_isolated, helper_fd_table_exact, only_supervisor_oom_protected, helper_cannot_open_home_config_tmp_devshm, offline_helper_socket_eperm, runtime_cannot_exec_helpers, runtime_cannot_precreate_helper_scratch, runtime_descendants_killed_on_runtime_exit, launcher_refuses_start_after_sigterm, init_wait_bounded_with_stopped_launcher, helper_raise_rlimit_denied}`. **Needs root on real hosts (H15 Ubuntu 22.04/24.04, Debian 12)**: every `iso_root_helper_*` and the existing `iso_root_full_launch_*` (now with supervisor OOM protection and init reset); that open_tree of a file from the staged clone, move_mount of file binds onto `/helpers` placeholders and onto files of the RO `/runtime` mount work on 5.15/6.1/6.8; the inode-walk reasoning for `/helpers` vs `/runtime` (the `runtime_cannot_exec_helpers` / `_access_proc_` cases are the proof); unprivileged `write("0")` through root-opened `cgroup.procs` (CVE-2021-4197 hosts only); that `-1000` is writable inside the run unit sandbox (ProtectKernelTunables etc.); that the 29-call list really covers glibc on each distro (e.g. no `newfstatat`/`brk` dependency surfaces as EPERM); - From H2: broker core `duduclaw-spawnd/src/work_broker.rs` (Linux only, no dispatch wiring). API: `open_work_file(&LiveRunView, &mut dyn Storage, run, employee, &WorkPath) -> Result<WorkFileSnapshot, WorkRefusal>`, built from `check_work_path` → `verify_lease` → `snapshot(root, path, &WorkExpectation)`. **Path**: H1 `WorkPath` rules ∩ H6 `WorkRelativePath::parse` (spawnd already depends on mcp-boundary; `WORK_PATH_CONFORMANCE` is used verbatim) + first component `.forks` refused; `MAX_WORK_FILE_BYTES == BROKERED_FILE_MAX_BYTES` is a compile-time assert. Divergence found: H1 `validate_work_path` refuses only ASCII controls, so `WorkPath` accepts C1 controls (`"a\u{85}b"`) that H6 refuses; the broker refuses them (`InvalidRequest`), but **H1/HX3a** (whoever next edits `ipc.rs`) should switch to `char::is_control` and copy `WORK_PATH_CONFORMANCE` into `h1_rejects_unsafe_paths`. **Lease**: `LiveRunView { run, employee, uid, running, receipt: Option<&Attachment>, work_root: Option<BorrowedFd> }` is read by H4 from its own core state; the run must match (`NotFound`), the employee must match (`Unauthorized`), the uid must be an employee-range uid, `running` (admission Running and `LiveRun` not closed) and `receipt.check` (observed device) else `NotReady`; then `Storage::attachments()` (durable inventory) must contain exactly one lease for this run/employee, equal to the receipt (same token and device): absent or another run's lease of this employee ⇒ `NotReady`, unreadable/duplicate/different token or device ⇒ `WorkspaceBrokerUnavailable`. **Open**: openat2 `RESOLVE_BENEATH|NO_SYMLINKS|NO_MAGICLINKS|NO_XDEV`, `O_RDONLY|O_NOFOLLOW|O_CLOEXEC|O_NOCTTY|O_NONBLOCK` (FIFOs open without blocking and then fail S_ISREG); the root fd must be a directory on the lease device (else Unavailable); EAGAIN/EINTR retried ×4; ENOENT/ENOTDIR ⇒ `WorkFileNotFound`; ELOOP/EXDEV/EACCES/EPERM/ENXIO/ENODEV/ENAMETOOLONG/EOPNOTSUPP/ETXTBSY/EISDIR ⇒ `WorkFileRefused`; anything else (ENOSYS, EMFILE, …) ⇒ `WorkspaceBrokerUnavailable`. statx of the fd: S_ISREG, nlink 1, uid = employee uid, device = lease device, mount id = root's mount id (else `WorkFileRefused`), size ≤ 16 MiB (else `WorkFileTooLarge`). **Decision: sealed memfd snapshot, not the live fd.** The file is copied with `pread` into `memfd_create(MFD_CLOEXEC|MFD_ALLOW_SEALING|MFD_NOEXEC_SEAL)` (retry without NOEXEC_SEAL on EINVAL, pre-6.3 kernels), reading at most limit+1 bytes so growth after the check is `TooLarge`, 10 s copy deadline; the source's full statx identity (inode, size, mtime, ctime, nlink, uid, device, mount) must be unchanged across the copy (one retry, then `WorkFileRefused`); then `F_ADD_SEALS` SEAL|SHRINK|GROW|WRITE, reopened O_RDONLY through `/proc/thread-self/fd/N` (new description, offset 0), seals/access mode/dev+ino re-verified, writable fd and the source fd closed. Why: the same-run employee owns the inode and can append/truncate/rewrite it after the check, so only a copy pins ≤ 16 MiB of immutable content; the snapshot holds no reference to the employee loop filesystem, so a gateway/worker-held fd can neither read the employee file after the run ends nor keep the mount busy and block `Storage::detach` at cleanup; and the gateway cannot `flock`/poll the employee's inode. Cost: one ≤ 16 MiB copy per call and ≤ 16 MiB shmem charged to spawnd's memory cgroup until the receiver closes the fd. Residual: a same-size rewrite within one timestamp tick can yield a torn (still bounded, immutable, employee-controlled) snapshot. `WorkFileSnapshot { file, metadata: ipc::WorkFileMetadata (honest fstat of the memfd: size, tmpfs inode, tmpfs st_dev), source: SourceIdentity (employee device/inode/size, audit only, never sent) }`. **H4** must: keep in `LiveRun` a root-held `bootstrap_mounts::detached_clone(storage.work, NOSUID|NODEV|NOEXEC[|RDONLY])` taken in `start` BEFORE `select_workspace` (today `storage` is dropped at the end of `start`, so no work root survives), drop it in `cleanup_run` before `Storage::detach`; build `LiveRunView` in the same worker turn (owned(run, connection) stays H4's check); serialize opens per run and bound in-flight snapshots (memory); send `file` as the single `FdRole::WorkFileRead` with `SpawnResponse::WorkFile { file: metadata }`; reply `refusal.code()`; audit `source` (never the path). **H5/H6 (blocking for the snapshot):** the received fd has nlink 0 and a tmpfs dev/inode, so `resources.rs::verify_brokered` (`actual.nlink() != 1` ⇒ Invalid) must instead accept a regular fd with `F_GET_SEALS ⊇ SEAL|SHRINK|GROW|WRITE`, O_RDONLY, size ≤ 16 MiB and dev/ino equal to the reply (and the H5 claim should report `nlink: 0` honestly); `TestWorkBroker::new` should emulate the snapshot. Tests (`work_broker.rs` mod tests, tempdir fixture + fake `Storage` inventory): work_broker_refuses_symlink_component, work_broker_refuses_hardlinked_file, work_broker_refuses_foreign_owner (+ chown variant when run as root), work_broker_refuses_cross_device (root on another device, file device/mount mismatch via `check_source`), work_broker_refuses_oversize (16 MiB ok, +1 refused, copy-time cap, deadline), work_broker_refuses_non_regular_and_reports_missing (FIFO, dir, ENOENT, ENOTDIR), work_broker_snapshot_is_sealed_read_only (in-place rewrite after open does not change the snapshot; seals, O_RDONLY, CLOEXEC, write/truncate fail), work_broker_requires_live_lease (all lease cases + code mapping; the end-to-end half runs only when the fixture fs has a non-zero device major and the test uid is in the employee range — on tmpfs/overlay builders it prints a skip line), work_broker_conformance_vectors. Root (`#[ignore]`, `ISO_STORAGE_TEST_ROOT` on ext4/XFS, private mount namespace of the test thread): iso_root_work_broker_refuses_mount_crossing_and_devices (detached nodev clone with a tmpfs submount ⇒ EXDEV refused, char device refused, chown to another uid refused, full `open_work_file` path), iso_root_work_broker_snapshot_does_not_pin_employee_mount (non-lazy umount succeeds while the snapshot is held). Not compiled or run here (Linux builder).

Entry = `*` (any public HTTPS origin) or exactly `https://<host>` (https, default port, no userinfo/path, lowercase LDH host with ≥2 labels, TLD starting with a letter so no IP/WHATWG numeric host, no `localhost`/`local`/`internal`/`arpa`/`test`/… TLDs); 1..=16 entries, no duplicates, `*` only alone. Fetch: `web_fetch_protected_cached` already existed with DNS pin (`resolve_public_addrs` + `resolve_to_addrs`, all-or-nothing `vet_resolved_addrs` over `duduclaw_core::net_addr`), no redirects, no proxy, 5 MiB bounded read, 30 s; not rewritten. Gateway `prepare_web` and CLI `fetch_for_tool` re-run `authorize_public_url` before fetching. Test-only seam `web_fetch::test_dns` (cfg(test)) feeds DNS answers. Open: (1) H9/H10 issuer must emit `PublicOrigins` (and add `web_fetch_cached`/`web_extract` to tools) for scoped records — until then tools stay hidden; (2) `web_fetch_protected_cached` still allows `http` for the legacy UrlOrigin path — https-only is enforced by the grant, not the fetch; (3) the protected cache is gateway-wide keyed by URL (pre-existing), so one employee's cached public page can serve another's; (4) no public-suffix list: an entry like `https://co.uk` is accepted but grants only that exact host; (5) the CLI listing gate still requires `record_resolver` for web_fetch_cached even though the PublicOrigins path doesn't use it. Tests added (not run here; Linux builder): public_origins_requires_employee_scope, public_origins_mac_covered, public_origins_rejects_ip_literal_and_userinfo (capability.rs); web_fetch_refuses_origin_not_granted, public_origins_wildcard_still_refuses_ip_literal_userinfo_and_private_names (tool_policy_tests.rs); public_origin_url_resolves_to_the_exact_authorized_origin (resources_tests.rs); web_fetch_refuses_private_address_after_dns, web_fetch_refuses_redirect (gateway web_fetch.rs).
- From H3: new `duduclaw-spawnd/src/work_anchor.rs` (`#[cfg(unix)]`). `WorkAnchors` holds a descriptor of the base `/var/lib/duduclaw-spawnd/work-anchors` (root:root 0700; `open_installed()` requires euid 0 and `registry::root_path(REGISTRY_DIRECTORY)`, creates the base with `mkdirat` on first use, never through a symlink) and keeps one anchor directory per employee named by the 32-hex EmployeeId: root:root, mode exactly 0500, same `st_dev` as the base (a mount over it is refused), never written into. All lookups are descriptor-relative (`mkdirat`/`openat(O_NOFOLLOW|O_DIRECTORY)`/`fstatat(AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW)`/`unlinkat(AT_REMOVEDIR)`), and the base's owner/mode/dev/inode are re-checked on every call. `ensure` (create, then fchown/fchmod on the new descriptor; an existing anchor must already pass every check — no repair), `read` (never creates; absent ⇒ `ErrorCode::NotFound`, any mismatch ⇒ `IsolationUnavailable`) and `remove` (absent ⇒ Ok; symlink/foreign/non-empty/busy ⇒ `IsolationUnavailable`, nothing followed or deleted). **Adapter hook:** `AnchoredStorage` wraps any `Storage`: `provision` = inner provision, then `ensure` (allocation, incl. replays: same inode); `destroy` = inner destroy, then `remove` (retire; a refused image destroy keeps the anchor, a failed remove leaves the allocation `Disabling` so the Disable retry finishes it); attach/detach/usage/attachments forward unchanged, so the identity survives runs and spawnd restarts. `Storage` gains a default method `work_anchor(employee) -> Result<WorkAnchorIdentity>` (default `WorkspaceBrokerUnavailable`; `AnchoredStorage` answers `WorkAnchors::read`). `host.rs::Components::installed` now wraps `LoopStorage` in `AnchoredStorage` with `WorkAnchors::open_installed()`; if the anchor base cannot be opened the whole installed storage is unavailable (fail closed, same message). **For H4:** answer `SpawnRequest::WorkAnchor { employee_id }` from `components.storage.work_anchor(employee_id)` after checking the employee is allocated (registry account not Tombstone / allocation `Ready`), map `NotFound` to `WorkspaceBrokerUnavailable` or a not-found reply as you prefer, and keep `server.rs`' fail-closed answer until then (not edited here). Open: (1) employees allocated before H3 have no anchor until the next Allocate replay (`provision` replays call `ensure`); ReadBack/`usage` deliberately do not create one — H4/H10 may need an explicit backfill step at upgrade; (2) a retired employee's anchor inode can be reused by a later employee's anchor on the same filesystem, so consumers must always pair `(device, inode)` with the EmployeeId (the IPC reply carries it; H6/H9 must compare both); (3) H2's note that H4 must keep a root-held detached clone of the work tree is independent of this anchor (the anchor is identity only and contains nothing); (4) the H1/H6 `WorkPath` mismatch is fixed: `ipc.rs::validate_work_path` now refuses `\` and every `char::is_control` character (C0 incl. NUL, DEL, C1 incl. U+0085), same as `WorkRelativePath::parse`; H6's `WORK_PATH_CONFORMANCE` vectors are copied into ipc.rs tests (`H6_WORK_PATH_CONFORMANCE`; keep both lists identical); README updated. The `.forks` first-component rule stays H1-only (stricter than H6; no vector covers it). Tests (not run here; Linux builder): `work_anchor::tests::{work_anchor_identity_stable_across_runs, work_anchor_not_writable_by_employee, work_anchor_removed_on_retire}` (non-root fixture: base/anchors owned by the test account, `AnchorOwner` injected), `iso_root_work_anchor_not_writable_by_employee` (`#[ignore]`, root, `/run`, forked child `setuid(70001)` must fail to create/mkdir/chmod/chown/rmdir both by path and through a held anchor fd), `ipc::tests::{work_path_refuses_unicode_controls, work_path_conformance_vectors_match_h6}`.

AND assigned_to=<agent>` plus the existing audience rule, on every call (reassigned/deleted task ⇒ `Invalid`; missing/unreadable store ⇒ `Unavailable`, never created).
