- From F6m (agent token v2, S8 / A11): **Format** `DUDUCLAW_AGENT_TOKEN = "v2." + hex(HMAC-SHA256(identity.key, "duduclaw-agent-token-v2" ‖ u32be(len(label)) ‖ label ‖ employee_hex(32 lowercase chars) ‖ u64be(incarnation)))` (`identity_token::mint_token_v2`/`verify_token_v2`); a v1 verifier rejects it (prefix is not hex) and a v2 verifier rejects every v1 token. **Protected employee** = a label with a row in `migration-authority/apply.db` `confirmed_employees` (live or disabled), read by new `duduclaw-core/src/identity_token/lifecycle.rs::protected_employee` → `Unprotected | Live(AgentIncarnation{employee, incarnation}) | Tombstoned`, or `LifecycleUnavailable` when the index exists but is unsafe (symlink/reparse point, not a regular file, nlink ≠ 1, foreign owner, group/other-writable, replaced during open — same store policy as `fs_safe::sqlite::open_existing`, sidecars not checked), unopenable, or holds a corrupt row (bad JSON, receipt label ≠ row label, NULL/negative incarnation). No `migration-authority/` dir, no `apply.db`, no table or no row ⇒ `Unprotected`. **Incarnation:** the column does not exist yet; the reader uses `confirmed_employees.incarnation` when present and 0 otherwise (F6a/F6b add `INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`, only restore writes it, +1). This is the token binding source only (no root ReadBack, no users.db); every protected authority still does the full `isolation_lifecycle::resolve` at use. **Rules:** live ⇒ only the v2 token of the current tuple verifies, in both modes (soft mode never applies; a missing key is `Rejected`, never `Disabled`); tombstoned or unreadable ⇒ `Rejected` and nothing is minted; unprotected ⇒ v1 exactly as before. New core helpers `current_agent_token(home,key,label)` (v1 / v2 / `None`) and `verify_agent_token`, re-exported at the crate root. **Mint sites changed:** `agent_identity_env_vars` (so every spawn env and `.mcp.json` writer that goes through it: `mcp_proxy_rewrite::canonical_duduclaw_server` → `mcp_template::ensure_duduclaw_absolute_path`/`prepare_mcp_config_for_spawn`, `runtime/mod.rs::duduclaw_mcp_server_json_for_home` (also used by codex), grok, generic_cli, third_party_mcp, workflow executor, `claude_runner::mcp_client_envs_in`, doctor probe, CLI `agent_identity_env_vars_default` callers); `mcp_isolation_identity::var(ENV_AGENT_TOKEN)` (isolated MCP projection); client signers `egress_hook::exchange`, `google_execute_client`, `google_selection_client` (each now refuses when `None`). Rotation is automatic: the token is recomputed from the live row at every spawn, so a restore (incarnation bump) or adoption/label reuse (new employee id) changes it at the next spawn and the old one stops verifying immediately. **Verify sites changed:** `verify_claim` (hence `verify_env_identity`, `verified_bridge_env_token`, remote bridge rechecks, `mcp_proxy_spawn_env`, `mcp_namespace::verified_employee`), `mcp/util.rs::verified_delegation_context`, `mcp/config_read.rs` standalone-client check; internal-request receivers `computer_use_sessions/auth.rs::authenticate`, `credential_declaration.rs`, `egress_guard.rs` (`/api/internal/egress`), `egress_guard/hook.rs`, `egress_guard/mail_approval.rs` now recompute the expected token with `current_agent_token` (refuse on `None`), so the existing signature (which MACs the token) binds employee id + incarnation for protected employees with no wire change. **Delegation context:** `DelegationContext` gains `worker_employee`/`worker_incarnation` (version 2, own MAC domain `duduclaw-delegation-context-v2`; both omitted from version 1, which serializes byte-identically); host mint `claude_runner::delegation_context_env` uses `mint_delegation_context_in` (v2 for a live protected worker, v1 unprotected, none when tombstoned/unreadable); MCP verify uses `verify_delegation_context_in` (binding from a fresh index read, never from the context). The label-only `mint_delegation_context`/`verify_delegation_context` remain (v1 only; a v2 context fails them). Not changed: `mcp_init_cmd` standalone client tokens (reserved client namespace, never an employee; still verified through the lifecycle-aware check), test-only `mint_identity_token` callers. Test fixture changed: `mcp_isolation_parity_tests.rs` (Linux) now hands its child a v2 token, because its `SharedEmployee` fixture confirms `main`. Tests (`identity_token/tests_v2.rs`): token_v2_binds_employee_and_incarnation, token_v1_refused_for_protected_employee, token_v1_still_accepted_without_protected_mode, token_v2_refused_after_tombstone, token_rotated_on_incarnation_change (covers agent_token_of_old_id_refused_on_reused_label), delegation_context_bound_to_employee_id (covers delegation_context_of_old_incarnation_refused), internal_signature_bound_to_employee_id, legacy_row_without_incarnation_column_reads_incarnation_zero, unreadable_or_corrupt_index_refuses_protected_tokens, unsafe_index_store_refuses_protected_tokens (unix); not compiled or run here. **Effect on non-protected installs:** none while no `apply.db` exists (one `symlink_metadata` NotFound per check); if a non-protected home has an `apply.db` that is unreadable or corrupt, every claim (any label) is refused until it is repaired — the reader cannot tell which labels are protected (fail closed, one `error!` per process). Open: (1) F6c must not rely on the next spawn alone: after the incarnation bump, live runs of the old incarnation keep v1/v2-old tokens that already fail, which is intended, but their `.mcp.json` is rewritten only at the next spawn; (2) delegation `sender`/`origin` are still label-only attribution (only `worker` is bound); (3) ephemeral sub-agents (`eph-<label>-…`) are not in the index and keep v1 tokens — decide whether they inherit the parent's tuple; (4) per-agent channel bots (A11) are F6h, not here; (5) the gateway `EmployeeLifecycle` still has no `incarnation` field (F6b adds it and should compare it with this reader's value in `recheck_with`).
- From F1: protected-mode connector stack wired at boot. New `isolation_connector_stack.rs` (+ `_tests.rs`). (1) **One catalog handle.** `boot_protected` opens `ConnectorCatalog` into `BootSources.connector_catalog` *before* `GatewayAuthority::new` (C2), then `install_connector_stack` builds `EmployeeConnectorProviders` over that same `Arc`; `gateway_factory` builds `CatalogAccountDetails` over it (`shares_catalog`) and the factory hands it to every build (Google bridge, native home providers, selections). `assemble` refuses (`Unavailable`) if the stack's catalog is not `Arc::ptr_eq` to the authority's. (2) **Factory.** `isolation_mcp::install_connector_factory(Arc<dyn ConnectorProviderFactory>)` (one-shot, refused after boot started, like `install_dispatcher_factory`) is an optional host override; otherwise `assemble` (after the worker exists) assembles `GatewayConnectorFactory` with `connector_executor_factories()` (D3 → `odoo_executors`/`channel_executors`), D2 home providers, `search = InstalledWorkerServices` (now `Clone`), `hardware = detect_hardware()` (once at boot), `computer_sessions = admin_sessions(home)` and the E2/E4 bridge `EmployeeKeyGoogleBridges` (users.db + the authority's run-turn resolver). `home_binding = Shared(users.db installation tenant)` via `connector_route_binding(home, None)`, read once at boot (lifecycle requires it to equal the apply.db pin; a tenant change makes every lifecycle/C2 check refuse; unreadable ⇒ `None`, i.e. no home routes, narrower never wider). The factory's `inventory()` must pass in `install_factory`, else it is not installed. (3) **Vault (C3, SR3 Info 5).** `AccountCredentialVault` is now keyed by (account, generation): `install(credential, generation)` (0 ⇒ `Invalid`, older than stored ⇒ `BindingMismatch`, newer replaces), `acquire_at(account, provider, generation)` serves only that exact generation; plain `acquire` serves nothing. New trait method `AccountCredentialProvider::acquire_at` (default = `acquire`); `NativeConnectorExecutor`'s `credential()` passes `selected.generation()`. New `VaultedCredentials { vault, source, routes: RouteGeneration (ConnectorCatalog) }`: route active at exactly the pinned generation before serving and after storing (else the account's material is dropped and the call refuses), vault hit while unexpired, miss ⇒ live source (D2 `HomeOAuthCredentialProvider`) ⇒ stored under the generation. The factory's native credentials are `VaultedCredentials` over `parts.credential_vault` (new required field of `GatewayConnectorFactoryParts`); boot creates ONE vault, calls `install_credential_material(vault)` and passes the same `Arc` to the factory — if the material slot was already taken, the stack is not installed (connector producer missing, boot paused) so revoke can never drop a different vault. (4) **Per-employee registry, rebuilt without restart.** `EmployeeConnectorProviders` caches one `InstalledConnectorProviders` per label with (employee id, lifecycle binding, route set = active routes of the binding + SharedHome routes of the home binding for Personal employees, with generations). `current(agent, employee)` re-reads the route set on every use and rebuilds on any change (provision, revoke, advance, C4 relink); `observe_lifecycle` (called in `prepare_launch` on the blocking pool, and by refresh) forgets a build whose employee id/binding changed or whose lifecycle is unreadable. A rebuild reads the lifecycle (A2, `RootAllocator`) — another employee id under the label ⇒ `Revoked` — builds, re-reads the route set (changed ⇒ `Unavailable`), and a failed rebuild removes the cached build (never an older one in service). `prepare_launch` gives each launch an `EmployeeConnectors` handle (both selection policy for the resolver and `DispatcherInput.connectors`; refuses another employee's record); the boot singletons `connectors`/`connector_selection` are the stack itself = inventory only (declares every connector tool, serves none) for `verify_producers`. Selection/recheck may rebuild synchronously (same blocking class as the existing catalog/C2 reads); `execute` never builds (uses the cached build; the selection's own rechecks fence it). Proactive refresh: new `isolation_connector_lifecycle::install_route_change_hook` / `routes_changed()` called after C3 provision, after the C3 revoke fence, after a C4 `fence_with` that revoked something and at the end of `Rotation::finish`; boot's hook queues one coalesced `refresh_all` on the blocking pool (also run once after the factory is installed = boot prewarm of every confirmed employee). (5) **Readiness.** Every build outcome (success, factory error, lifecycle refusal, unreadable catalog) goes through `record_connector_build` (`readiness_report()`); no factory ⇒ no build ⇒ no report ⇒ F3 `connector_setup_required` (matrix 12) and every connector tool refuses (`Unavailable`). (6) **Google bridge input (decision for SR5).** `EmployeeKeyGoogleBridges` uses the employee's own legacy MCP key reference: the unique `[mcp_keys]` entry with `client_id = <agent>`, a `ddc_{prod,staging,dev}_` key and a `google:` scope (digest only; expiry/scope still checked per tool by `credential_registry::authorize`); none or several ⇒ no bridge. `GoogleBridgeFactory::bridge_for` (new, default = `bridge`) returns `Ok(None)` ⇒ `ConnectorBootProviders.google` is now `Option` and Google is a refusing adapter, reported `SetupRequired` (`connector_setup_required{google}`) while the employee has any active Google route (own, home or own SharedTenant), else `NoRoute`. Unreadable/malformed `config.toml` ⇒ whole build `Unavailable`. (7) `ComputerConnectorProvider` now prefers the gateway's live session registry for its home (`workspace_admin::live_sessions`) at action time, because boot builds providers before `server.rs` creates the live `ComputerUseSessions`. Tests (`isolation_connector_stack_tests.rs`; not compiled or run here — Linux builder): f1_boot_shares_one_catalog_handle, f1_factory_uses_cli_executor_factories, f1_rebuild_on_route_revoke_and_relink, f1_vault_material_dropped_on_revoke, f1_vault_keyed_by_generation, f1_readiness_reports_every_build, f1_missing_factory_setup_required, f1_google_bridge_uses_unique_employee_key; updated vault call sites in `isolation_connector_clients_tests.rs`, `isolation_connector_lifecycle_tests.rs`, `handlers/isolation_connectors_rpc.rs` tests. **F2** (CLI `mcp_isolation_boot.rs`): no code change was needed for the dispatcher path — `CliDispatcherFactory::create` already consumes `DispatcherInput.connectors`/`resolver`, which are now the per-employee handle; remaining for F2: decide whether `verify_producers` should also require `ConnectorProviderFactory::inventory()` (today the stack's inventory object declares every connector tool and the factory inventory is checked at install), and retire or adapt `IsolationDispatcher::boot(.., &InstalledConnectorProviders, ..)` (no production caller). **G1** must run the parity suite through `gateway_factory` + `EmployeeConnectorProviders`/`EmployeeConnectors` (real issuer, real factory, 62 tools) instead of singleton registries. **G2** should cover end to end: revoke during a live run (cached build fenced, next use rebuilt), relink picked up by a running employee, matrix 12 (no factory ⇒ setup required), vault material gone after C3 revoke / C4 consent revoke, and the Google key-reference path. Open: (a) whether the gateway selecting the employee's own MCP key digest as the bridge's host attestation is acceptable (E2/E4 owner; alternative is a dedicated per-employee bridge credential); (b) a rebuild inside a selection recheck runs on whatever thread called it (async executors included) — if root ReadBack latency matters, move rebuilds to `refresh` only and refuse on a stale route set; (c) `detect_hardware` and the boot prewarm run once per boot; model discovery does not see later hardware changes.
- From H5: gateway work-broker client + `SpawndWorkspaceBridge`. **Placement:** mcp-boundary already depends on duduclaw-execution (`execution-integration`), so the spawnd client is in execution (`spawnd/work_file.rs`: `SpawndBackend::open_work_file(run, employee, path, max_bytes) -> Result<WorkFileSnapshot, WorkFileError>`, blocking, ≤ 12 s) and the `WorkBroker` impl is `duduclaw_mcp_boundary::work_bridge::SpawndWorkspaceBridge::new(Arc<SpawndBackend>)` (Linux + `execution-integration`); it forwards `WorkFileRequest::{run, employee, path, max_bytes}` unchanged (H6 builds them from the verified capability) and reports the snapshot's fstat honestly (`regular: true`, tmpfs dev/inode, `nlink: 0`). **Feature bit:** no IPC/readiness projection of spawnd features exists yet (ipc.rs/spawnd are H4's), so the backend reads them per call from an injected `FeatureSource` (`SpawndBackend::with_feature_source`, `SpawndFeatures::from_advertised(names)`, `WORK_BROKER_FEATURE = "work_broker"`; unknown names enable nothing; a source error reads as none). Default is no source ⇒ every open is `BrokerUnavailable` (→ `WorkBrokerError::Unavailable`, code `workspace_broker_unavailable`) without sending a byte. **H4/H16 must** publish `work_broker` from spawnd itself (not config) and **H10/F1** must install the source plus `insert_brokered(EMPLOYEE_WORK_ROOT, employee, Arc::new(SpawndWorkspaceBridge::new(backend)))` with the SAME backend Arc that reserved the run. **Client checks:** feature bit → `WorkPath::new` (invalid ⇒ `Refused`) → run must be in this backend with `entry.employee == employee` and control open (else `NotLive`, nothing sent) → `OpenWorkFile` on that run's own control connection (`Connection::work_request`, no fds sent) → `check_work_reply` (also in the transport) → exactly one fd → `sealed_snapshot` (S_IFREG, nlink 0, ≤ 16 MiB, `F_GET_SEALS ⊇ SEAL|SHRINK|GROW|WRITE`, `fstatfs` = TMPFS_MAGIC) → fstat size/dev/ino == reply metadata → `size ≤ max_bytes`. Any reply/fd failure fences (closes) the run's control connection; a root `Error` keeps it. Codes: `WorkFileNotFound`→NotFound, `WorkFileRefused`→Refused, `WorkFileTooLarge`→TooLarge, `WorkspaceBrokerUnavailable`→BrokerUnavailable, `NotFound`/`NotReady`/`Unauthorized`→NotLive, other→Unavailable. **Transport (`transport.rs`):** `validate_response(request, response, received_fds)` delegates OpenWorkFile/WorkAnchor to `ipc::check_work_reply` (exact fd counts; a work reply to a non-work request is refused); `validate_fds` accepts `FdRole::WorkFileRead` = O_RDONLY, not O_PATH, CLOEXEC + `sealed_snapshot`. `map_error` unchanged (work ops never reach it). **Boundary (`resources.rs`):** `verify_brokered` now accepts ONLY a sealed read-only memfd: claim regular + nlink 0 + ≤ 16 MiB; fstat regular, nlink 0, ≤ 16 MiB; not WRONLY/RDWR/O_PATH; all four seals; tmpfs; then dev/ino/size == claim (mismatch `Unavailable`, shape `Invalid`). Any unsealed regular file (live inode nlink 1, unlinked file, partially sealed memfd, O_TMPFILE on tmpfs, writable description) is `Invalid`; non-Linux is `Unavailable`. `TestWorkBroker::new` now emulates H2's snapshot (`test_broker::sealed_snapshot`, also public for H11); `unchecked` still returns the live fd (now always refused); the module and the h6 tests are Linux-only. **File-tool errors:** `WorkBrokerError::{code(), user_message()}` (fixed zh-TW text, never a path/name/host location) and `FileRefusal::{Verification, Broker}` with `ResourceRoots::authorize_file_with_refusal` (same checks; `authorize_file`/`authorize_brokered_file` still map every broker error to `Unavailable`). **H11** should call `authorize_file_with_refusal` and show `refusal.broker().map(user_message)` (else its existing generic text), and must not assume nlink 1 on the brokered `AuthorizedFile` (e.g. when handing it to the parse worker). Open: (1) a work op holds the run's control-transaction gate for up to 12 s; `SpawndBackend::live_snapshot` waits only 250 ms for the gate and a gate timeout closes the control connection, so a concurrent liveness probe during a slow (multi-MiB) open can fence the run — H4/H10 should serialize brokered opens with the authority probe, or make a pre-send gate timeout non-fatal in `ControlIo::transact`; (2) `WorkAnchor` has transport validation but no client method yet (H9 needs it); (3) the feature source is per backend, not per connection — a spawnd restart is caught because old connections close and new reservations re-read it. Tests (not run here; Linux builder): `duduclaw-execution` `spawnd::work_file::tests::{h5_bridge_requires_feature_bit, h5_bridge_validates_reply_and_single_fd}` and the extended `transport::tests::replies_bind_operation_and_run`; `duduclaw-mcp-boundary` `resources::tests::h5::{h5_verify_brokered_accepts_sealed_memfd, h5_verify_brokered_refuses_unsealed_file, h5_error_mapping_no_path_leak}`, updated `h6::h6_non_regular_refused`, and `work_bridge::tests::h5_bridge_maps_spawnd_refusals` (needs `--features execution-integration`).
- From H4: spawnd wiring of the Package H ops plus two new F6 ops. **Opcodes** (`ipc.rs`, README registry): `stop_employee_runs` 0x0103 and `employee_idle` 0x0104 in the Package H range; `Opcode::WORK` lists all four Package H ops. `SpawnRequest::StopEmployeeRuns { employee_id }` → `SpawnResponse::EmployeeRunsStopped { employee_id, runs: List<RunHandle, 64>, confirmed }`; `SpawnRequest::EmployeeIdle { employee_id }` → `SpawnResponse::EmployeeIdle { employee_id, report: EmployeeIdleReport { runs, attachments, processes, units } }` (`idle()` only when all zero); `check_work_reply` pairs all four. **No new `ErrorCode`**: `duduclaw-execution/src/spawnd/transport.rs::map_error` matches `ErrorCode` exhaustively and is H5's file, so the snapshot caps answer `resource_limit` and a fenced/stopping employee `not_ready`. **Feature bit**: `EvidenceReport.features: SpawndFeatures { work_broker }` (`#[serde(default)]`, struct deliberately without `deny_unknown_fields`, so an older reader ignores newer bits and an older report reads `false`); `EvidenceReport::work_broker()` is the gateway gate; **HX4** adds `helper_calls` to the same struct. It is a decode bit, not availability: every op still fails closed on the host. **Server** (`server.rs`): the four ops are deferred replies — the coordinator returns a `Ticket`, the loop polls it every iteration and stops reading that connection until the reply is sent, always within `BROKER_REPLY_MS` = 4 s (below the client's 5 s `CONTROL_TIMEOUT_MS`); past the deadline the ticket is abandoned (late results and their fd are dropped in `WorkerHost::receive`) and the caller gets `workspace_broker_unavailable`; a disconnect abandons it too. Authentication is unchanged (listener SO_PEERCRED gateway uid/gid). **Coordinator** (`admission.rs`): OpenWorkFile requires `owned(run, connection)` (`not_found`), the run's employee (`unauthorized`), admission `Running` and no pending stop (`not_ready`), ≤ `MAX_SNAPSHOTS_PER_RUN` 4 / `MAX_SNAPSHOTS` 16 outstanding (`resource_limit`), ≤ 64 tickets overall; a snapshot that arrives after the run left `Running` is dropped and answered `not_ready`. New `Host` trait methods with refusing defaults: `broker` (sync, adapter lifecycle thread), `broker_submit`/`broker_poll`/`broker_abandon` (non-blocking facade). **Worker** (`host_worker.rs`): `Key::Broker(ticket)`; `BrokerStep::Deferred` copies go to a new `spawnd-work-broker` thread (bounded FIFO of 16, one copy at a time ⇒ opens of a run are serialized and ≤ 1 copy is in memory; a full queue ⇒ `resource_limit`), so an H2 copy (≤ 10 s) never stalls polls, fences or cleanup on the lifecycle thread. **Host** (`host.rs`): `Core::start` takes `bootstrap_mounts::work_broker_root(&storage)` (detached RO/nosuid/nodev/noexec clone of the WHOLE H work tree) right after attach and BEFORE `select_workspace`, kept in `LiveRun.broker: work_broker::BrokerRoot`; a failed clone disables the broker for that run only. `cleanup_run` calls `BrokerRoot::release()` first (closes the clone, `not_ready` while any in-flight copy still holds a dup + pin; `CopyLease` drops the dup before the pin) — before the trampoline close and any `Storage::detach`. `broker_open` builds `LiveRunView` in the same lifecycle turn (`running` = not closed ∧ trampoline reported Running ∧ unit present ∧ run fence not raised), runs `check_work_path` + `verify_lease`, then defers `work_broker::copy_snapshot` (re-reads the fence before and after the copy). WorkAnchor: registry account Ready and allocation journal Ready (`Allocator::ready_employees`), else `not_found` (absent/Tombstone) / `not_ready` (Reserved/not Ready); then `Storage::work_anchor`, any anchor error ⇒ `workspace_broker_unavailable`. **StopEmployeeRuns** (F6 §3.2 S13): per-run `cancel_entry` (the existing Cancel, now also reachable without the owning connection) on every non-terminal non-draining entry of the employee, re-applied on each poll; new reservations and opens of that employee refused while pending; `confirmed = true` only when no admission entry of the employee is non-terminal (Finished already requires the complete unit/cgroup/uid readback and the H detach) AND a fresh root `EmployeeIdle` readback is all zero; else `confirmed = false` after `STOP_WAIT_MS` 3.5 s. Unknown employee with no runs ⇒ `not_found`. The fence is in memory only (a spawnd restart reconciles and kills every run anyway). **EmployeeIdle** (`idle_report`, pure and unit-tested): open root runs + `runs.json` records by employee OR uid + non-terminal admission entries; durable leases; `accounts::employee_process_count(uid, gid)` (any of the four uid OR gid fields, S14; new `process_gids`/`employee_owned_status`); `duduclaw-run-*` units that map to the employee or to nobody (an unattributable unit makes every employee non-idle). Any unreadable source ⇒ error, never zero; tombstoned accounts are still answered. **Backfill** (H3 open item 1): `LinuxHost::open` runs `Core::backfill_anchors` after reconcile — every allocation Ready in journal and registry → new `Storage::backfill_work_anchor` (default refuses; `AnchoredStorage`: read, create only if `NotFound`, never repair) via `work_anchor::backfill` → `Backfilled::{Present, Created, Failed}`. **Audit** (`audit.rs`): new events `work_file_opened` (detail `work_source` = source device major/minor, inode, size), `work_file_refused` (detail `code`), `employee_runs_stopped` (`stopped` = count, confirmed), `work_anchor_backfilled`, `work_anchor_backfill_failed`; `Record.detail: Option<Detail>` is omitted when `None` (old records keep their 5-field shape), `Detail` is `deny_unknown_fields` and has no text field. Tests (not compiled or run here; Linux builder): admission `stop_employee_runs_cancels_and_confirms`, `stop_employee_runs_refuses_unknown_employee`, `employee_idle_counts_admitted_runs`, `h4_snapshot_cap_enforced`; host_worker `h4_snapshot_copy_never_blocks_lifecycle_worker`, `h4_fixture::h4_open_work_file_end_to_end_with_fixture` (coordinator → worker → copy thread → real sealed snapshot of a tempdir); work_broker `h4_clone_dropped_before_detach`; host `employee_idle_reports_processes_and_attachments`; host_state `h4_feature_bits_advertised`; work_anchor `anchor_backfill_idempotent`; accounts `employee_owned_status_matches_any_uid_or_gid_field`; audit `h4_work_events_carry_identity_never_paths`; ipc `h4_employee_ops_codec_and_pairing` (+ updated `h1_opcode_space_reserved_for_hx`); root `tests/work_broker_chain.rs::iso_root_work_broker_chain_backfill_snapshot_and_detach` (`#[ignore]`; real loop storage: backfill once, RO clone, real `verify_lease`, H detach refused while a copy holds the clone and succeeds after release, snapshot outlives detach). **For H5/H16:** send any `Opcode::WORK` op only when `EvidenceReport::work_broker()`, else report `workspace_broker_unavailable`; expect exactly one reply per request within 4 s; `validate_response`/`validate_fds` must accept the four new pairs (use `check_work_reply`); `resource_limit` on OpenWorkFile = too many outstanding snapshots (retry later), `not_ready` = run not Running/being stopped. The H2 snapshot fd is a sealed memfd (nlink 0, tmpfs dev/ino): `resources.rs::verify_brokered` must accept it (H2 note, still open). **For F6a:** call `StopEmployeeRuns` after the tombstone commit; `confirmed = false` or any error ⇒ record a pending fence and retry (idempotent); audit the returned run ids. **For F6c/F6l:** use `EmployeeIdle` (or reuse `host::idle_report` for the schema-2 ReadBack `idle`); F6l should also make the retire liveness gate use it. Open: (1) the per-run/global caps count tickets the server still waits for; a copy abandoned at the 4 s deadline still occupies the copy queue (bounded at 16) until it finishes or sees the fence; (2) one copy thread serializes all employees' opens (throughput, not authority); (3) start-up backfill events share the host audit budget (60/min), so very large homes may see suppressed counts; (4) `EmployeeIdle` does not cover SysV IPC or `/tmp`/`/dev/shm` files of the uid (F6 S14 residual); (5) `employee_process_count` reads every `/proc/*/status` per call, and a non-idle stop re-probes until its deadline.
- From HX4 (spawnd side of helper calls; D8 revision §2.2/§2.5/§3, F4/F5/F7/F8/F14/F17, Q4 = A; merged with H4): **Merge**: `ipc.rs` keeps both op sets (`Opcode::ALL` 16; `WORK` = H4's four, new `HELPER` = HX's four; H4 `StopEmployeeRuns`/`EmployeeIdle` + HX3a helper variants, replies and error codes side by side; `every_request` covers all 16 so `h1_opcode_space_reserved_for_hx` holds); `server.rs` keeps H4's deferred-ticket dispatch and adds the helper arms. **One feature set**: H4's readiness struct `host_state::SpawndFeatures { work_broker: bool }` is replaced by the HX3a bitset `ipc::SpawndFeatures` (u32, JSON number, omitted when empty, unknown bits ignored): `helper_calls` = bit 0, `work_broker` = bit 1 (`WORK_BROKER`), `Opcode::feature()`/`SpawndFeatures::allows(op)` are the gateway gate. The value comes from one place (`Host::features`, default = `WORK_BROKER` ∪ `HELPER_CALLS` iff `Host::helpers_ready`) and is advertised the same way on both channels: `EvidenceReport.features` (`Core::refresh` sets `report.features = self.features()`) and every `SpawnResponse::Reserved.features` (`LinuxHost::features` returns the published report's value, so the two can never differ). **Wire change vs H4**: `readiness.json` `features` is now a number (`2`/`3`), not `{"work_broker": true}`; `EvidenceReport::{work_broker, helper_calls}()` are the readers (H5/H16/HX5 must use them, nothing consumed the H4 shape yet). `helper_calls` is set iff `runner::LAUNCHER_SUPPORTED` ∧ the publication has ≥ 1 launcher with helpers (`RootPolicy::launchers_active`) ∧ `helper_cgroup::linux::open_time_credentials_observed() != Some(false)` ∧ not poisoned; a failed probe clears it and calls answer `helper_cgroup_unavailable`. Not-yet-probed (`None`) counts as available because the probe needs a helper-enabled run's cgroup; that run's own prepare failure is per-run `helper_cgroup_unavailable`. **Coordinator** (`spawnd/src/admission_helpers.rs`, child module of `admission`, pure): `helper_call(connection, request, memfd, random_call, now) -> Ticket` checks `owned` (`not_found`), `entry.employee == employee_id` (`unauthorized`, F7), admission `Running` + no pending employee stop + runtime not exited (`not_ready`, F4), run remaining ≥ 5 s (`run_time_exhausted`), `helpers_ready`, run-wide closed code, budgets (queued + starting memfds count against `pending_input_bytes`/`input_descriptors` with StartRun inputs, and `start()` now counts helper inputs too), fresh non-zero call id (server CSPRNG, deduplicated per run, ≤ 65 536). Slots: one fg + one rec; a busy slot queues in a per-run FIFO of ≤ `MAX_QUEUED_CALLS` 4 (both slots together), each ≤ `HELPER_QUEUE_MS` 3 s, then `helper_busy` (the 5th waiting call is refused at once); a free slot never queues. Drops (memfds closed, tickets answered): run leaves Running (`drain` → `end_helpers`: queue `not_ready`, live calls `RunEnded`), host `Draining` (runtime exit), host `Closed` (launcher lost / slot cgroups failed: that code for the queue and all later calls), `HelperCancel` of a queued call, server abandon (deadline/disconnect). `HelperCall` is a deferred reply (`Pending::Helper`, `poll_helper_ticket` → `HelperStarted` + pipes); `HelperWait` is a bounded poll (`not_ready` → `HelperEnded` exactly once; ≤ 16 unwaited ended records per run, oldest dropped); `HelperStop` (recording only, else `invalid_request`) and `HelperCancel` call only `Host::helper_signal` (F8: no code path to `drain`/revoke/kill); a stop/cancel racing the host start is applied when it lands; a start whose reply nobody receives is cancelled and its pipes dropped (`poll_orphans`). The host's `effective_timeout_ms` must be ≤ the request (else the call is cancelled, `internal`). **Root engine** (`spawnd/src/helper_host.rs`, Linux, one `HelperEngine` per Running run in `LiveRun.helper`, driven from `Core::maintenance` on the lifecycle thread): `Trampoline::take_helper_link` (new trait method, default `helper_unavailable`; `NativeTrampoline` moves the control socket end, the `UnitTree` and the run table `Vec<LinkedHelper{index, slot, limits, timeout_ms}>` out once; HX2's `helper_channel` removed) when the run first polls Running. `Core::helper_run`: root `LiveRun.employee` must equal the job's (F7 again), run not closed/fenced, helper re-derived per call with `derive_helper(entry.employee, parent, kind)` (`runtime_denied`), slot = binding slot, run remaining = min(coordinator, root `runtime_deadline`). `HelperEngine::start`: memfd revalidated (`fds::validate_sealed`, `invalid_fds`), `effective_timeout_ms(tool, binding, remaining, termination_grace)` (budget = remaining − grace − 5 s, < 5 s ⇒ `run_time_exhausted`), slot `populated 0` else `helper_busy`, `write_slot_limits(binding)` + readback, `oom_kill` baseline, two CLOEXEC pipes, `SpawndChannel::start`, `helper_wire::linux::send` with `[memfd, stdout w, stderr w]`, spawnd's copies closed. `service(now)`: ≤ 16 frames/turn via `linux::recv(max_fds 0, nonblocking)` (fds dropped at once; any `receive` error or EOF ⇒ close: `SpawndChannel::close`, `shutdown(SHUT_RDWR)`, both slots killed, `Closed{helper_unavailable}`); watchdog at effective + `WATCHDOG_GRACE_MS` 2 s ⇒ kill hint + root `cgroup.kill`; `rt-runtime/cgroup.events` populated → 0 ⇒ `Draining`. `finish` (every call, exactly once): `kill_and_wait` (100 × 10 ms), `oom_kill` delta ⇒ `OomKilled` (wins over any frame), else launcher end / `TimedOut` (watchdog) / `Signalled(9)` (cancel, channel lost after Started) / `StartFailed`, `abandon` when ended root-side, slot closed again (`SlotLimits::CLOSED`); a drain/readback failure closes the run's helpers with `helper_cgroup_unavailable`. `cleanup_run` shuts the engine down (live ⇒ `RunEnded`, no Closed event) before `broker.release()` and the trampoline close. **WorkerHost**: `Key::Helper(ticket)`/`Work::Helper` for starts, fire-and-forget `Key::HelperSignal`, and an mpsc of `HelperEvent`s drained by the lifecycle loop after `maintenance` and after every job; `LinuxHost` delegates. **fds**: `validate_start` checks the HelperCall memfd (sealed tmpfs, exact size). **Audit**: `helper_started`/`helper_ended` (`Detail::Helper{call, helper index, slot, input_bytes, effective_timeout_ms|outcome}`), `helper_refused` (HelperCall error code; `HelperWait` `not_ready` polls are not audited), `helper_channel_closed`. Tests added (not compiled or run here: macOS worktree, no shell; Linux builder): spawnd `admission_helpers_tests::{helper_call_never_occupies_d8_slot, helper_call_other_connection_refused, helper_call_wrong_employee_refused, helper_call_on_draining_run_refused, queued_call_counts_against_input_budgets, helper_busy_after_queue_bound, queue_dropped_on_run_end_revocation_cancel, helper_queue_depth_4_and_drop_rules, in_run_job_cancel_never_kills_run, deadline_refuses_below_five_seconds, hx4_call_ids_and_feature_bit}`; `helper_host_tests::{helper_start_writes_signed_limits_and_sends_exact_fds, memfd_seals_revalidated, control_channel_closed_on_cmsg_or_truncation, watchdog_kills_slot_with_stopped_launcher, oom_reported_from_memory_events_not_frame, fg_kill_leaves_rec_running, runtime_exit_reports_draining_and_refuses_start}` (Linux, unprivileged: fake `CgroupTree` + real SEQPACKET socketpair as the launcher); `fds::tests::helper_call_input_must_be_one_sealed_memfd_of_the_stated_size`; `audit::tests::hx4_helper_events_carry_ids_sizes_and_outcome_never_content`; `host_state::tests::h4_feature_bits_advertised` (rewritten for the bitset); isolation `ipc::tests::hx4_one_feature_set_gates_work_and_helper_ops`. **Not done / open**: (1) the HX4 task text also lists the client `duduclaw-execution/src/spawnd.rs::run_helper` — not implemented here (spawnd side only); `transport.rs::validate_response`/`validate_fds` still refuse the helper pairs (use `check_helper_reply`) and its `map_error` stays HX3a's; (2) root tests `iso_root_helper_{watchdog_kills_slot_with_stopped_launcher, oom_reported_from_memory_events_not_frame, fg_kill_leaves_rec_running, escaped_daemon_killed_after_call}` are not written (need a real launcher run on H15 hosts; unprivileged halves exist); (3) `HelperWait` is a poll, not a held reply — HX5 polls it after the pipes reach EOF; (4) MCP cancel of a still-*queued* call: the gateway learns the call id only from `HelperStarted`, so it waits ≤ 3 s for the reply and then sends `HelperCancel` (or gets `helper_busy`); a queued call is also dropped when the gateway disconnects; (5) capability revocation is gateway-side for helpers (HX5 sends `HelperCancel`/`HelperStop`); spawnd's own revocation path is the run drain; (6) `HelperCancel` kills the slot synchronously on the lifecycle thread (`kill_and_wait` ≤ 1 s), like other lifecycle work; (7) the slot-cgroup probe still runs on the first helper-enabled run, so `helper_calls` may be advertised before it is known; (8) `kill_and_wait` of a slot after the unit stopped fails quietly at shutdown (unit stop already killed it); (9) root `HelperBusy` when a slot is still populated at Start (stale process) is not retried by the coordinator; (10) [SEC] Daybreak review HX3+HX4 still pending.
- From P1: Personal data visibility, protected mode only. (1) **Team grants:** `duduclaw_auth::org::team_grants_on` and `overseen_reports_on` exclude every agent with an `isolation_personal_agents` row in SQL (`NOT EXISTS`), so a manager gets no derived Operator/Viewer grant, no `can_call_agent`/`can_assign_to` and no team-history oversight of a subordinate's Personal employee; the owner keeps the direct Owner binding; a missing mirror table is an `Err` (callers narrow). No protected-mode flag is needed there: only protected installs write mirror rows. `users.my_team` filters through `can_call_agent`, so it drops them too (an Admin-manager still sees the roster row = metadata only). (2) **Shared predicate** `crate::personal_visibility` (pub; also used by the CLI): `personal_owner(home, agent)` (fresh users.db read through `fs_safe::sqlite::open_existing` ReadOnly; any error = `Unavailable` ⇒ refuse), `classify_reader(home, protected, user, is_admin, agent)` → `Unrestricted` (not protected / Shared) | `Owner` | `BreakGlass{owner}` (Admin, not owner) | `Refused` (everyone else incl. `system`), `hidden_from(home, protected, user)` (Personal employees not owned by the caller), `management_refusal(home, protected, acting, target)`. This is the users.db mirror, not the full A2 lifecycle (no root ReadBack on dashboard reads); the run-time authority still checks the lifecycle. (3) **Dashboard gate** `handlers/personal_data_gate.rs`, one helper `MethodHandler::personal_data_gate(ctx, Target{method, category, agent}, csrf)`: `dispatch` runs it (after the edition gate, before any handler/extension) for the `agent_scoped` table on `params.agent_id` — memory.{search,browse,key_facts,decay_overview,history,at,get_at,graph,invalidate_origin,forget}, wiki.{pages,read,auto_pages,search,lint,stats,promote,archive,share}, agent_files.{list,get,runtime_files,diff,history,save,rollback}, agents.inspect, contract.{get,update}, and chat.sessions.list / search.query / runs.list when scoped; `chat.sessions.history` (and the attachment paths, with no token) call it after resolving the session's agent in `history_session_access`; `runs.get` calls it for both the dispatch-run and session branches. Unscoped Admin `chat.sessions.list`, `search.query` and `runs.list` hide `hidden_from` agents (listing ⇔ access). Break-glass = the migration dashboard proof (`UserDb::migration_confirmation_session` for this Admin, dashboard session and credential epoch; written by `migration.confirmations.step_up` / `isolation.connector_step_up`) + `authorize_step_up` with the request field `step_up_csrf` (32-byte array, removed from params before the handler); then `auth_audit_log` row `isolation.personal_data_read` (target = employee, detail `{admin_user_id, employee, owner_user_id, category ∈ sessions|memory|agent_files|wiki, method}`) must be written or the read refuses; refusals write `isolation.personal_data_read_refused` best effort. No-step-up refusal text is zh-TW + English (`STEP_UP_REQUIRED`). (4) **MCP** (CLI `mcp/personal_target.rs`, called once in `mcp/dispatch.rs::handle_tools_call` before any handler/isolation route, protected mode via `runtime_readiness::is_protected_linux()`): `PERSONAL_TARGET_TOOLS` (agent_update, agent_update_soul, agent_remove, agent_status, evolution_toggle/status, skill_security_scan/graduate/synthesis_status/synthesis_run/extract/gaps/list, shared_skill_adopt `target_agent`, wiki_ls/read/write/search/lint/stats/export/dedup/graph/rebuild_fts/trust_audit/trust_history, mail_list/read/send, codrive_run `agent`) refuse a target that is another person's Personal employee unless the acting agent (`acting_agent_id`) is that employee itself; audited via `append_tool_call_denied(.., "personal_employee_target", ..)`. Tests (not compiled or run here; Linux builder): duduclaw-auth `org::tests::p1_manager_has_no_derived_access_to_personal_employee`; gateway `personal_visibility::tests::{p1_reader_is_owner_admin_break_glass_or_refused, p1_unreadable_mirror_fails_closed, p1_management_refusal_only_for_another_employees_personal_target, p1_unprotected_install_unchanged}`, `handlers::personal_data_gate::tests::{p1_admin_read_requires_step_up_and_audits, p1_gated_methods_and_step_up_field}`; CLI `mcp::personal_target::tests::{p1_cross_agent_tools_refuse_personal_target, p1_cross_agent_tools_unchanged_outside_protected_mode}`. The tests exercise the predicates and the break-glass core with real users.db stores; no test drives `dispatch` with protected mode on (it is an env flag). Open after P1 (status after **P1b**, below): (a) the dashboard UI does not yet prompt for step-up or send `step_up_csrf` on these pages (F4/F5) — **still open**; (b) attachment downloads (`history_attachment_bytes`) have no step-up path, so they are owner-only for Personal employees — **accepted for v1.81** (documented in docs/features/05-security-defense.md ×3), step-up path not added; (c) other cross-agent read surfaces — **closed by P1b** except the residuals listed there; (d) users.db unreadable inside an isolated run — **closed by P1b** (self-targets never read it; cross targets still fail closed); (e) ephemeral sub-agents — **closed by P1b**.
- From P1b (Personal visibility gaps left by P1; protected mode only, nothing changes otherwise). **Predicate** (`personal_visibility.rs`): `DataCategory::Work` (`"work"` in the audit detail) for the work record; `acting_employee(home, acting)` = the id itself for a regular id (no I/O), and for `eph-…` the root of `duduclaw_core::autonomy_gate::effective_identities` (scaffold `ephemeral.toml` `parent` must equal the host-owned `org.toml` `reports_to` at every hop, ≤ 32 hops; anything else ⇒ `None` ⇒ refused) — this is the parent linkage trusted for (e); `same_employee(home, acting, target)` (exact equality, never reads users.db); `management_refusal` now decides `same_employee` BEFORE the users.db read (closes (d) for self-targets and lets a sub-agent act on its parent); `hidden_from_employee(home, protected, acting)` = every mirror row except the acting employee (AI callers get no metadata). The refusal text now says "read, delegate to, change or remove". **MCP** (`mcp/personal_target.rs`, one call `pre_dispatch_refusal` in `mcp/dispatch.rs::handle_tools_call`, same audit row `personal_employee_target`): table additions `spawn_agent.agent_id`, `send_to_agent.agent_id` + `.agent` (every key of a tool is checked: `named_targets`), `check_responses.agent_id`; `task_status` reads the process store, so `PROCESS_STORE_TOOLS` refuses an acting id ≠ `default_agent` when `default_agent` is another person's Personal employee, and `bus_tasks.rs::handle_task_status` replaces the output of steps run by another person's Personal employee (`hidden_step_agents`; unreadable store hides every other employee's step output); `create_task` steps naming one are refused (`bus_tasks.rs`, same predicate as `spawn_agent`); memory `namespace` (after `mcp_namespace::assert_can_access`): a bare id or `internal/<id>` naming another Personal employee is refused, own namespace/`shared/`/`external/` never read users.db (`namespace_refusal`; today `assert_can_access` already confines callers to their own namespace, so this is defence in depth); `list_agents` = `list_agents_hiding` → `agents.rs::list_agents_filtered` hides every Personal employee except the acting one (hidden, not redacted; unreadable store ⇒ tool error `OWNERSHIP_UNAVAILABLE`). **Dashboard** (`handlers/personal_data_gate.rs`, `dispatch` now calls `personal_gates_for_params` for every method): `agent_scoped` adds `activity.list`, `timeline.list`, `decisions.list`/`dismiss`, `canvas.get`, `tasks.list`/`list_page`/`flow_metrics`, `tasks.assign` (destination) as `Work`; new `task_scoped` (tasks.update/remove/assign/archive/unarchive/pin/unpin/rename/comment/comments/iterations/timeline/changes/artifacts/role_turns/goal_decide and `activity.list` by `task_id`) resolves the task's `assigned_to` read-only from `tasks.db` (`task_employee`; unreadable ⇒ refused, unknown ⇒ handler answers) and runs the same owner / audited step-up gate — gets and writes refuse. Unscoped lists hide through `TaskReader` (`task_privacy.rs`): `TaskReader::build(home, ctx, protected)` carries `hidden_from(user)` (unreadable ⇒ `permission denied`), `hides(agent)`, `can_read`/`can_read_row`/`activity_json` restrict hidden employees' rows, `admit_scope(agent_id)` re-admits the listing's own (dispatch-gated) scope in `task_list_reader`/`task_board_reader`/`monitoring_reader`/`tasks.list_page{on_behalf_of:me}`; `tasks.list`, `tasks.list_page` (+ mine), `activity.list`, `timeline.list` (tasks, activities by row and by task employee, heartbeats) drop them. Approvals (`approvals_topology_rpc.rs::handle_approvals_list` → `personal_approval_hidden`): a card of another person's Personal employee is `personal_metadata_card` (id, employee, kind, status, request kind, timing, `restricted`/`personal_employee`; no summary, payload, digest, binding, answer, simulation, channel link, `may_decide:false`); `approvals.get` (one id) by an Admin with `step_up_csrf` runs the break-glass gate (`method = approvals.get`, category `work`) and returns the full card. Tests (not compiled or run here; Linux builder): gateway `personal_visibility::tests::{p1b_ephemeral_subagent_is_its_parent_for_own_data, p1b_hidden_from_employee_hides_every_other_personal_employee, p1b_self_target_decided_before_users_db}`, `handlers::personal_data_gate::tests::{p1b_work_methods_gated_by_agent_or_task, p1b_task_employee_read_before_the_handler, p1b_admin_work_read_is_audited_as_work, p1b_task_reader_hides_personal_rows_from_non_owner, p1b_unprotected_task_reader_unchanged, p1b_approval_card_of_personal_employee_is_metadata_only}`; CLI `mcp::personal_target::tests::{p1b_list_agents_hides_other_personal_employees, p1b_delegation_and_response_tools_refuse_personal_target, p1b_task_status_refuses_foreign_store_and_hides_personal_step_output, p1b_memory_namespace_of_another_personal_employee_refused, p1b_self_targets_never_read_users_db, p1b_ephemeral_subagent_acts_as_its_parent, p1b_unprotected_install_unchanged}`. As in P1, no test drives `dispatch`/`handle_approvals_list` with protected mode on (env flag); the tests cover the predicates, the tables, `TaskReader::build(.., true)` and the metadata card. Still open (status after **P1c**, below): (1) `approvals.decide` on a Personal employee's card is unchanged (an Admin can decide a card it sees as metadata only; the listing says `may_decide:false`) — needs an owner/step-up rule with the approval owner — **closed by P1c** (dashboard + every channel decision path); (2) `tasks.create` with `assigned_to` = another person's Personal employee is not gated (the isolated runtime already refuses background work for Personal targets); `team_handoff`, `goals_create`, `plan_*` and other delegation tools beyond the four above are not in the table — **closed by P1c** (`team_handoff`/`goals_create`/`plan_start`/`plan_update_step` name no other employee; `plan_get` by id is gated); (3) `tasks.list_page`/`activity.list` `total` still counts hidden rows (a count, no content), and unscoped Admin `tasks.flow_metrics` still lists per-employee counts for Personal employees (metadata) — **closed by P1c**; (4) live push events (`task.*`, `activity.new`, `approval.*`) are not Personal-aware — they carry ids/status only and the refetch goes through the gates above, but an Admin still sees that a hidden employee's task changed — **closed by P1c** (per-subscriber filter, not id-stripping); (5) the step-up `approvals.get` and break-glass `tasks.list` replies still pass through `TaskReader`/may_read for everything else, and a `tasks.update` reply after break-glass is a board card; (6) MCP memory namespaces are NOT widened for sub-agents (an `eph-` sub-agent still cannot read its parent's namespace; `assert_can_access` unchanged); (7) the eph parent chain trusts the host-owned `org.toml` + scaffold sidecar (the same authority as the autonomy gate); a legacy, non-isolated employee that can write `<home>` could forge both, as it can forge anything there.
- From P1c (closes P1b open items 1–4; protected mode only, unprotected installs unchanged). **Predicates** (`personal_visibility.rs`): `DataCategory::Approval` (`"approval"` in the audit detail); `WorkItem::{Task, Plan}` + `work_item_employee(home, item, id)` (read-only `tasks.db`: `tasks.assigned_to` / `plans.agent_id`; unreadable ⇒ `Unavailable`); `delegation_refusal(home, protected, acting, target)` = `management_refusal`, except an acting employee that is itself a Personal employee of the same owner (both owners read fresh, exact equality, unreadable ⇒ `OWNERSHIP_UNAVAILABLE`) may hand work over (the owner's own work); `delegation_refusal_for_user(home, protected, user, agent)` = owner only (`classify_reader` with `is_admin=false`, so an Admin is refused: delegation has no break-glass); texts `PERSONAL_DELEGATION_REFUSED`, `PERSONAL_DECISION_REFUSED` (zh-TW + English). New `crate::personal_decision` (gateway): `card_employee(home, rec)` (= `agent_id`; blank ⇒ the binding task / payload task ref → `tasks.assigned_to`, unreadable ⇒ refused), `decider_refusal(home, protected, employee, decider)` (owner only; `system`/blank/none refused; the decider lookup runs only for Personal cards), `channel_card_refusal(_with)` and `channel_agent_refusal` (decider = `trigger_route::access::verified_user_for_channel_sender`, i.e. a verified, active account). **(1) Decisions.** Dashboard: new `handlers/personal_decide_gate.rs::personal_decide_gate(ctx, record, csrf, method)` — card employee + live identity (`live_reader_context`) → the P1 `personal_data_gate` with `Target{method, category: Approval}`: owner passes; Admin not owner needs the fresh step-up + `step_up_csrf` and the `isolation.personal_data_read` row (`category = "approval"`, `method = approvals.decide | mail.decide`) is written before the decision, else `DECIDE_STEP_UP_REQUIRED` (says step-up is needed); everyone else `PERSONAL_DECISION_REFUSED`. Call sites: `handlers/approvals_decide.rs::handle_approvals_decide_inner` right after the Personal→Shared delegation-card check (before every per-kind gate, expiry, digest or specialized path; `params` now `mut`, `take_step_up`), `handlers/mail_rpc.rs::handle_mail_decide` after the outbound-kind check. Channels (no step-up possible ⇒ owner's own verified account only): `approval_notify.rs::apply_decision` after the dashboard-only check (covers button presses, `decision_text` replies, the Mini App via `decision_notify::route_press`, and unbound strict text commands through `apply_bound_request`), `decision_notify.rs::apply_bound_request` after the bound-context check (bound decisions and question answers), `goal_notify.rs::authorize_goal_press` (goal kickoff approvals and needs-human goal cards). `me.isolation.decide_personal_delegation` (owner) and `install_requests.decide` (InstallRequestStore, no employee card) unchanged. **(2) Delegation.** Dashboard: `handlers/personal_delegation_gate.rs` (`delegation_target_key`: `tasks.create`/`tasks.update` `assigned_to`, `tasks.goal_create`/`plans.create` `agent_id`; `delegation_refusal` re-reads the caller only for a Personal target, revoked ⇒ `permission denied`), called first in `personal_gates_for_params` (so for every method). MCP (`mcp/personal_target.rs`): schema scan of `duduclaw_core::mcp_tool_defs` for `agent_id`/`agent`/`assigned_to`/`target_agent`/`reports_to` — table additions `tasks_create`/`tasks_update`/`tasks_list` `assigned_to`, `discovery_catalog`/`discovery_list`/`activity_list`/`list_cron_tasks`/`list_reminders`/`submit_feedback`/`audit_trail_query`/`reliability_summary`/`cost_summary` `agent_id`, `create_agent`/`agent_update` `reports_to`; `DELEGATION_TOOLS` (spawn_agent, send_to_agent, tasks_create, tasks_update, discovery_catalog) use `delegation_refusal`, the rest stay self-only; `PERSONAL_ID_TARGETS` (`tasks_update`/`activity_list` `task_id`, `plan_get` `plan_id`) resolve the employee via `work_item_employee` in `id_target_refusal` (own items never read users.db; skipped while an isolated call is current, where the signed Task grant + boundary resolver already pin live ownership). `team_handoff` (role→role inside the member's host-pinned task), `goals_create` (no employee), `plan_start` (caller's own plan) and `plan_update_step` (holder rule: own steps) name no other employee — not listed, documented in the table comment. **(3) Counts.** New `task_store/personal_hiding.rs` (child of `activity.rs` via `#[path]`; `task_store/mod.rs` is over 800 lines): `list_tasks_page_hiding` (the old `list_tasks_page_for_principal` body moved out of `tasks.rs`, which now delegates with an empty set) + `hidden_json`; `activity.rs::list_activity_hiding` (row employee and task employee, `json_each`), `principal.rs::list_principal_page_hiding`, `iterations.rs::flow_metrics_hiding` (per-employee rows and board totals); - From P1h (5th security review: GO conditional on N1; protected mode only — unprotected installs unchanged). **N1 (channel calls / assignments).** `trigger_route::access::SenderAccess::grant` (→ `grant_under`) and `trigger_route::assign::assign_right` (→ `assign_right_under`) ask the installed restriction's `denies_delegation(person, agent)` (calling or assigning makes the employee run work: delegation, never break-glass): the mapped person must be the owner — Admins, bindings, team grants and the conversation default no longer reach someone else's Personal employee; an unmapped sender is `""` (no person ⇒ never an owner); the synthetic "everyone" sender of `calls_anyone` (`Admin { user_id: None }`) is not a person and is not checked. Exception: the employee's own `[channels] public_trigger` (written only by `agents.update`, a P1e Config write: owner or audited Admin step-up) keeps calling open to everyone as `GrantSource::Public`, and assignment becomes `CallOnly`. WebChat: `authorize_command` lost its Admin early return — `/交辦 <text>` goes through `webchat::access::assignment_text_allowed` (Admins: refused only when the mention resolves to nothing but a refused employee, unknown names keep the legacy usage reply; others: the existing non-enumerating target list), `assignment_mention_targets` filters an Admin's WebChat targets too, resuming a schedule goes through `authorize_target` (Personal check before its Admin shortcut) while an Admin may still pause / remove their OWN schedule (it only stops work; legacy rows stay cleanable); `resolve_new_agent`'s Admin `None` is followed by `authorize_target` on the default it falls back to. **Runtime trace:** a default-routed (no `@`) channel turn on a Personal employee is refused by the isolated runtime for anyone but the owner — `isolation_mcp::ingress::reply_scope` captures only a verified mapped channel account (an unmapped sender captures nothing ⇒ `Unavailable`), and `authorize_current` → `boot::record_authorization` → `issuance_facts` → `authority::personal_facts` requires principal = lifecycle owner = mirror owner, sole member (only when `execution_gate::isolation_required()`). **Low:** `UserContext::can_call_agent` uses `denies_delegation` (a break-glass read of the request never opens calling). **N2 (availability).** `personal_visibility::owner_map`: one owner-map snapshot per dashboard request (the `with_break_glass_scope` task-local now holds break-glass + owner maps) and a process cache keyed on `auth_meta.acl_generation` (bumped by every mirror write) plus users.db / users.db-wal file stamps (size, mtime, dev, inode, mode, uid — also catches raw writes and permission changes the hardened opener would refuse); `isolation_lifecycle::record_personal_mirror` / `remove_personal_mirror` call `invalidate_owner_cache`; a read error is never cached (every caller still refuses). `personal_owner` / `personal_owners` / `hidden_from` / `hidden_from_employee` all read through it. **N3.** `personal_p1g_gate::LIFECYCLE_ALLOWLIST`: a frozen, per-method reviewed list (21 methods: 5 connector, 2 recording, 4 employee, 4 `me.isolation.*`, 6 `migration.confirmations.*`) replaces the `isolation.*` / `migration.*` / `me.isolation.*` prefix match; `p1h_lifecycle_allowlist_equals_the_dispatch_table` keeps it equal to the dispatched set. `isolation.recording_status` / `_stop` return `result: null, withheld: true` for a Personal employee's recording unless the caller is its owner (`SqliteExecutionJobs::admin_recording_owner`, read from the job's `binding`); `connector_list` routes name an owner user id, never an employee. **N4.** `fire_time_refusal`: the "acting employee" exception applies only when NO principal is recorded (blank values count as absent); a recorded non-owner principal (Admin, unmapped, `system`, malformed) always refuses, as B1 in `personal_delegation::gate` — a forged JSONL bus line naming the target as its own sender is refused (`dispatcher::bus_fire_refusal_in`). **N5.** `db_sources.grants.list` → `db_source_grants::list_hiding` leaves hidden employees out (their rows carry `id`, which the generic hidden-row filter does not match); `db_sources.grants.set` → `set_hiding` merges hidden employees' current grants back (never in added / removed / unchanged, never named in the activity row) and refuses a request naming one. **N6.** `agents.org_chart`: `agents_list_rpc::org_chart_full` drops hidden employees from the full rows (Admin and department scope included); a hidden ancestor of a visible employee stays a name + role stub. `topology.list` left the metadata allowlist (`METADATA_CEILING` 14 → 13), so its `from_agent` / `to_agent` rows go through the hidden-row filter. **Release note:** legacy crons / reminders on a Personal employee with no recorded creator stop at fire time (CHANGELOG Changed, `docs/` note). Tests: `p1h_*` (trigger_route/p1h_tests.rs, personal_visibility_tests.rs, personal_p1g_tests.rs, db_source_grants.rs, dispatcher.rs, duduclaw-auth acl.rs). Open: `db_sources.remove` (`revoke_everywhere`) still names every former holder in its activity row (no caller context there); the request-scope owner map is a snapshot — a lifecycle write in a blocking-pool closure cannot drop it (the generation key covers every later request).
- From F6a/F6b (F6 design r5 §3.1–§3.2, §3.4 log): **Schema (apply.db)** lives in new `duduclaw-gateway/src/isolation_quarantine.rs::ensure_employee_schema` (the handler's `ensure_employee_schema` delegates): one IMMEDIATE tx, idempotent; adds `confirmed_employees.incarnation INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` (and `tenant_id` as before), `employee_quarantine` (PK `(employee_hex, incarnation)`, `seal` CHECKed to the §3.4 states, `kind`/`owner_user_id` consistency CHECK, `pending_fences`/`stripped` JSON, the F6c/F6g/F6i columns present but unused), `root_allocations(employee_hex PK, root_item, root_state CHECK, retire_proposal_id, retired_at)` and `employee_schema_version(id=1, version)`; a version newer than `EMPLOYEE_SCHEMA_VERSION = 1` refuses writers (`SchemaError::Future`) and readers (`confirmed_row` → `Unavailable`). `save_confirmed_receipt` keeps `incarnation` for the same employee id and writes 0 for a new id. Readers use the column when present, else 0 (like F6m); NULL/negative/>u32 is `Unavailable`. **Schema (users.db)**: new `duduclaw-auth/src/admin_eligibility.rs` — `admin_eligibility_log(seq AUTOINCREMENT, user_id, eligible, reason, actor_user_id, notice_address, at unix-seconds)` written by **SQLite triggers** (so every writer, present or future, logs in the writer's own statement/tx): users insert of an Admin, update of `role`/`status`/`must_change_password`/`email` on a row that is or becomes Admin, delete of an Admin (notice address kept); insert/delete of `mfa_credentials`/`mfa_passkeys`/`mfa_email_credentials` (+ email-credential update) of an Admin; lockout = `mfa_credentials.failed_attempts` or `mfa_factor_failures.attempts` reaching 5 (logged with unchanged eligibility). Eligible = role admin ∧ active ∧ no forced password ∧ (TOTP ∨ passkey ∨ email factor for the current address). Append-only (delete and field updates aborted by triggers) except a one-time `actor_user_id` stamp: `high_water` + `stamp_actor` inside the writer's tx, wired into `set_user_status_as`, `apply_user_update_as`, `create_user_as`, `create_member_at_epoch`, `offboard_user`, both admin MFA resets, and the gateway `users.create/update/remove` handlers (old APIs delegate with `None`). Seeded once (`seed` row per eligible account) with `auth_meta.admin_eligibility_log_since`; `auth_meta.admin_eligibility_log_schema` > 1 makes `UserDb::new` fail. **F6c2 must:** treat a NULL actor on a promotion/creation as possibly the requester (CLI, migration, self-service and any unwired writer are unattributed); read `log_since` for the 7-day coverage rule; note the lockout threshold is duplicated as `LOCKOUT_ATTEMPTS` (keep equal to `mfa::check_factor`). **Lifecycle (A1)**: `EmployeeLifecycle.incarnation: u32` from the same row read; `recheck_with` refuses another incarnation `Stale` (another id stays `Tombstoned`). Not re-read through the F6m reader at each recheck (same row); `lifecycle_incarnation_matches_token_reader` asserts they agree. **Tombstone** = `isolation_lifecycle::tombstone_employee(home, label, employee, &TombstoneEnv{actor, runs, now}) -> TombstoneOutcome` (Personal and Shared): reads (row, Applied item, principal snapshot — Personal: credential_epoch, email, Google `sub` from `person_credentials`, SHA-256 of the TOTP/email/passkey set, notice address; Shared: pinned tenant + every `(user_id, access_level, is_primary)`; unpinned legacy rows `Denied`) → one apply.db tx (re-check id + incarnation, NOREPLACE move to `isolation-quarantine/<hex>-<inc>-<label>` from held fds, disable, drop marker, insert quarantine row `pending`/`absent`/`invalid` and `root_allocations` once per id with `{item, receipt}`) → seal outside the lock through the descriptor held since the move (`walk`: `fstatat(NOFOLLOW)` + `openat(O_NOFOLLOW)`, opened inode must equal the stat'ed one, same `st_dev`, owner = euid, nlink 1, no special files, Linux `flistxattr` except `security.selinux` (verify on the box; symlinks' own xattrs are not checked), ≤ 65 536 entries / 1 GiB / depth 16; digest domain `duduclaw-quarantine-v1`, `put` framing, raw-byte path order, root mode, synthetic `org:<label>`; stat manifest = root dev/ino + `[hex path, ino, size, ctime_ns]`) → run stop + C6 detail-route revocation with pending fences grouped `runs:` / `route…` / `approvals:` (`replace_fence_group`) → users.db (Personal mirror, or new `duduclaw_auth::db::remove_shared_bindings_in_transaction`, both bump `acl_generation`) → audit `isolation.employee_tombstoned`. Retries are idempotent (first row and snapshot kept, `pending` seal re-run via the quarantined dir checked against the recorded dev/ino). **Deviation**: `[channels.*]` and `[capabilities] db_sources` are stripped from the moved `agent.toml` right after the rename, inside the same apply.db tx, not before it — so a refused move (existing target, symlinked roots; the SR2 tests) never edits a live employee; the seal still covers the stripped file. **S13**: spawnd has only `Cancel{run}`, no stop/list by employee, so production `SpawndRunStopper` fails closed: the tombstone completes and records `runs:stop_unconfirmed` (blocks every restore until a later retry confirms). Add a spawnd stop-and-confirm-by-employee request (F6l/F6c) and replace that body. **RPC**: `isolation.employee_tombstone` calls `tombstone_employee`, stays Personal-only (`NOT_PERSONAL` for Shared until F6d), returns `seal`, `digest_prefix`, `incarnation`, `pending_fences`, `routes_revoked`, `runs_stopped`; afterwards `invalidate_employee_approvals` (A8) invalidates pending cards of the label and Personal-delegation cards from/to it, failures ⇒ `approvals:unavailable`. **Authorities**: A2 through the lifecycle pinned in `PreparedAuthority` (`incarnation()` getter; `GrantPolicy` in mcp-boundary unchanged, it carries no employee id); A3 `RecordAuthority.incarnation` + `caller_incarnation` (serde default 0, omitted when 0, so pre-F6 receipts and their approval payloads stay byte-identical); A4 `Gate.source_incarnation` in the card payload, checked at `admit` against the receipt and in `me.isolation.decide_personal_delegation` against the live lifecycle (pre-F6 cards without the field are not approvable); A5 `GoogleAccountRoutes.pinned` + `pinned_to` (the double lifecycle read already compared the whole lifecycle). **Deferred / not done**: A6 — no `incarnation` column on C6 detail rows (prior pins die by the tombstone-time revocation; `prior_incarnation_detail_pin_refused`, `revokes_employee_detail_routes_only`, `keeps_tenant_routes_for_other_employees` not written); A5 for Shared-tenant `SelectedConnector` selections; A7 `policy_epoch` bump at tombstone (only `acl_generation` is bumped; restore/adoption bumps are F6c/F6g); A8 `ActionGrant`s and incarnation in other approval payloads; A9 (F6c); A10 sessions (no `incarnation` on session rows); A12 provider-cache eviction (only the existing registry rescan); A13, store marks, writer gate, global-route pause and `website_credentials.allowed_agents` stripping (F6h; `stripped.deferred` lists them); `oversize_still_tombstones_and_reports` exists only at walker level (`manifest_bounds_report_oversize`). Tests added (not compiled or run here; Linux builder): auth `admin_eligibility_log_written_on_every_change`, `admin_eligibility_log_shares_the_writer_transaction`, `admin_eligibility_log_is_append_only`, `admin_eligibility_log_seeded_once_and_future_schema_refused`; gateway `lifecycle_incarnation_change_is_stale` (A1), `lifecycle_incarnation_matches_token_reader`, `apply_preserves_incarnation`, `new_id_starts_at_incarnation_zero`, `legacy_row_reads_incarnation_zero`, `future_employee_schema_refused`, `incarnation_mismatch_refused_everywhere` (A1 + A11 + fence writer), `prior_incarnation_grant_policy_refused` (A2), `prior_incarnation_record_authority_refused` (A3), `delegation_card_from_prior_incarnation_never_binds` + `delegation_decide_requires_card_incarnation` (A4), `prior_incarnation_selection_refused` (A5), `tombstone_invalidates_employee_approvals` (A8), `tombstone_seals_manifest_with_ctime`, `tombstone_seals_manifest_outside_lock`, `tombstone_absent_dir_seals_absent`, `tombstone_strips_channel_tokens` (incl. audited), `tombstone_cancels_live_runs_or_fails_closed`, `tombstone_snapshots_owner_identity`, `tombstone_shared_snapshots_tenant_and_members` (incl. bindings removed + ACL bump), `tombstone_legacy_unpinned_shared_refused`, `tombstone_second_tombstone_after_restore_uses_new_key` (incl. root_allocations once per id), `manifest_orders_raw_bytes`, `manifest_rejects_hardlink_foreign_uid_xattr`, `manifest_bounds_report_oversize`, `employee_schema_idempotent_and_upgrades_legacy_rows`, `employee_schema_refuses_unknown_future_version`, `quarantine_rows_keyed_by_employee_and_incarnation`, `fence_groups_replace_only_their_own_entries`, `strip_agent_toml_removes_channels_and_db_grants_only`. - From F6c (same-id restore engine, F6 design r5 §3.4–§3.7): new `duduclaw-gateway/src/isolation_restore.rs` (journal, request filing, cancel/deny/expire, digests, `Refusal` codes) with child modules `isolation_restore_root.rs` (`restore_precheck` + seams), `isolation_restore_checks.rs` (common checks) and `isolation_restore_apply.rs` (approval call, tx1/tx2 apply, crash recovery); registered in `lib.rs` next to `isolation_quarantine` (`#[allow(dead_code)]` until F6d). **Journal**: apply.db `employee_restores` exactly as §3.4 (CHECKed kinds/states, partial unique index = one open request per quarantine row), created by `ensure_restore_schema` (additive; `EMPLOYEE_SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 1). L1: ≤ 3 requests per row per 30 days; `expires_at = min(created + 72 h, quarantined_at + 90 d)`. Request digest (`duduclaw-restore-request-v1`, `put` framing) covers id, kind, employee, incarnation, quarantine digest, tenant, owner, member selection and the route digest (Q4, `(account, generation)` list); both re-checked at approval. **Common checks** (`check_outside` before tx1, `check_apply_db` inside each apply.db tx with `Stage::{Request, Approval, Apply}`): row `sealed` (`restoring` in tx2) inside 90 d, `root_state = allocated` (`retiring` ⇒ `RETIRE_IN_PROGRESS`), no other row of the label `restoring`/`adopting`, no other open request, fences empty (runs/route fences retried once first), no detail route naming the hex, no marker row, no open Employee proposal (`label_pending` rule), `agents/<label>` absent, pin == row tenant == users.db tenant, disabled confirmed row with the same id + incarnation + owner, users.db side finished (mirror, bindings, removal grant; retried once ⇒ `TOMBSTONE_INCOMPLETE`), agent cap (`RestoreEnv.agent_cap` from the RPC's license read, L2), store marks recorded and equal (§3.3), root precheck. Personal: caller == receipt owner (others `NOT_FOUND`), factor proof present (`OwnerProof`; strongest-factor rule is F6d), `owner_eligible` at request, approval and inside the mirror tx; Q3 via new **users.db `credential_changes`** (`duduclaw-auth/src/credential_changes.rs`, trigger-written for password/email/TOTP/passkey/email-factor changes, append-only, one-time stamp: `stamp_owner` = owner-authenticated self-service, `stamp_actor` = anyone else): any change since the tombstone not `by_owner`, any snapshot difference without a log row, or a changed Google `sub` (not in users.db) refuses `CREDENTIAL_CHANGED`. **R1 hook**: trait `ApprovalRule::decide(users_tx, requester, approver, kind) -> Result<Approval{self_approved}, &'static str>` called in tx1's users read and again inside the tx2 users.db write tx; the engine also refuses an answer whose `self_approved` ≠ `approver == requester`. Production `ApprovalRuleUnavailable` refuses everything until **F6c2** plugs in. **Root precheck** `restore_precheck(home, label, employee, &dyn RootProbe) -> RootClass {Ok, Unavailable, Gone, Inconsistent, Retired, Busy}`: `RootError::NotFound` is the only `Gone`; `Inconsistent`, any ReadBack mismatch (employee, UID, binding, label, bytes, inodes, principal, disabled) and phase Disabling/Disabled ⇒ `Inconsistent`; phase Retiring/Retired or `root_state` retiring/retired ⇒ `Retired` (`root_absent` ⇒ `Gone`); transport/no reply/timeout/`IsolationUnavailable`/`NotReady` ⇒ `Unavailable`; idle from the schema-2 `idle` field or `RootProbe::employee_idle`. Production `SpawndRootProbe`: schema-1 ReadBack (`Authentication` ⇒ `Inconsistent`, every other error ⇒ `Unavailable`, never `Gone`); **`employee_idle` fails closed** (no gateway client for H4's `EmployeeIdle` exists) ⇒ every production restore is `ROOT_UNAVAILABLE` until F6d wires that client or F6l adds `idle` to ReadBack. **Apply** (§3.5): recovery first; verify in quarantine outside every lock through `reopen_quarantined` + `quarantine::seal` (digest ≠ but stat manifest = ⇒ `ORG_CHANGED`, row stays sealed; else `INTEGRITY` + row `invalid`); tx1 (checks again, request `applying` + approver, row `restoring`, `closed_by_request` = request id); tx2 (`synchronous=FULL`, IMMEDIATE held to the end): NOREPLACE move back from held fds, live root `(dev, ino)` = recorded, stat re-walk against step 1 (mismatch ⇒ moved back, `needs_reseal`), route digest and store marks again, users.db IMMEDIATE tx (tenant, `owner_eligible`, rule, new `duduclaw_auth::db::restore_personal_mirror_in_transaction` / `restore_shared_bindings_in_transaction`, both refuse any existing mirror/binding/removal grant and bump `acl_generation` **and** the isolation policy epoch), then the exact-text `UPDATE confirmed_employees … incarnation = incarnation + 1`, row `restored`, request `done` (+ `self_approved`); any refusal after the move moves the tree back and records `sealed`/`needs_reseal` + `failed: <code>` in tx2; after commit `read_lifecycle_with` must see the new incarnation, else Critical audit and `RESTORE_INCONSISTENT`. **Recovery** `recover_label` (also run before every request and approval): expire stale requests; `restoring` + disabled row ⇒ tree renamed back if it carries the recorded `(dev, ino)` (foreign tree ⇒ alert), users.db side removed (`remove_personal_mirror`, whose contract in `db.rs` and `isolation_lifecycle.rs` now names this path, or `remove_shared_bindings`), `needs_reseal` then immediate reseal, request `failed: interrupted`; live row with incarnation + 1 and `closed_by_request` = the applying request ⇒ roll forward; otherwise Critical alert, request `failed: inconsistent`, row left `restoring` (every later step on the label refuses). `needs_reseal` ⇒ re-verify against the original digest ⇒ `sealed` (fresh stat manifest) or `invalid`. `restore_in_progress(home, label)` and `all_rows_closed(home, label)` (S7) are exported for F6d/F6h/F6l. Every transition is audited through `audit_and_emit` with request id, kind, employee hex, incarnation, digest prefix, requester, approver, `self_approved` (`isolation.employee_restore_{requested,refused,failed,approved,cancelled,denied,expired,recovered,inconsistent,self_approved}`, `isolation.employee_restored`, `isolation.quarantine_resealed`). Lifecycle helpers `confirmed_row`, `applied_item`, `principal`, `principal_snapshot`, `owner_eligible`, `reopen_quarantined`, `open_store`, `valid_label`, `mirror_error` and the store-name consts became `pub(crate)` (no behaviour change). **Deferred / not done**: Shared request filing (F6k; apply/recovery already take the Shared kind and enforce selection ⊆ snapshot at ≤ snapshot level); adoption (F6g); the RPCs, notices, registry rescan and provider-cache eviction after commit (F6d, async); marker re-insert after restore (§3.5 d "inferred, verify": not done, so the restored dir carries no creation marker and no proposal can adopt it — stricter); stamping `credential_changes` from the self-service password/email/MFA writers (F6d: until then every owner change reads unattributed and refuses); `RESTORE_IN_PROGRESS` inside `tombstone_employee`/propose/purge/retire (F6d uses `restore_in_progress`); `LabelStores` production registry (`UnregisteredStores` refuses `STORES_UNAVAILABLE`; F6h records marks at tombstone); A9 work-item re-approval and new route selections for the new incarnation (§3.7, F6d/F6k). **So production restore stays refused** by three fail-closed seams (rule, idle, store marks) until F6c2/F6d/F6h/F6l land. Tests (`isolation_restore_tests.rs` and its child `isolation_restore_recovery_tests.rs`, children of `isolation_lifecycle_tests` to reuse the real-store fixture; Unix; not compiled or run here — Linux builder): `restore_same_id_same_owner_ok`, `restore_refuses_ineligible_owner` (request, approval, mirror tx), `restore_refuses_credential_change_by_non_owner`, `restore_allows_owner_own_credential_change`, `restore_refuses_incomplete_tombstone`, `restore_refuses_pending_fence_and_retries`, `restore_refuses_after_90_days`, `restore_refuses_unsealed_or_oversize`, `restore_rechecks_quota`, `restore_refuses_open_proposal_or_marker`, `restore_integrity_mismatch_in_quarantine`, `restore_ctime_change_after_move_moves_back`, `restore_routes_changed_refused`, `restore_store_marks_changed_refused`, `restore_org_entry_changed_refused`, `restore_root_live_process_refused`, `restore_transport_error_is_unavailable_not_gone`, `restore_readback_principal_mismatch_is_inconsistent_not_gone`, `restore_timeout_is_unavailable_not_gone`, `restore_readback_requires_idle`, `restore_crash_rename`, `restore_crash_users_db`, `restore_crash_commit`, `restore_live_row_without_request_match_alerts`, `restore_every_transition_audited`, `restore_apply_path_still_never_revives_disabled_row`, `restore_then_tombstone_purge_retire_frees_label` (purge and retire simulated by SQL: F6i/F6l); auth `credential_changes::tests::credential_changes_logged_by_trigger_and_stamped_once`. **F6fa fix round (branch `fix/iso181-f6fa-20261109`, review NO-GO items 2–4, 6):** (2) the apply tx re-runs what `check_outside` read: `apply_locked` recounts live `confirmed_employees` on its own apply.db IMMEDIATE connection (`check_quota_in`) and re-reads open Employee proposals (`check_no_open_proposal`) under that writer lock before the move; `restore_users` runs the Q3 `check_credentials` inside its users.db IMMEDIATE tx (`principal_snapshot_in` reads the current facts on that tx); new test hook `HookPoint::BeforeTx1`. (3) Q3 maps each compared snapshot field to the log fields that may explain it (`SNAPSHOT_LOG_FIELDS`: `email`←email, `factors_digest`←totp/passkey/email_factor, `credential_epoch`←all five) and needs ≥ 1 owner-stamped row of a mapped field per differing field; the tombstone snapshot records `credential_seq` (the `credential_changes` high-water `seq`, same users.db read) and the log is read with new `credential_changes::changes_after(seq)`, never by wall clock; a snapshot without it refuses `SNAPSHOT_INCOMPLETE` (new `Refusal`, terminal). Residual: a sessions revoke-all (owner or Admin) after the tombstone bumps `credential_epoch` without a credential row and therefore refuses (fail closed). (4) `OwnerProof.factor` accepts only `PROOF_FACTORS` = passkey/totp/email (`google_reauth` removed; `isolation_adopt.rs:446` still lists it — out of this branch's scope, unreachable through the RPC whose `verify_factor` parses only the three names; part (b) should drop it). (6) §3.5 d marker re-insert, verified: **not re-inserted** — `employee_dir_markers` only lets a Personal proposal adopt a directory that flow created while no live row holds the label (`check_marker` refuses `LABEL_TAKEN` for a live row before reading any marker; F6g's `adopted` marker identifies an adoption's tree); a restored label is held by its live confirmed row and the R7/restore checks, and a re-inserted `created` marker would let a later proposal treat the restored tree as its own. Tests (`f6fa_`; not compiled or run here — macOS worktree, no shell): `isolation_restore_tests::{f6fa_quota_recounted_in_apply_tx, f6fa_open_proposal_rechecked_in_apply_tx, f6fa_credential_change_rechecked_in_apply_tx, f6fa_q3_maps_each_snapshot_field_to_its_log_fields, f6fa_q3_counts_by_seq_not_clock, f6fa_legacy_snapshot_without_credential_seq_refused, f6fa_google_reauth_not_an_accepted_factor, f6fa_restored_label_protected_without_marker}`.
