`RootAllocator`/`EmployeeAuthority` stay schema 1); `RETIRE_FIELDS` (all eleven required: `early, employee_hex, employee_id, quarantine_digest_prefix, reason=purged, requested_by, storage=wipe, target_proposal_digest, target_proposal_id, target_revision, uid` — canonical lowercase hex/decimal, UID in 70 000–99 999) with `retire_specification`/`retire_fields`; `operation(binding, OperationKind)` with `Allocate 0 / Compensate 1 / Retire 2` (old ids unchanged, golden-tested); `Request::validate` refuses `Retire`, `validate_retire` requires schema 2 + Applying + approver; `check_response` (client side: error/receipt exclusivity, Retire receipt must equal target/label/employee/UID with phase Retired, ReadBack any phase, idle only for Ready); `transport::exchange` (schema 2) beside the unchanged `transport::request` (schema 1 only). `confirmation.rs`: `ConfirmationError::ForwardOnly`; `reject` of an `EmployeeRetire` past Pending calls the compensation read-back and, if it fails, restores the prior status (Rejecting ⇒ Failed) and returns `ForwardOnly` (nothing sticks in Rejecting); `retry` of a retire skips the compensation call; `ConfirmationStore::approver(binding)` = actor of the latest `approved` audit row. **spawnd** (`allocation.rs`, Linux): journal schema 3 (`retire`, `retired_at`; schemas 1–2 read and may not carry them; shape and S6 collision checks in `decode_journal`); `RegistryOps` seam (real `RegistryStore` / in-memory registry for portable tests); `Env { registry, accounts, storage, host_busy, idle, now_unix }`; the host gate moved into the allocator (Retired replay answered before it, S15; ReadBack never gated); `Allocator::subject` tells the host whose `EmployeeIdle` (H4 `employee_idle`, uid **or** gid) to read for a schema-2 ReadBack or a Ready retire; retire steps 1–8 exactly as §3.12 (account must exist with uid = gid = spec UID, else `Inconsistent`; a process with no run ⇒ `Intruder` audit + `not_ready`; `Retiring` saved before side effects; `registry.retire` → `recover_locked` → `storage.destroy(operation(retire, Retire))` → no trace/lease → `Retired`); `absence()` = S5b (`NotFound` only with no trace of the expected employee anywhere and no untracked non-Tombstone account or image; unreadable storage ⇒ unavailable); `Allocator::open` refuses a missing journal while the registry has a non-Tombstone account or `Storage::traces` reports anything (S5a; new `Storage::traces` default refuses, `LoopStorage` = inventory ∪ non-Destroyed ledger, `AnchoredStorage` forwards; **deviation**: an unreadable storage at open does not refuse — nothing can allocate/retire/`NotFound` without it); `Host::allocation` now returns `Result<allocation::Reply, allocation::ErrorCode>` (`host_worker::AllocationWork` follows); `audit::Event::Retired` + `Detail::Retired { uid, label, used_bytes, used_inodes, quota_bytes, quota_inodes, quarantine_digest_prefix, requested_by, approved_by, early }` (validated values only). **Gateway**: `isolation_retire.rs` (+ `isolation_retire_apply.rs`): `RootClass` (`ROOT_UNAVAILABLE/GONE/INCONSISTENT/RETIRED`) with `classify_readback` (any UID/label/quota/binding/principal mismatch or non-Ready/Retired phase ⇒ Inconsistent; transport/NotReady/unavailable ⇒ Unavailable; only `NotFound` ⇒ Gone) and `root_precheck` (retiring/retired ⇒ `ROOT_RETIRED`, root_absent ⇒ `ROOT_GONE`, both without the root; allocated ⇒ schema-2 ReadBack, returns `idle` for F6c M8); `employee_retire_propose` (R11 read, root ReadBack, then ONE apply.db IMMEDIATE tx: R11 again, stage the proposal in the confirmation store, `root_state = retiring` + `retire_proposal_id`; `NotFound` ⇒ `root_absent` + operator alert, no proposal; `Inconsistent` ⇒ alert, stays allocated; phase Retired ⇒ S5c reconcile to `retired`); `purge_hook(home, employee, retire_ticked, env)` for F6i; `RetireBackend` (approval/apply/compensation; approver = live Admin while Pending, else the approval record; early-purged rows run the F6c2 rule (`ApprovalRule` seam) with requester = proposer at approval and at every apply step; `disable_and_read_back` is `Ok` only while the root reports Ready, and resets `root_state = allocated` only when the card is being rejected); a Retired receipt matching `root_item` sets `root_state = retired`, `retired_at` and replaces the label's (disabled) confirmed row receipt if it still holds the old id, so `resolve` answers `Tombstoned` without the root; host busy is recorded in the additive table `employee_retire_waits` (no employee-schema bump) and alerts the operator once after 24 h (S15); `label_free(home, label, &dyn LabelStores)` (R7 + S10 positive zero via the F6h seam). `handlers/migration_authority_retire.rs`: `EmployeeRetireAuthority` (`MigrationAuthorityBackend`, error mapping; `ForwardOnly` ⇒ `Compensation`). Audit events: `isolation.employee_retire_proposed`, `_approved` (per stage attempt), `_self_approved`, `_rejected`, `_host_busy`, `isolation.employee_retired`, `isolation.employee_root_absent`, `isolation.employee_root_inconsistent`; each also goes to the `RetireNotifier` seam (owner + log-eligible Admins, F6d delivers). Tests (not compiled or run here: macOS worktree, no shell; Linux builder): migration `allocation_retire_requires_applying_retire_item`, `allocation_retire_fields_digest_bound`, `allocation_operation_kinds_distinct_and_stable`, `allocation_response_schema2_error_code`, `confirmation_retire_reject_forward_only_after_root_started`, `confirmation_retire_reject_allowed_while_root_ready`; spawnd `allocation_readback_reports_idle`, `readback_retired_reports_phase`, `journal_schema3_reads_schema1_and_2`, `retire_never_creates_entry`, `decode_rejects_retire_binding_collision`, `disable_refused_on_retiring_or_retired`, `retire_scenarios_with_in_memory_registry` (all 15 scenarios portably), root `iso_root_retire_{refuses_live_run_unit_lease_or_process, gid_match_counts_as_live (real /bin/sleep with the employee gid), resume_skips_intruder_refusal, refuses_target_mismatch, refuses_non_ready_entry, replay_same_op_idempotent, retired_replay_answered_before_host_gate, second_retire_binding_refused, crash_after_retiring_resumes, partial_destroy_resumes, frees_label_for_new_allocation, uid_never_recycled, allocate_and_readback_refused_after, audits_retired_with_requester_approver, missing_journal_with_registry_or_images_refuses_open, not_found_only_without_any_trace}`; gateway `retire_{only_when_every_row_closed, refused_with_open_request, refused_for_live_old_id, early_purged_row_needs_second_admin, reject_forward_only_after_retiring, compensation_ok_while_ready, retired_row_reads_tombstoned_without_root, not_found_records_root_absent, inconsistent_keeps_label_blocked, host_busy_24h_alerts_operator, label_free_only_after_all_r7_conditions, audited_and_notified}` (real `GatewayAuthority` card path). **Deviations from §3.12**: approval audit is emitted at the apply `stage` step (the approval validator also runs for card listings, so it cannot audit), not inside the approve call; a purged row without a seal digest carries the prefix `0000000000000000`; an apply-time `NotFound`/`InvalidRequest` for a staged retire is treated as `ROOT_INCONSISTENT` (alert, stays `retiring`), never `root_absent`; the "last bytes/inodes" in `Event::Retired` are a best-effort usage read before `Retiring` (`None` on a resumed retire), plus the quota. **Deferred / open**: (1) F6d wires `EmployeeRetireAuthority` into `migration_confirmations_rpc` (the backend slot holds one `Arc<dyn MigrationAuthorityBackend>`; a composite with `EmployeeAuthority` is needed) and adds `isolation.employee_retire_propose` (+ `retire` in `isolation.quarantine_purge`), the `RETIRE_*` messages and a production `RetireNotifier`; (2) F6c2 supplies the production `ApprovalRule`; F6h the production `LabelStores`; (3) the gateway-side retry with backoff for `HostBusy` (the store leaves the item `Failed`; something must call `retry` + `apply_next`) and boot recovery for `root_state = retiring` (F6d/boot); (4) **journal compaction for Retired entries** (§7 N4): the 4096-entry journal remains the per-host lifetime cap; (5) S14 residual: SysV IPC and `/tmp`, `/dev/shm` files of a retired UID are not swept; (6) root-only checks need a real host: `iso_root_retire_*` on H15 hosts, `recover_locked` of a retired account with live processes, `LoopStorage::traces`/`destroy` under a real image, and a spawnd restart with a `Retiring` entry and a `Destroying` storage phase; (7) `[SEC]` Daybreak review of F6l before merge.

ReadBack/Retire keep their `absence()` re-check. Item 10 (`Disable` is host-gated like `Allocate`, so compensation waits while any run is live): left fail closed and documented at the gate (`allocation.rs`); scoping it to the entry's UID needs a per-employee busy readback from the host (`host_busy` is a host-wide bool) — not done. Test (not compiled or run here): spawnd `f6fb_empty_open_with_unreadable_storage_refuses_allocate_until_traces_read`.
- From H11: protected file tools over the brokered root (`crates/duduclaw-cli/src/mcp_files.rs`, `mcp/dispatch.rs`). **Open:** both protected paths now call `ResourceRoots::authorize_file_with_refusal` (H5/H6), so a call whose `workspace` is `employee-work` goes through root's broker, and the run/employee come only from the verified capability. No new resolution call and no boundary change: `authorize_file` already routed brokered handles. `file_read` gains an async entry `handle_isolated_file_read`, which `mcp::dispatch` uses whenever an isolated call is current. It runs the open AND the bounded read on the blocking pool (a broker round trip can take up to H5's 12 s). The sync `handle_file_read` keeps its inline isolated branch (same shared checks: `isolated_file_read_arguments` → open → `finish_isolated_file_read`). `handle_isolated_table_read` resolves its input on the blocking pool too (`authorize_isolated_input`). **Read:** only the returned `AuthorizedFile` is read (for `employee-work` that is root's sealed read-only memfd, nlink 0). It is never reopened by path, and live authority is rechecked on every `read`. `file_read` stays capped at `max_bytes` ≤ 512 KiB and computes `truncated` from the snapshot's length. csv/xlsx keep `table_request`'s 64/32 MiB caps; brokered inputs are ≤ 16 MiB anyway. **Parsing:** the bytes go to the installed `FileParser`: boundary `GatewayTableParser` → gateway `isolation_execution_worker::invoke_table`, which already rewrites the request to `ParseOperation::ReadTable` + `ParseOptions::Table` and streams through `LimitedInput` into E's own snapshot. ReadTable already existed in `duduclaw-isolation::worker` and the parse worker, so the parse worker was NOT changed. I checked the whole chain for an nlink-1 assumption on the input (`table_request` = `is_file` + size; `AuthorizedFile::from_opened` = `is_file` + dev/ino; `GatewayTableParser`; `LimitedInput`; E's client copies from a reader) and found none. **Refusals:** a root refusal shows `WorkBrokerError::user_message()` (fixed zh-TW, no path). Any boundary refusal (bad path, other workspace, missing grant, run not live, an unsealed or misreported descriptor) keeps the tool's generic text (`無法讀取這次執行授權範圍內的文字檔。` / `無法解析這次執行授權範圍內的資料檔。`), so it cannot serve as an oracle. A refused protected open now writes a `tool_calls.jsonl` row with `refused: true` and `reason` = `WorkBrokerError::code()` or `isolation_resource`, and never the requested or a host path. Argument-shape refusals before the open still write no row (unchanged). Success rows keep the employee-relative `path` + `table` as before. **Unchanged outside protected mode:** the legacy `vet_path` fence, the canonical host path in payloads, the in-process csv/calamine readers and the `client::required()` worker branch were not touched. `RM-103 S1` (code_map containment) and `RM-155` (`run_guarded`) do not exist in this tree (no match for either name anywhere in the repo), so there was nothing to keep. Matrix rows for the three tools are updated (`docs/architecture/native-linux-isolation-tools.md`; still no `Refused:`, so `check_isolation_tool_matrix.py` is unaffected). **Parity:** `pending_production_selectors` deliberately still lists `file_read`/`csv_read`/`xlsx_read`. Nothing issues `Workspace{employee-work}` until H9/H10 install a production broker (H9 note), and H14 removes them. `duduclaw-cli` dev-dependencies now enable `duduclaw-mcp-boundary/test-support` for `TestWorkBroker`. Tests (`crates/duduclaw-cli/src/mcp_files_h11_tests.rs`, a child of `mcp_isolation_tests`; the first four are Linux-only; not compiled or run here — Linux builder): h11_file_read_uses_brokered_snapshot (bounded read, relative path only, capability run/employee, sync path identical, unsealed live inode from a faulty broker refused), h11_csv_read_parses_via_worker and h11_xlsx_read_parses_via_worker (real `GatewayTableParser` over a stand-in for the gateway ReadTable call: exactly one worker call, received descriptor nlink 0 with the file's bytes and declared size, `sheet` option passed, legacy in-process readers refuse inside isolation), h11_refusal_messages_no_path_leak (NotFound/Refused/TooLarge/Unavailable/NotLive ⇒ exact `user_message`, no path fragment/host path/`employee-work` in results or audit, boundary refusals generic and never reach root, nothing reaches the worker), h11_unprotected_behaviour_unchanged (legacy payloads/messages/audit; the protected entry points refuse without an isolated call). Open: (1) the CLI tests replace `invoke_table` with a stand-in: installing a real launcher would flip `client::required()` for the whole CLI test binary. The real ReadTable worker over a brokered memfd is therefore proven only by composition. H15/G2 should run csv/xlsx on `employee-work` end to end. (2) `max_bytes` for brokered opens is always 16 MiB (`WorkFileRequest` is built by the boundary). `file_read` still copies ≤ 16 MiB into root's snapshot to return ≤ 512 KiB. A per-tool request limit would need a boundary API change (owner H6). (3) A `file_read` reads in several `read` calls, each doing a live check (unchanged; now on the blocking pool). (4) H5 open item 1 (the 12 s work op vs the 250 ms liveness gate) still applies to every brokered file call.
- From H12a: protected `skill_extract`, `shared_skill_adopt`, `skill_security_scan`, `skill_graduate` and `skill_curator_status` (incl. `run=true`). **Policy (`tool_policy.rs`):** required kinds drop `Workspace` (skill files are host-owned `agents/<label>/SKILLS`, not workspace inputs): `skill_extract` = `[Employee, Wiki]`, the other four `[Employee]`. New `pub const OWN_SKILL_TOOLS`, `pub fn check_own_skill_arguments(name, args)` (exact protected schema: curator `{run: bool}`; adopt `{skill_name}`; scan/graduate/extract `{skill_name, agent_id?}`; slugs ≤ 64 `[A-Za-z0-9_-]`; `target_agent`, `scope`, `namespace`, paths and every identity key refuse) and `pub fn own_skill_selector(context, name)` (record must carry EmployeeScope AND its own `Employee{record.employee}`; `skill_extract` additionally exactly one valid `Wiki{agent:<non-empty>}` io grant). `eligible` ANDs the selector for these tools (task-limited records without EmployeeScope never list them — behaviour decision; owner may relax); `authorize` uses the exact-schema check instead of the old `agent_id` strip and, after the resolver (which still does the live identity check and picks the own namespace), requires the resolved set to equal the selector (no workspace, recipient, second namespace or foreign employee). The curator stays out of `employee_service_scope`, so it is authorized through the resolver with exactly `[Employee]`. `resources.rs` `HostSnapshotSource::Skill` now binds the snapshot to the call's own `Employee` grant (was: the single Workspace grant) after re-running `own_skill_selector` and refusing any non-Employee/Wiki resource. **Identity:** the producer subject is the boot-bound `(agent, employee)` of `GatewayWorkerPlans::for_employee`, checked against `record.employee`; a legacy `agent_id` must equal it, `target_agent` is refused at the boundary and in the producer (was: allowed if equal). The CLI `check_tool_arguments` delegates to `check_own_skill_arguments`, and `project_tool_schema` removes `agent_id`/`target_agent` (properties and `required`) from the protected schema of these tools (no `target_employee` rewrite). **Content as data:** unchanged E ops (`ScanSkill`/`PrepareSkill`/`ExtractSkill`), 64 KiB host read cap, CONTRACT `must_not` strictly parsed; the scan reply is rendered by new `scan_report` with every finding flattened to one line (controls → spaces) so worker text cannot forge `Passed:`/heading lines. **Publication:** `skill_extract` → `publish_extraction` (own `agent:<label>` grant + `verify_wiki_root`, body pages read back before the `sources/skill-<name>.md` completion marker; unchanged); adopt → `write_host_file` into own `agents/<label>/SKILLS` (no-clobber + readback; unchanged); **`skill_graduate` now first files/consumes the dashboard admin card** (`isolated::approve`, kind `isolated_skill_publication`, action `graduate`, target `<agent>:<skill>`, bound to `{agent, skill, source, source_sha256}` — not to the prepared bytes, whose `graduated_at` changes per run), then the journaled atomic global publication (`isolation_skill_publication.rs`, unchanged). Global scope is loaded by every employee, so it is not own-namespace publication; owner may decide to refuse graduation instead. A pending card is returned as an MCP tool error with the card text. **Curator (D-H4):** `curator::run_owned_pass(home, store, cfg, now, agent)` + `curator::owned_scope(agent)`; `curator_protected.rs` is now `run_scoped(.., owner)`: owned discovery opens only `agents/<agent>/SKILLS` (`collect`), rows filtered to `agent:<agent>` (`owned_records`), the bound registry usage limited to the pass's own names (`owned_usage`), `require_owned` before every mutation, approval filed by the employee with target `skill-curator:agent:<id>` and the owner in the bound state, totals over own rows only, and the report returned to the caller, not published to `shared/wiki` (no own-wiki root is available there). The scheduled system pass (`run_pass`/`maybe_run`) is unchanged. CLI: the dispatch arm uses `handle_skill_curator_status_owned(args, home, current.agent)` whenever an isolated call is in scope (agent from the capability-bound dispatcher, never arguments); the status list is filtered to the own scope. **For H9 (selectors.rs not edited):** no new selector is needed — the issuer already emits `Employee{own}` + `EmployeeScope` + exactly one Confined `Wiki{agent:<label>}` (when the wiki exists); H9 must not add `Workspace`/`Recipient` grants for these tools and must keep exactly one own `agent:` wiki grant (a second `agent:` namespace makes `skill_extract` ineligible); tool names come from `ingress_tools` as before. **For H14:** the five tools are out of `pending_production_selectors`; the parity test asserts they are eligible and listed for the production fixture. Open: (1) curator `run=true` is not gated by `can_modify_own_skills` (pre-existing in non-protected mode too); (2) the registry usage signal is keyed by slug, so a same-named skill in another scope still shares its `last_used_at` (pre-existing data model; only the binding/display is narrowed); (3) `skill_security_scan` keeps the global fallback (`skills/<name>.md`, read-only scan of company-wide skills); (4) the owned curator report is not persisted anywhere; if wanted, publish it into the caller's own wiki through `ResourceRoots::verify_wiki_root` from the CLI side; (5) H12b/c/d tools (`skill_synthesis_run`, `skill_hub_install`, `skill_from_recording`) untouched. Generic worker/resource fixtures in `worker_execution_tests.rs`/`resources_tests.rs` now use `model_load` (a workspace-selecting Worker route) instead of `skill_security_scan`. Tests added (not compiled or run here; Linux builder): mcp-boundary `tool_policy_tests::{h12a_identity_never_from_args, h12a_skill_extract_requires_exactly_one_own_namespace_wiki}` (+ updated `i1c_curator_run_flag_is_data_and_keeps_finite_grants`, `i1c_source_proposals_…`, `source_and_skill_producers_…`); gateway `skill_lifecycle::curator::protected::h12a_tests::{h12a_curator_run_own_skills_only, h12a_curator_refuses_other_employee_skill}` (Linux), `isolation_worker_plans::tests::{h12a_skill_extract_publishes_to_own_wiki_atomically, h12a_shared_skill_adopt_copies_into_own_namespace, h12a_security_scan_treats_content_as_data}` (Linux) (+ updated `i1c_skill_scan_is_bound_to_boot_employee_and_rejects_cross_agent`, `isolation_issuer_real_output_preserves_service_and_connector_scope`); CLI `mcp_isolation_tests::h12a_worker_skill_schema_offers_no_subject_selector` (replaces `i1c_worker_skill_schema_keeps_the_subject_checked_by_the_producer`) and the parity inventory. The full owned curator pass (worker identity inspection + approval broker) has no unit test; H15 should cover `run=true` end to end (approve, archive own stale skill, other employee's rows untouched).
- From H9: issuer selectors in new `crates/duduclaw-gateway/src/isolation_mcp/selectors.rs` (Linux, private module of `isolation_mcp`). `select(&SelectorSources, &SelectorInput{home, agent, tools, task, record}) -> Selection{workspace, public_web, task: Option<SelectedTask{id, owned}>}`; `Selection::apply` pushes the plain (not `Confined`) grants and registers the brokered root. **Workspace** `{employee-work}`: only when `SelectorSources.work_broker` is installed, the run's tool snapshot lists `file_read`/`csv_read`/`xlsx_read`, and the employee's current `agent.toml [capabilities]` still allows at least one listed file tool through the same `tool_catalog::tool_list_verdict` the ingress catalog uses; then `ResourceRoots::insert_brokered("employee-work", employee, broker)` (H6), never a dirfd. **PublicOrigins** `{*}`: only when no task is selected (D-H2 requires EmployeeScope), the snapshot lists `web_fetch_cached`/`web_extract`, the profile still allows one of them, and `agent.toml [container] network_access = true`. Decision: there is no dedicated per-employee "public web" key, so the existing per-agent network switch is reused — `[container]` is in `org_field_guard::AGENT_SECURITY_SECTIONS` (frozen against the employee's own Write/Edit; changed only via dashboard `agents.update` / MCP `agent_update`), it defaults to `false` (= no web authority), and only an exact `true` (typed `AgentConfig` parse) grants `*`. Explicit `https://<host>` lists are not produced (no store holds them). **Owner decision needed:** `network_access` also gates the task sandbox's network (task_sandbox doctor asks admins to turn it on so sandboxed CLIs reach their model provider), so enabling it now also grants protected public web; if that coupling is unwanted, add a dedicated admin-only key and change only `selectors::select` (one line). **Task**: only for a record-backed run (`HostTurn.background`) that names a task (an interactive run naming one is `Invalid`); `owned` = `tasks.db` `SELECT 1 FROM tasks WHERE id=?1 AND assigned_to=?2` + `review_evidence::audience::agent_may_read_task` (same rule as the H8 resolver's `task_owned`, duplicated rather than shared because that one is a private method on the resolver). **Inputs / fail closed:** the profile is read through new `ingress::own_agent_dir` (the no-follow walk from `/` that `own_wiki_root` now also uses), then `openat(agent.toml, O_RDONLY|O_NOFOLLOW|O_CLOEXEC|O_NOCTTY|O_NONBLOCK)`, regular file, nlink 1, owner = euid, ≤ 1 MiB, typed `AgentConfig` parse; the task store only through `fs_safe::sqlite::open_existing(home, "tasks.db", ReadOnly)` (never created). Missing/unsafe/unreadable/malformed ⇒ the whole issuance `Unavailable`; an absent setting only withholds its grant. Selectors that a run does not need (no matching tool, no broker, no task) perform no I/O, so runs without file/web tools never read the profile and interactive runs never read `tasks.db`. Tests (`isolation_mcp/selectors_tests.rs`, child of `boot`, real stores + fixture root + non-opening broker double; not compiled or run here, Linux builder): h9_workspace_grant_only_when_profile_allows, h9_public_origins_only_when_admin_enabled, h9_task_grant_only_for_owned_record_task, h9_selector_store_unreadable_fails_closed. No dashboard control (there is no protected-mode settings area); documented as config-only in `docs/guides/dashboard-settings.md` (English page only; zh-TW/ja-JP guides not updated). (D2) A task run whose task the employee no longer owns is refused outright: `select` now refuses (`Revoked`) instead of issuing a task-limited record without the grant (`SelectedTask.owned` and `RuntimeAuthorization::limit_to_task_with` removed; the issuer calls `limit_to_task`); the shared ownership rule moved to `isolation_background::task_owned_now` (same SQL + audience rule; private-SQLite opener). `isolation_background::scope_record` pre-checks `kind == "task"` records (`task_run_precheck`) before any receipt reuse or approval card: foreign ⇒ `REFUSAL` = `FOREIGN_TASK_REFUSAL` (zh-TW), no record; unreadable ⇒ no record, no reason, no card. Every foreign refusal writes `security_audit.jsonl` `isolation_task_run_refused` {task, reason `task_not_owned`, stage `schedule`/`issuance`}. `dispatcher::pending_isolation_approval` now returns `DispatchError::Other(refusal_reason())` for any scope that holds no authority and knows why (foreign task, Personal employee, pre-v1.81 receipt), so the queue row records the reason instead of the generic launch refusal (those runs were already refused at launch). (D3) Interactive `create_task`: new grant `ResourceGrant::NewTask { requester: Text<128> }`, MAC tag 12 (`0x0c` + u32-length text; encoding version stays 2, golden bytes unchanged); `CapabilityRecord::check_new_task` (decode and sign): own EmployeeScope, no task, at most once, never Confined, principal `Human { user_id == requester }` (a Background principal can never carry it). Resolver (`isolation_resources::new_task_selector`): refuses `TASK_SELECTOR_KEYS`, re-derives the grant from the record principal, rechecks the Personal owner, and refuses (`BindingMismatch`) any step `agent`/`agent_id`/`assigned_to`/`assignee` other than empty/null/its own agent label; never opens tasks.db. - From HX5 (gateway/MCP side of in-run helper calls for `execute_program`/`code_map`/`office_script`; D8 revision §2.2/§2.3/§2.5/§3/§5, F7/F8/F9/F17, Q5 = A): **Policy**: `tool_policy::Route::InRunHelper` + `IN_RUN_HELPER_TOOLS` for the three tools (required kinds unchanged: own Employee + `Workspace{employee-work}`); CLI `boundary_route` maps it to the execution bridge; their protected schema loses the pending-job `action`/`job` controls (only `browser_record_start` keeps them). **Boundary** (`mcp-boundary/src/execution_in_run.rs`, child of `execution.rs`): `ExecutionToolBridge::execute` sends these tools to `execute_in_run` before any reserve/spawn/job code (`execution_route` returns `None` for them, so `kill(run)`/`kill_authorized(run)`/job rows are unreachable [F8]). Order: `context.transport_run() == Some(record.run)` (new `RequestContext::with_transport`, set only by `Boundary::verify` from the activated connection binding; delegated/Admin-RPC/record-only contexts have none) → `supports` (factory ∧ `backend.supports_helper_calls()`) → `authorize_async` → per-run `helper_calls` bit (missing ⇒ `helper_launcher_unavailable{reason: spawnd_feature_missing}`, nothing sent) → `authorize_handler` → `ExecutionPlanFactory::prepare_in_run` (plan run must equal the caller's run and employee) → re-authorize (same resources) + handler + `verify_live` → bit re-read → `start_helper`. The call then reads stdout/stderr (bounded) and polls `wait_helper`, racing a 250 ms `verify_live` poll (revocation/principal generation) and a deadline of effective timeout + 4 s; outcomes map to typed results (`run_ended`, `helper_timed_out`, `helper_oom_killed`, `helper_signalled`, `helper_unavailable`, `invalid_result`, `helper_busy`, `run_time_exhausted`, `helper_cgroup_unavailable`), success = strict decoder output + `effective_timeout_ms`. `InRunJob {run, call}` (no `RunHandle` accessor) is armed from `HelperStarted` until `HelperEnded`; revocation, uncertainty and dropping the future (MCP cancel, connection close, request deadline) send `HelperCancel` only. **MCP server** (`server.rs`): request deadline = `config::CLI_TOOL_TIMEOUT_MS` (305 s, was 300 s); a `notifications/cancelled` for the in-flight request id (`cancels_request`) now drops that call and keeps the connection (any other second frame still closes it); EOF mid-call drops the call as before. **Factory**: `isolation_execution_program.rs` `prepare_in_run` (re-derives the helper from the signed publication for `record.employee`, `valid_helper`, input ≤ `memfd_total_bytes`, timeout = tool bound clamped to `MAX_HELPER_TIMEOUT_MS`, output limit = record `output_total_bytes`), `decode_helper_result` (same strict decoders, stderr empty, `Exited(0)`), `collects_results` only for the recording, `prepare_with_roots`/`decode_result` refuse the three tools; `factories.rs::RootedPlans` forwards both new methods (it would otherwise hide them behind the refusing defaults). **Client** (`duduclaw-execution/src/spawnd/helper_call.rs`): `SpawndBackend` implements `supports_helper_calls`/`helper_features`/`start_helper`/`wait_helper`/`cancel_helper` (+ inherent `stop_helper` for HX6) on the run's own control connection. Features: `Entry.features` = that connection's `Reserved.features`; `connection_features(run)` = those bits ∩ the installed `FeatureSource` (which can only narrow). H5's name-based `SpawndFeatures` struct is gone: `spawnd::SpawndFeatures` is now `ipc::SpawndFeatures` (bitset) and `features_from_advertised(names)` maps wire names; work ops require a source AND the connection bit. `HelperWait`/`HelperStop`/`HelperCancel` are built ONLY in `helper_call::control_request(control, run, call, employee)` with `employee = Entry.employee` — **the HX4 security round adds `employee_id` there**. Every helper RPC runs in a detached task (`helper_rpc`), because dropping an in-flight transport transaction closes the run's control connection, which spawnd treats as the gateway leaving (that would turn an MCP cancel into a run kill). Root errors map to typed `HelperError`s (not `map_error`); `transport.rs::validate_response` pairs helper ops via `ipc::check_helper_reply` and refuses helper replies to other requests; new `Connection::helper_request` returns root `Error`s as packets; `fd_io::make_helper_io` wraps the two pipes with one shared budget; the input is a sealed memfd (`sealed_input`). **Helpers** (`helper_layout.rs`): in-run = argv0 `/helpers/<entry>`, cwd = `HOME` = `TMPDIR` = `/helper-tmp/<32 hex>`; legacy = cwd `/work`, `TMPDIR` not under `/helper-tmp`; any mix refused. `helper_input_args` now checks the layout instead of cwd `/work` (the launcher starts helpers in their scratch dir, so in-run helpers could not have started); program/office use `<root>/python3|bash|node`, `<root>/office-<skill>-<script>.py`, Office staging `<scratch>/office`, interpreter `HOME`/`TMPDIR` from the layout. **CLI timeout projection** (`config.rs`): `CLI_TOOL_TIMEOUT_MS = 305_000` (compile-time ≥ 300 s + 5 s), `RuntimeProjection.tool_timeout_ms` (in the digest ⇒ every runtime's MCP acceptance must be re-run): Codex `tool_timeout_sec = 305`, Grok `tool_timeout_sec = 305` (key inferred, verify), Claude per-server `"timeout": 305000` in `mcp.json` (inferred, verify: Claude Code documents `MCP_TOOL_TIMEOUT` env; if the key is ignored, the signed runtime record must set that env), OpenAI-compatible worker and registered generic CLIs unchanged (`None`; acceptance must show their default ≥ 305 s). **Readiness**: `isolation_readiness::reasons::Reason::{HelperLauncherUnavailable{reason}, HelperRuntimeMissing{runtime}}` + pure `helper_reasons(helper_calls, published_ids)`; NOT yet called from `report()` (needs spawnd's `EvidenceReport::helper_calls()` and the publication's runtime ids at status time — H16). **Parity**: the three tools left `pending_production_selectors` (listing doubles support `InRunHelper`, `ListingBackend` panics if a helper is started). Tests (not compiled or run here: macOS worktree, no shell; Linux builder): mcp-boundary `execution::in_run::tests::{execute_program_returns_result_synchronously, helper_target_must_equal_capability_run, transport_run_must_equal_record_run, admin_context_cannot_issue_helper_call, in_run_job_cancel_never_kills_run, revocation_mid_call_kills_helper, mcp_cancelled_maps_to_helper_cancel, run_end_mid_call_returns_run_ended, gateway_refuses_helper_without_spawnd_feature}`, `config::tests::cli_tool_timeout_projection_at_least_305s`, `server::tests::mcp_cancelled_frame_matches_only_the_in_flight_request`; execution `spawnd::helper_call::tests::{hx5_helper_client_requires_connection_feature_bit, hx5_helper_client_start_wait_cancel_on_the_run_connection, hx5_helper_client_typed_refusals_keep_control, hx5_control_requests_have_one_constructor}`, `helper_layout::tests::helper_layout_in_run_uses_helpers_root_and_private_scratch`, updated `work_file::tests::h5_bridge_requires_feature_bit`; gateway `isolation_readiness::reasons::hx5_tests::gateway_helper_readiness_names_missing_feature_and_runtimes`, updated `program_plans_validate_code_map_and_typed_results_before_publication`; CLI updated schema tests and parity inventory; legacy `execution_tests.rs` now drives the child-run path with `codrive_run`. **Open for HX6/H14/H16**: (1) the H9 `Workspace{employee-work}` selector fires only when a file tool is listed and a work broker is installed; the in-run tools need the grant but not the broker, so an employee without file tools never sees them — extend `selectors::select` (owner H9/H14); (2) HX3b binds only each helper's own signed entry/script under `/helpers`: the program helper's `python3`/`bash`/`node` and the Office helper's `python3` + `office-*.py` must be signed helper-table files (HX2 publication), else in-run helpers fail `verify_runtime_file` (fail closed); (3) wire `helper_reasons` into `report()`; (4) the 305 s server deadline includes pre-start authorization and spawnd's ≤ 3 s queue, so a call that uses the full 300 s plus slack can still hit it (spawnd clamps by run remaining time; consider deadline = 305 s from `HelperStarted`); (5) recording (`browser_record_start/stop`) still uses the child-run/job path (HX6).
- Merge H9/H10 into the W12 integration branch (with F1, F6a/F6b, H11, H12a): `BootSources` carries both `work_broker` (H9) and F1's `connector_stack`/`connector_inputs`; `BootOwner` both `connector_stack` and `selectors`. The issuer order is unchanged: B1/B2 gates → `select` → wiki walk → `apply` → `lifecycle::recheck_with` (employee id AND F6b incarnation) → users.db recheck → `select` re-run; `record_authorization` (the only production caller) then re-runs the policy, lifecycle (incarnation) and users.db checks, so the second selector read is also covered by an incarnation recheck. Selectors add Workspace only for the three file tools (H11 expects exactly `Workspace{employee-work}` with the brokered root) and never Workspace/Recipient for the H12a own-skill tools; H12a's `authorize` still requires the resolved set to equal `own_skill_selector`, so a Workspace grant present for file tools is never part of a skill call. `tool_policy::eligible`: H9's early `create_task` return replaces H12a's redundant `create_task_selector` conjunct; the H12a `own_skill_selector` conjunct applies to the whole disjunction. Parity: `pending_production_selectors` loses both the H9 tools (file/web/`create_task`) and the H12a skill tools; `authorization_tests` keeps `skill_extract` eligible (H12a) and `file_read`/`web_extract` hidden (no broker / `public_web` there). - From HX4 (spawnd side of helper calls; D8 revision §2.2/§2.5/§3, F4/F5/F7/F8/F14/F17, Q4 = A; merged with H4): **Merge**: `ipc.rs` keeps both op sets (`Opcode::ALL` 16; `WORK` = H4's four, new `HELPER` = HX's four; H4 `StopEmployeeRuns`/`EmployeeIdle` + HX3a helper variants, replies and error codes side by side; `every_request` covers all 16 so `h1_opcode_space_reserved_for_hx` holds); `server.rs` keeps H4's deferred-ticket dispatch and adds the helper arms. **One feature set**: H4's readiness struct `host_state::SpawndFeatures { work_broker: bool }` is replaced by the HX3a bitset `ipc::SpawndFeatures` (u32, JSON number, omitted when empty, unknown bits ignored): `helper_calls` = bit 0, `work_broker` = bit 1 (`WORK_BROKER`), `Opcode::feature()`/`SpawndFeatures::allows(op)` are the gateway gate. The value comes from one place (`Host::features`, default = `WORK_BROKER` ∪ `HELPER_CALLS` iff `Host::helpers_ready`) and is advertised the same way on both channels: `EvidenceReport.features` (`Core::refresh` sets `report.features = self.features()`) and every `SpawnResponse::Reserved.features` (`LinuxHost::features` returns the published report's value, so the two can never differ). **Wire change vs H4**: `readiness.json` `features` is now a number (`2`/`3`), not `{"work_broker": true}`; `EvidenceReport::{work_broker, helper_calls}()` are the readers (H5/H16/HX5 must use them, nothing consumed the H4 shape yet). `helper_calls` is set iff `runner::LAUNCHER_SUPPORTED` ∧ the publication has ≥ 1 launcher with helpers (`RootPolicy::launchers_active`) ∧ `helper_cgroup::linux::open_time_credentials_observed() != Some(false)` ∧ not poisoned; a failed probe clears it and calls answer `helper_cgroup_unavailable`. Not-yet-probed (`None`) counts as available because the probe needs a helper-enabled run's cgroup; that run's own prepare failure is per-run `helper_cgroup_unavailable`. **Coordinator** (`spawnd/src/admission_helpers.rs`, child module of `admission`, pure): `helper_call(connection, request, memfd, random_call, now) -> Ticket` checks `owned` (`not_found`), `entry.employee == employee_id` (`unauthorized`, F7), admission `Running` + no pending employee stop + runtime not exited (`not_ready`, F4), run remaining ≥ 5 s (`run_time_exhausted`), `helpers_ready`, run-wide closed code, budgets (queued + starting memfds count against `pending_input_bytes`/`input_descriptors` with StartRun inputs, and `start()` now counts helper inputs too), fresh non-zero call id (server CSPRNG, deduplicated per run, ≤ 65 536). Slots: one fg + one rec; a busy slot queues in a per-run FIFO of ≤ `MAX_QUEUED_CALLS` 4 (both slots together), each ≤ `HELPER_QUEUE_MS` 3 s, then `helper_busy` (the 5th waiting call is refused at once); a free slot never queues. Drops (memfds closed, tickets answered): run leaves Running (`drain` → `end_helpers`: queue `not_ready`, live calls `RunEnded`), host `Draining` (runtime exit), host `Closed` (launcher lost / slot cgroups failed: that code for the queue and all later calls), `HelperCancel` of a queued call, server abandon (deadline/disconnect). `HelperCall` is a deferred reply (`Pending::Helper`, `poll_helper_ticket` → `HelperStarted` + pipes); `HelperWait` is a bounded poll (`not_ready` → `HelperEnded` exactly once; ≤ 16 unwaited ended records per run, oldest dropped); `HelperStop` (recording only, else `invalid_request`) and `HelperCancel` call only `Host::helper_signal` (F8: no code path to `drain`/revoke/kill); a stop/cancel racing the host start is applied when it lands; a start whose reply nobody receives is cancelled and its pipes dropped (`poll_orphans`). The host's `effective_timeout_ms` must be ≤ the request (else the call is cancelled, `internal`). **Root engine** (`spawnd/src/helper_host.rs`, Linux, one `HelperEngine` per Running run in `LiveRun.helper`, driven from `Core::maintenance` on the lifecycle thread): `Trampoline::take_helper_link` (new trait method, default `helper_unavailable`; `NativeTrampoline` moves the control socket end, the `UnitTree` and the run table `Vec<LinkedHelper{index, slot, limits, timeout_ms}>` out once; HX2's `helper_channel` removed) when the run first polls Running. `Core::helper_run`: root `LiveRun.employee` must equal the job's (F7 again), run not closed/fenced, helper re-derived per call with `derive_helper(entry.employee, parent, kind)` (`runtime_denied`), slot = binding slot, run remaining = min(coordinator, root `runtime_deadline`). `HelperEngine::start`: memfd revalidated (`fds::validate_sealed`, `invalid_fds`), `effective_timeout_ms(tool, binding, remaining, termination_grace)` (budget = remaining − grace − 5 s, < 5 s ⇒ `run_time_exhausted`), slot `populated 0` else `helper_busy`, `write_slot_limits(binding)` + readback, `oom_kill` baseline, two CLOEXEC pipes, `SpawndChannel::start`, `helper_wire::linux::send` with `[memfd, stdout w, stderr w]`, spawnd's copies closed. `service(now)`: ≤ 16 frames/turn via `linux::recv(max_fds 0, nonblocking)` (fds dropped at once; any `receive` error or EOF ⇒ close: `SpawndChannel::close`, `shutdown(SHUT_RDWR)`, both slots killed, `Closed{helper_unavailable}`); watchdog at effective + `WATCHDOG_GRACE_MS` 2 s ⇒ kill hint + root `cgroup.kill`; `rt-runtime/cgroup.events` populated → 0 ⇒ `Draining`. `finish` (every call, exactly once): `kill_and_wait` (100 × 10 ms), `oom_kill` delta ⇒ `OomKilled` (wins over any frame), else launcher end / `TimedOut` (watchdog) / `Signalled(9)` (cancel, channel lost after Started) / `StartFailed`, `abandon` when ended root-side, slot closed again (`SlotLimits::CLOSED`); a drain/readback failure closes the run's helpers with `helper_cgroup_unavailable`. `cleanup_run` shuts the engine down (live ⇒ `RunEnded`, no Closed event) before `broker.release()` and the trampoline close. **WorkerHost**: `Key::Helper(ticket)`/`Work::Helper` for starts, fire-and-forget `Key::HelperSignal`, and an mpsc of `HelperEvent`s drained by the lifecycle loop after `maintenance` and after every job; `LinuxHost` delegates. **fds**: `validate_start` checks the HelperCall memfd (sealed tmpfs, exact size). **Audit**: `helper_started`/`helper_ended` (`Detail::Helper{call, helper index, slot, input_bytes, effective_timeout_ms|outcome}`), `helper_refused` (HelperCall error code; `HelperWait` `not_ready` polls are not audited), `helper_channel_closed`. Tests added (not compiled or run here: macOS worktree, no shell; Linux builder): spawnd `admission_helpers_tests::{helper_call_never_occupies_d8_slot, helper_call_other_connection_refused, helper_call_wrong_employee_refused, helper_call_on_draining_run_refused, queued_call_counts_against_input_budgets, helper_busy_after_queue_bound, queue_dropped_on_run_end_revocation_cancel, helper_queue_depth_4_and_drop_rules, in_run_job_cancel_never_kills_run, deadline_refuses_below_five_seconds, hx4_call_ids_and_feature_bit}`; `helper_host_tests::{helper_start_writes_signed_limits_and_sends_exact_fds, memfd_seals_revalidated, control_channel_closed_on_cmsg_or_truncation, watchdog_kills_slot_with_stopped_launcher, oom_reported_from_memory_events_not_frame, fg_kill_leaves_rec_running, runtime_exit_reports_draining_and_refuses_start}` (Linux, unprivileged: fake `CgroupTree` + real SEQPACKET socketpair as the launcher); `fds::tests::helper_call_input_must_be_one_sealed_memfd_of_the_stated_size`; `audit::tests::hx4_helper_events_carry_ids_sizes_and_outcome_never_content`; `host_state::tests::h4_feature_bits_advertised` (rewritten for the bitset); isolation `ipc::tests::hx4_one_feature_set_gates_work_and_helper_ops`. **Not done / open**: (1) the HX4 task text also lists the client `duduclaw-execution/src/spawnd.rs::run_helper` — not implemented here (spawnd side only); `transport.rs::validate_response`/`validate_fds` still refuse the helper pairs (use `check_helper_reply`) and its `map_error` stays HX3a's; (2) root tests `iso_root_helper_{watchdog_kills_slot_with_stopped_launcher, oom_reported_from_memory_events_not_frame, fg_kill_leaves_rec_running, escaped_daemon_killed_after_call}` are not written (need a real launcher run on H15 hosts; unprivileged halves exist); (3) `HelperWait` is a poll, not a held reply — HX5 polls it after the pipes reach EOF; (4) MCP cancel of a still-*queued* call: the gateway learns the call id only from `HelperStarted`, so it waits ≤ 3 s for the reply and then sends `HelperCancel` (or gets `helper_busy`); a queued call is also dropped when the gateway disconnects; (5) capability revocation is gateway-side for helpers (HX5 sends `HelperCancel`/`HelperStop`); spawnd's own revocation path is the run drain; (6) `HelperCancel` kills the slot synchronously on the lifecycle thread (`kill_and_wait` ≤ 1 s), like other lifecycle work; (7) the slot-cgroup probe still runs on the first helper-enabled run, so `helper_calls` may be advertised before it is known; (8) `kill_and_wait` of a slot after the unit stopped fails quietly at shutdown (unit stop already killed it); (9) root `HelperBusy` when a slot is still populated at Start (stale process) is not retried by the coordinator; (10) [SEC] Daybreak review HX3+HX4 returned conditional GO, see the security fix round below. **Security fix round (2026-11-09, 8 findings, tests `hxfix_*`)**: (1) `LinkedHelper` carries the run-start `RuntimeKind`, `ProfileId` and `helper_policy::binding_digest` (sha256 of the signed binding); `CallPlan` carries the freshly derived ones and `HelperEngine::start` refuses `runtime_denied` unless equal, so a policy reload that withdraws/reorders helpers cannot reuse an index; (2) offline helpers: publication refuses an F_H listing `socket`/`socketpair`/`connect`/`bind`/`listen`/`accept`/`accept4`, and `seccomp::helper(…, egress)` denies them with EPERM when `!egress`; (3) publication refuses, and F_H denies, path-based `truncate`, `chmod`/`fchmodat`/`fchmodat2`, `utime`/`utimes`/`utimensat`/`futimesat`, `setxattr`/`lsetxattr`/`removexattr`/`lremovexattr`/`setxattrat`/`removexattrat` (fd forms kept; HX doc T19 updated); (4) same for SysV shm/sem/msg and POSIX mq, and the engine revokes the run's MCP connection on `Draining` (failure fences the run); (5) a slot not confirmed `populated 0` (or with unreadable `memory.events`) ends `StartFailed`, never the launcher's outcome, and closes the run's helpers `helper_cgroup_unavailable`; (6) a per-call signal refused by the worker mailbox is held per call and retried every `tick_helpers` until delivered or the host reports the call ended (stop/cancel then answer `Ack`); (7) audit: refusals of all four helper ops are attributed to the run's admitted employee with the request's value as `Detail::HelperRefusal.claimed_employee`, `helper_ended` carries `reason` (`launcher_timeout` vs `watchdog_kill`, `start_failed_{descriptors,sandbox,exec,…}`) and `drained`, `helper_channel_closed` carries `Detail::HelperClosed{code, reason}` (wire error / eof / cgroup), and accepted Stop/Cancel emit `helper_stop_requested`/`helper_cancel_requested`; (8) **wire change**: `HelperWait`/`HelperStop`/`HelperCancel` gain `employee_id` (checked against `entry.employee`, `unauthorized`) — HX5's client must send it. `host.rs` was split (`host_monitor.rs`, `host_helpers.rs`, `host_tests.rs`) to stay below 800 lines. Not compiled or run here (no shell).
- From W1 ([SEC] production boot wiring of the work broker and the spawnd feature source; closes the H5/H9/H10/H11/HX5 "tools stay hidden in production" gaps): **Feature source**: `isolation_readiness::connect_backend` now builds the protected backend as `SpawndBackend::connect_with_admission(..).with_feature_source(isolation_mcp::readiness_feature_source())`; the source = spawnd's own `EvidenceReport::read(egid)` + `check()` → `report.features` (unreadable/stale ⇒ error ⇒ no features), never config. It only narrows: `connection_features(run)` stays `Reserved.features ∩ source`, so each run's own admission decides its work/helper ops. New execution accessors (`spawnd/work_file.rs`): `SpawndBackend::has_feature_source()`, `run_transport(run) -> Option<RunTransport{employee, control, features}>` (no root I/O; `None` for an unknown run or closed connection). **Run-bound brokers** (new `isolation_mcp/run_broker.rs`, Linux): `assemble` calls `SelectorSources::installed(home, sources.work_broker.take(), backend)`; without a host broker it builds the factory `RunBrokers` over `SpawndRunTransport` (backend + H5 `SpawndWorkspaceBridge`); no feature source on the backend or a relative home ⇒ no factory (bridge cannot be built). The factory is not a broker: `Selection::apply` (now takes the pinned `EmployeeLifecycle`) mints a fresh `RunBoundBroker` per capability via `RunBrokers::bind(label, employee, incarnation)` and inserts only that into the authorization's `ResourceRoots`. Every open: request employee == bound employee; F6m `protected_employee(home, label)` must be `Live` with exactly (employee, incarnation) (tombstone/restore/unreadable ⇒ `NotLive`); `run_transport(request.run)` must exist with the same employee; that run's admission bits must contain `work_broker` (else `Unavailable` = `workspace_broker_unavailable`, nothing sent); the broker latches `(run, control connection id)` on its first admitted open (`OnceLock`) and refuses any other run, or the same run handle on another connection, afterwards; only then H5's bridge sends `OpenWorkFile` on that run's own connection, pinned to the latched control id (`SpawndBackend::open_work_file_on(run, Some(control), ..)` refuses `NotLive` before sending if the run's entry is now on another connection). `BootSources.work_broker` / `SelectorSources.work_broker` and the `latched` wrapper exist only under `cfg(any(test, feature = "test-support"))`: that wrapper is a test double with weaker checks (employee + first-run latch only; no incarnation pin, no control-connection latch, no per-run `work_broker` bit, latch set before the inner broker accepts), so a production build always uses `RunBoundBroker`. Selectors: "broker installed" = the factory while the readiness view advertises `work_broker` now (or a test host double; `broker_available`, used by the file-tool and HX5 helper selectors), evaluated only after a file/helper tool is found in the snapshot (no readiness read otherwise, H9); the HX5 helper view is the same readiness source. **Readiness** (`isolation_readiness/reasons.rs`): new `Reason::WorkspaceBrokerUnavailable` (`workspace_broker_unavailable`); pure `feature_reasons(features, broker_installed, published)` = `workspace_broker_unavailable` when the view lacks `work_broker` (or is unreadable) or boot installed no broker, plus HX5's `helper_reasons(helper_calls, published ids)`; `installed_feature_reasons()` reads the live feature source, `run_broker::work_broker_installed()` (set when `SelectorSources::installed` installed a producer) and the fresh `GatewayPublication`'s `Registered` runtime ids (unreadable ⇒ none published); `report()` adds them to the global reasons only (not per employee), and only those some protected employee's configured tool list needs (`reasons::ToolNeeds` over each employee's `agent.toml [capabilities]` via the selectors' no-follow `read_profile` and `tool_list_verdict`; an explicit non-empty `allowed_tools` must name the tool: file tools ⇒ `workspace_broker_unavailable`, `execute_program`/`code_map`/`office_script` ⇒ `workspace_broker_unavailable` + `helper_launcher_unavailable` + their own `helper_runtime_missing{runtime}`, `browser_record_*` ⇒ `helper_launcher_unavailable`; an unreadable profile counts as needing all). The rest go to a new informational `notices` array that never makes `status` `action_required` and that the banner does not show. `report()` is still `None` outside protected mode, so unprotected installs are untouched. Dashboard (`IsolationReadinessBanner.tsx`, `i18n/{zh-TW,en,ja-JP}.json`): plain-language texts for `workspace_broker_unavailable`, `helper_launcher_unavailable`, `helper_runtime_missing` (per known runtime: program / code analysis / Office, else generic) and `helper_recording_unsupported` (UI only; no Rust reason yet, HX6), next step = security status page. Tests (not compiled or run here; no shell): gateway `isolation_mcp::run_broker::tests::{w1_production_boot_installs_run_bound_work_broker, w1_feature_source_comes_from_run_admission, w1_missing_feature_bit_keeps_tools_hidden_and_reports_reason, w1_broker_never_shared_across_runs, w1_open_refused_when_control_connection_changed, w1_selector_without_file_or_helper_tools_does_no_readiness_io, w1_readiness_reports_helper_runtime_missing, w1_unprotected_mode_untouched}` (transport double for the backend; real issuer, profile and apply.db incarnation reader), gateway `isolation_readiness::tests::w1_helper_reasons_informational_when_no_employee_uses_helper_tools`, execution `spawnd::work_file::tests::{w1_feature_source_comes_from_run_admission, w1_open_refused_when_control_connection_changed}`, web `IsolationReadinessBanner.test.tsx` (new reason texts, notices alone hide the banner); gateway dev-dependencies enable `duduclaw-mcp-boundary/test-support` (`WorkFileRequest::for_test`). Open: (1) the run latch is set at first open, not at reservation (the boundary's `ProtectedMcpLaunch::prepare` is the only place that knows the run at issue time; binding there needs a boundary hook); the MAC-verified record run is the only run that can reach a capability's broker, so this is defence in depth; (2) closed (review follow-up): helper/broker reasons are gated on employee tool lists (see Readiness); an employee with the default empty allowlist counts as not configured with these tools; (3) H5 open item 1 (12 s work op vs 250 ms liveness gate) unchanged; (4) closed: dashboard texts added; `notices` are not rendered anywhere yet; (5) a real `SpawndBackend` end-to-end (root socket) is H15/G2.
- From H13: production selectors for `spawn_agent` / `spawn_ephemeral` (Package H tools 20–21). **No new capability grant and no issuer change**: both tools are host-queue work of the own `Employee` grant (the production issuer already emits it), template = H8/H9 `create_task`. **Boundary** (`mcp-boundary/src/tool_policy_spawn.rs`, `pub mod tool_policy::spawn`, re-exported `SPAWN_TOOLS`, `check_spawn_arguments`, `spawn_selector`, `ephemeral_tools`): policies `required: [Employee]` (was `[Employee, Workspace]`, never issued); `eligible` = `spawn_selector` = own `Employee` grant + (task/schedule run) the exact signed `Task{record.task}` grant + (spawn_ephemeral) `ephemeral_parent_allowed` (Shared, or Personal with a `Human` principal that `personal_owner_matches` — D-H3 owner-only; a Background or other-human principal is `BindingMismatch`). `authorize` replaces the generic `check_arguments` (which refuses `agent_id`) with exact schemas (`agent_id` label/`task` ≤ 100 000 B/`session_key` ≤ 256; `instruction`/`context`/`tools` 1..=64 exact names, no `*`/controls/`tier` ∈ cheap|standard|preferred; every other key — `sender_agent`, `origin_agent`, `delegation_depth`, `hop_depth`, `turn_id`, `on_behalf_of`, identity keys — refuses) and requires the resolver to return exactly the selection. `ephemeral_tools` = requested (`name` or `mcp__duduclaw__name`) ∩ `record.tools` (exact equality, request order, dedup; native/unknown dropped; empty refuses). File-size rule: the H8/H9/H12a selector helpers moved verbatim to `tool_policy_selectors.rs` (re-exported, paths unchanged); `tool_policy.rs` 887 → ~790 lines. **Resolver** (`gateway/src/isolation_spawn_selectors.rs`, child of `isolation_resources`): exact schema again, selection's Employee must be the resolver's employee, record task live-owned via the existing `task_owned` (reassigned/deleted ⇒ `Invalid` + `isolation_task_run_refused` with `stage` = tool name; missing store ⇒ `Unavailable`, never created); `spawn_agent` target: not self/reserved/untrusted/`eph-*`, live `can_delegate_rules(delegation_rules_from_home, DispatchOrgView::new(home, None))` (same predicate as the dispatcher gate), then `isolation_lifecycle::confirmed_receipt` (refusal source only): absent ⇒ `Invalid`, disabled ⇒ `Revoked`, Personal (any owner) ⇒ `BindingMismatch`, Shared in another tenant than a Shared source ⇒ `BindingMismatch`, legacy/no binding ⇒ allowed (the handler's full lifecycle read decides). Personal source → Shared target is not refused here: `persist_spawn_authority` → `record_bus_delegation` files the existing `isolation_personal_delegation` owner card (reused, unchanged). **Deviation** (closed by H13b): the spec said "Personal targets of another owner refused"; H13 refused every Personal target because B1 (`capture_binding`) refused any delegated background work of a Personal employee, including the same owner's — H13b (owner decision B) now allows the same owner's. **CLI** (`mcp/spawn.rs`): under an isolated call `spawn_agent` runs `authorize_isolated_spawn` (`authorize_async` with the record resolver + `verify_live`) after the existing delegation/depth gates and before the runaway guard/queue write (refusal: fixed zh-TW, no oracle); `spawn_ephemeral` never scaffolds: `spawn_ephemeral_isolated` authorizes, computes `ephemeral_tools` and calls `duduclaw_gateway::isolation_ephemeral::queue_for_parent`. Unprotected paths unchanged. **Ephemeral job (D-H3 + HX/D8 §1)**: new `gateway/src/isolation_ephemeral.rs`: `queue_for_parent` (Linux; refuses unless `isolation_required()`) takes the parent from `isolation_mcp::delegation_source` (live run association: Human principal, running run, prepared employee/binding/incarnation), requires it equal to the record (employee, run, binding), builds `EphemeralJob { id, parent: ParentRun { agent, employee, incarnation, binding, run, lane, requester, task }, tools, tier, instruction, context, created_at, expires_at = +24 h }` (Personal ⇒ requester must equal owner) and stores it `pending` in private `isolation-ephemeral-jobs.db` (`fs_safe::sqlite::open`; ≤ 4 pending per parent run, ≤ 256 overall ⇒ `Unavailable`), audit `isolation_ephemeral_job_queued` (no text, counts only). D8: the parent never awaits the child (a same-employee wait is `WouldDeadlock`); `EphemeralJobs::take_startable(&dyn ParentRuns, &dyn ParentIdentity, now)` releases a job once, only when `ParentRuns::holds_capability(employee, run)` is `Ok(false)` and `ParentIdentity::current(agent)` equals `(employee, incarnation)`; still held/unreadable liveness or `Unavailable` identity ⇒ stays pending; expired, corrupt, row/job mismatch, another id or incarnation, tombstoned ⇒ `dropped`. Production `ParentIdentity` = `LiveLifecycle` (A2 lifecycle read). **Open (not wired here; (1)–(2) closed by H13b, see below):** (1) no production `ParentRuns` (needs spawnd liveness: H4's `EmployeeIdle` or the authority run snapshot) and no consumer calls `take_startable`, so queued jobs currently expire unstarted after 24 h — the handler tells the employee the child starts only after this run ends; (2) starting a released job needs a background launch under the parent identity: a receipt kind (or `delegation` with a host system sender) that passes `scope_record`/`recheck`, the dispatcher gate's self-delegation denial (`can_delegate_rules` refuses sender == target under every policy) and B1's Shared-only `check_lifecycle` (D-H3 allows a Personal parent's child on the owner's request — owner decision needed whether such a child runs as background work), plus narrowing the child capability's `GrantPolicy.tools` to `job.tools` in `authorize_current` and composing the prompt from `instruction` + `context`; `tier` is recorded but the child uses the parent's model routing until then; (3) a Background-principal parent (cron/task run) cannot file a child (`delegation_source` requires a Human principal) — fail closed; (4) `spawn_agent` in a task run now additionally requires the record task to be live-owned at each call. **Parity:** `spawn_agent`/`spawn_ephemeral` left `pending_production_selectors` and are asserted eligible from real issuer output (spawn_ephemeral stays unlisted in the fixture because `can_create_agents = false`, same as the ordinary list). Tools matrix rows + an H13 paragraph updated; no refusal category changed (`check_isolation_tool_matrix.py` row names unchanged, not executed here). (B) same-owner Personal→Personal `spawn_agent` delegation is allowed (no card: the owner started the source run); other owners refused; Personal→Shared keeps the `personal_delegation` card. **B1 narrowed exactly** in new `isolation_background/same_owner.rs`: the only Personal background shape is a `delegation` receipt captured from a live run (`caller`/`parent_run`/`parent_agent`/`parent_policy` present) whose target binding AND the source's live binding are the same `Personal(owner)` and whose receipt owner is exactly `user:<owner>` (`personal_receipt_allowed`); it is applied at `store::validate`, `check_lifecycle`, `recheck_receipt` (`check_binding_facts` replaces `check_tenant`: Shared = same tenant check; Personal = fresh users.db snapshot's human, mirror owner, sole member and binding are the owner; `check_source_binding` = the source is still that owner's Personal employee) and the issuer (`boot::issuance_facts` takes the receipt; `personal_record_allowed`). Capture (`record_delegation_inner`): `capture_lifecycle` + `personal_target_allowed(source, target, requester)`; no card for a Personal target. Cron/reminder/heartbeat/task/task_spec of a Personal employee and every Shared→Personal delegation stay refused. The H13 resolver's `target_binding_allowed` admits `Personal(o)` only from `Personal(o)` and then requires `ephemeral_parent_allowed` (owner-started run). No `crates/duduclaw-cli/src/mcp/personal_target.rs` existed in the H13 tree (P1c not merged there), so the gateway rule was the only one and the CLI handler is unchanged. Merge note (W12 branch, with P1c): P1c's CLI pre-dispatch rule (`personal_target::DELEGATION_TOOLS` → `personal_visibility::delegation_refusal`) also allows the same owner's Personal employees for `spawn_agent`, so the two layers agree; both still run (CLI first, then the gateway resolver). **Ephemeral start (gaps 1–3 closed):** (1) production `ParentRuns` = new `isolation_ephemeral_runs.rs` ledger fed by `GatewayAuthority::bind_run` (bound) and `revoke_run` (exited: cancel, terminal, disconnect, failed prepare, launch-guard drop): `Ok(false)` only for a run bound and revoked in this gateway epoch while no other run of the employee is bound (D8); unknown run ⇒ `Unavailable` (job waits, expires after 24 h; a gateway restart therefore drops jobs of pre-restart parents), another employee ⇒ `BindingMismatch`; bounded to 4096 exited runs. Runner: new `isolation_ephemeral_start::start_released`, called on every dispatcher tick before `poll_and_dispatch_sqlite` (never creates the job store); `take_startable(LiveRuns, LiveLifecycle)` then `QueueMessage` `ephemeral-<job uuid>` on `message_queue.db` (sender = target = parent label, `sender_agent`/`origin_agent` = label, `on_behalf_of` = `user:<requester>`, depth 1, lane from the parent, no reply channel); audit `isolation_ephemeral_job_started` / `_start_failed` (one-shot: a failed enqueue is not retried). Authority: `queue_for_parent` now captures the child's `delegation` receipt from the LIVE parent run via `ephemeral_child::record_child` → `record_delegation_inner` (target = source; fresh lifecycle + users.db reads) before storing the job; material = `ephemeral_child::child_material` (masked prompt = instruction + context, same fields the dispatcher/`read_current` derive from the row), so `scope_record`/`recheck` authorize the child like any delegation; a child's run cannot file another child (depth 1). (2) Dispatcher: `delegation_gate::gate_bus_dispatch` replaces only the org-tree predicate (`SelfDelegation`) when sender == target, the sender is not a system sender, and `self_delegation_allowed`: the row id names a `released` job (`EphemeralJobs::released`, keys rechecked) of that label for exactly its requester whose employee id + incarnation equal the live lifecycle (`LiveLifecycle`); the person check still runs. (3) `authorize_current` → `ephemeral_child::child_tools`: a self-delegation must be a released job's receipt matching it exactly (label, employee, incarnation, binding, parent run, requester) and `GrantPolicy.tools` = live snapshot ∩ job tools (grant order; empty refuses); any other self-delegation refuses. (4) Tier recorded only; the child uses the parent's model routing. **Open:** the child receipt's `expires_at` is the parent capability's (≤ 30 min after its issue, as for every delegation), so a long parent run can leave the child refused at recheck; the child's reply has no delegation callback (it is not routed back to the owner's chat); jobs of a parent from before a gateway restart never start. Tests (`h13b_`; not compiled or run here, Linux builder): gateway `isolation_ephemeral_start::tests::{h13b_personal_child_runs_after_parent_exit_as_same_identity, h13b_self_delegation_exception_only_for_matching_job, h13b_stale_incarnation_job_never_starts}`, `isolation_background::ephemeral_child::tests::{h13b_personal_spawn_ephemeral_requires_owner_initiated_parent, h13b_child_tools_narrowed_to_job}`, `isolation_resources::spawn::tests::{h13b_same_owner_personal_delegation_allowed, h13b_cross_owner_personal_delegation_refused, h13b_shared_unchanged}` (+ updated `spawn_agent_refuses_foreign_personal_target`, `isolation_mcp::boot` `issuer_personal_background_refused` / `issuer_shared_unchanged` for the new `issuance_facts` signature). **H13c security fix round (2026-11-09, independent review NO-GO; tests `h13c_*`; not compiled or run here, no shell):** (1, High) "the owner started this run" now means a LIVE interactive run: a run issued from a background receipt also gets `Principal::Human{receipt owner}` (`boot::issuance_principal`), so a same-owner delegated background run or an ephemeral child could fan out to the owner's other Personal employees with no owner present. `same_owner::personal_target_from_run(source, target, requester, source.parent)` (used by `record_delegation_inner`, i.e. `record_bus_delegation` for `spawn_agent` and `record_child` for `spawn_ephemeral`) refuses a Personal target unless `DelegationSource.parent` is `None`; `queue_for_parent` refuses a Personal parent whose run has a parent receipt (`same_owner::ephemeral_parent_live`); mirror: the issuer (`human_authorization_with` → `same_owner::record_run_tools`) never signs `spawn_agent`/`spawn_ephemeral` into a record-backed run of a Personal employee (not discovered, not eligible — the boundary's `ephemeral_parent_allowed` cannot see the difference; Personal→Shared from such a run is therefore not offered either; Shared runs unchanged). (3) Job lifecycle `pending → released → started` (schema CHECK widened; an H13b store is migrated in place, rows kept): the dispatcher gate's `self_delegation_check` moves `released → started` with one conditional UPDATE after its checks (once per job; a retried/duplicated row is refused, a refused check consumes nothing), and `authorize_current` accepts only a `started` job (`EphemeralJobs::started`). The receipt material binds a digest of `job.tools` (`ephemeral_child::JOB_TOOLS_FIELD`, `child_material`); `scope_record` and `recheck_receipt` add the stored job's digest for every `delegation` record whose id is `ephemeral-<uuid>` (`bind_job_tools`; missing/unreadable job refuses), so a job row edited after capture never runs. (4) A job never grants a spawn tool: boundary `ephemeral_tools` drops `SPAWN_TOOLS` from the intersection and gateway `narrowed_tools` filters them again. (5, D8) `take_startable` releases at most one job per employee per call and none while another job of that employee is `released` and unexpired (enqueued, run not yet admitted/reserved/bound); a released job whose enqueue failed is now `dropped` (`drop_released`) so it never holds the slot. Verified: spawnd's admission coordinator already enforces D8 per employee — a second run of the same employee is queued behind the first (`deferred_until_current_run_ends`, `admission_helpers_tests::helper_call_never_occupies_d8_slot`) and a same-employee wait is `WouldDeadlock` — so the gateway ledger is defence in depth; the window between gate admission (`started`) and the child's `bind_run` is covered only by spawnd. Tests: gateway `isolation_background::ephemeral_child::tests::{h13c_background_source_never_reaches_personal_target, h13c_spawn_tools_not_offered_to_personal_background_runs, h13c_child_never_holds_spawn_tools}`, `isolation_ephemeral_start::tests::{h13c_self_delegation_exception_once_per_job, h13c_d8_one_child_per_employee_at_a_time, h13c_job_tools_bound_into_child_material, h13c_job_store_migrates_started_state}` (+ updated `h13b_personal_child_runs_after_parent_exit_as_same_identity`, `h13b_self_delegation_exception_only_for_matching_job` for the once-only exception and the tools digest); boundary `tool_policy::spawn::tests::h13c_ephemeral_job_never_grants_spawn_tools`. Open: the `record_delegation_inner`/`queue_for_parent` call sites are Linux-only and covered through the pure rules; an end-to-end background→spawn refusal needs the H15 Linux fixture. **H13d follow-up (round 3, Low; tests `h13d_*`; not compiled or run here, no shell):** (A) `EphemeralJobs::begin_start(id, now, check)` refuses and drops (`dropped`) a released job with `now >= expires_at` (the gate's `self_delegation_check_at` takes `now`; production passes `now_seconds()`), so an expired released job — ignored by the D8 busy set — can no longer start beside the next child of the same employee. (B) the busy set in `take_startable` is `state IN ('released','started') AND expires_at > now`: a started job holds its employee's slot before its run binds. (C) new terminal state `finished` (`JobState::Finished`, schema CHECK widened; stores without `'finished'` are migrated in place, rows kept): `EphemeralJobs::finish(id)` = `started → finished`, idempotent, other states untouched. The run ledger remembers which bound run is a child (`GatewayAuthority::bind_run` → `record_child_bound(run, receipt.home, job)` when `ephemeral_job_of(receipt)`), and `revoke_run` → `record_exited` marks that job finished (best effort; if the write fails the job stops holding the slot at its TTL). `authorize_current`/`child_tools` read only `started`, so a stored child receipt cannot produce a second run after the first ended; a started job whose run never binds (no `revoke_run`) keeps its slot until `expires_at` (fail closed). - Merge H12b/H12c/H12d into the W12 integration branch (with HX4/HX5/W1, P1/P1b/P1c, H13/H13b): the pending list has one source, `mcp-boundary` `workspace_selection::PENDING_WORKSPACE_SELECTOR_TOOLS` (issuer `drops_from_signed` and the CLI parity test). `skill_hub_install` and `skill_synthesis_run` were removed from it; `skill_from_recording` stays until HX6 installs the `RecordingJobs` seam. The parity test now asserts that hub/synthesis are not pending and that `skill_from_recording` is, and pins their exact selection: hub = own Employee + PublicOrigins, synthesis = own Employee only. `tool_policy::authorize` keeps both early returns (H13 `spawn::authorize_spawn`, then H12 `skill_jobs::authorize`). Owner decisions confirmed in code. (a) Hub install is tied to `[isolation] public_web`. (b) `skill_synthesis_run` needs no admin card: an owned run skips the global-bank card (`pipeline.rs` `protected && owned.is_none()`), which closes H12c's "owner may require a card" item as "no card". - From HX6 ([SEC] browser recording as an in-run helper; D8 revision §4, F8/F10/F11/F16, Q3 = A): **Route**: `browser_record_start/stop` are `Route::InRunHelper` (`tool_policy::IN_RUN_RECORDING_TOOLS`); `execution_in_run::is_in_run` stays the three foreground tools; the legacy child-run recording path (`stop_recording`, `collects_results`/`cooperative_stop` for the recording) is gone (`hx6_recording_never_uses_the_child_run_path`). **Boundary** (`mcp-boundary/src/execution_recording.rs`, child of `execution.rs`): start = transport run == `record.run` → `authorize_async` → `helper_calls` bit → `helper_recording` bit (else `helper_recording_unsupported`) → `authorize_handler` → `prepare_in_run` (must be `AuthorizedHelperPlan::recording`, run/employee = caller) → re-authorize + `verify_live` → `start_helper(slot: recording)`; `HelperError::Absent` (new, from spawnd `runtime_denied` = no such binding in the run's signed table; by publication check 4 an offline parent) ⇒ `helper_parent_offline`; then the row is inserted (a `StartGuard` cancels the slot and stores `uncertain/start_abandoned` if the request is dropped first) and a collector task owns the pipes. Result `{status: recording, id: <call hex>, effective_timeout_ms}`. Stop: exact keys `id`(+`workspace`), `parse_id` (32 lowercase hex, never zero) → owner read → terminal or other run ⇒ stored view, no helper interaction; same run (the `:180` inversion) requires the run's own transport → `request_stop` (live check under the store lock) → `HelperStop` (`ExecutionBackend::stop_helper`, new trait method; `SpawndBackend` → `helper_call::Control::Stop`) → waits ≤ 60 s for the collector's terminal row, else `stop_requested`. Collector: bounded stdout/stderr (64 KiB) + `wait_helper`; `decode_helper_result` (gateway: strict `recording_helper::decode_result`, stderr empty, `Exited(0)` or — recording only — `RunEnded`, the launcher's SIGTERM flush), id must equal the call id; `ended_by` = stop / admin / revoked (from the row) else `run_end` (`RunEnded`, `NotLive`, or a driver `stop` nobody here asked for = launcher drain) else `deadline`; failures: `invalid_result` (exit 0, bad bytes), `uncertain` + `run_killed` / `helper_timed_out` / `helper_oom_killed` / `helper_unavailable` / `helper_failed{exit_code}` / `helper_recording_unsupported` (exit 78, nested sandbox not installable) / `collector_timeout` (effective + 60 s; kills only the slot). Revocation [F16]: the collector polls the start context's `verify_live` (250 ms); on failure `HelperStop` and, if accepted, `stop_by = revoked`. Admin RPC (`isolation.recording_status/stop`, `isolation_mcp::control_recording`): Admin role re-check → `mcp_boundary::execution::control_recording` (live ⇒ `mark_stop(admin)` + `HelperStop`; ended ⇒ stored view; never `HelperCall`). **Store** (`mcp-boundary/src/recording_jobs.rs`, `SqliteRecordingJobs`, table `recording_jobs` in `isolation-execution-jobs.db`, own connection, opened at boot next to `SqliteExecutionJobs`): PK `call`, `run` column, employee + principal (incl. generation) + binding like `execution_jobs`, state `recording|stop_requested|complete|uncertain`, first `stop_by` wins, `finish` once, insert only for the capability's own run, ≤ 32 rows/employee and 4096 total, 7-day expiry of terminal rows, live rows become `uncertain/gateway_restarted` at open. [F11] `artifacts_from_result` + `verify_artifact(digests, name, bytes)` (size and sha256; mismatch ⇒ `ArtifactError::Modified`, code `artifact_modified`) and `RecordingJobs::artifacts(employee, call)` are the seam for the D-H1 broker consumers and H12d. **H12d interplay — NOT wired**: this branch has no `tool_policy::RecordingJobs` / `install_recording_jobs` and no `duduclaw_isolation::recording_layout` (H12b/c/d are not merged here), so `skill_from_recording` stays in `PENDING_WORKSPACE_SELECTOR_TOOLS`; when H12d merges, implement its seam over `SqliteRecordingJobs` (`artifacts` + `verify_artifact`, layout = `recording_jobs::{RECORDINGS_DIR, ARTIFACT_NAMES}` or H12d's shared layout — keep them identical), then remove it from the list. **Gate**: new `SpawndFeatures::HELPER_RECORDING` (bit 2, `helper_recording`, availability only, gates no opcode); spawnd advertises it via `Host::recording_ready` (default false; `Core` returns `host_state::RECORDING_PROBE_PASSED` = **false**: no in-process Chromium probe exists, so production recording stays refused until one is added and proven on the H15 hosts); `EvidenceReport::helper_recording()`; readiness `Reason::HelperRecordingUnsupported` when `duduclaw-browser-record-v1` is published but the bit (with `helper_calls`) is absent; dashboard banner reason + admin egress statement (`isolationReadiness.recordingEgress`, en/zh-TW/ja-JP). **Selector** (`isolation_mcp/selectors.rs`): `Selection.recording` = a recording tool listed ∧ `helper_calls` ∧ `helper_recording` ∧ interactive run (no task) ∧ profile allows the tool ∧ `[isolation] public_web` (Q3 = A: recording egress is the employee's public web); it selects the brokered `Workspace{employee-work}`; `workspace_selection::drops_from_signed_with(name, scoped, helpers, recording)` signs exactly the two recording tools for it (the foreground `helpers` bit never does); the B2 re-read compares the new field too; with `recording` it also signs the D-H2 `PublicOrigins { * }` grant (tag 11, see **URL authority** below), never a `UrlOrigin`. **Helper** (`recording_helper.rs`): in-run layout only (`helper_layout::current().in_run()`, legacy `/runtime` + `/tmp` refused), id = spawnd's call id from `/helper-tmp/<call>` (`in_run_request`; the gateway sends `PLACEHOLDER_ID` and can never choose it), signed files `/helpers/{node,playwright.cjs,chromium}` (`BROWSER_FILES`, digest order of the fixed argv — HX3b: they must be flat signed helper-table files), `forwarder_port(HTTPS_PROXY)` = `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/` only, staging `/helper-tmp/<call>/recording` (0700), driver env `DUDUCLAW_RECORD_{DIR,ROOT,CONTAINED}`; node runs in its own process group under **nested Landlock** (`recording_sandbox.rs`, raw syscalls prepared before fork, `pre_exec` = `setpgid` + `landlock_restrict_self`): `/helper-tmp/<call>` RW, the three signed files RX, `/usr` RX (`/lib*` only if real dirs; merged-usr symlinks skipped), `/etc` `/proc` RO, `/dev/null` `/dev/urandom`; never `/work`; ABI ≥ 4: TCP connect only to the forwarder port, no TCP bind; ABI ≥ 6: abstract-UNIX + signal scoping; a layer that cannot be built ⇒ exit 78 (never an unconfined browser). After the driver: SIGKILL the whole group and wait for `ESRCH` (≤ 5 s) before `recording_io::publish` into `/work/recordings/<id>/` (unchanged `openat2` no-symlink publication). Driver: `chromiumSandbox: !contained` (Chromium's own sandbox cannot start under the common filter; the helper sandbox is the containment), `--disable-dev-shm-usage`, Playwright's default pipe transport (no DevTools TCP port), `--js-flags=--jitless` in contained mode (decision: less exploit surface for page script; cost is slower page JS). **CLI**: protected `browser_record_start` schema lost `action`/`job`; descriptions say in-run/flushed at run end/public-web egress. the production gate stays off)**: (1) [Medium] new `duduclaw-execution/src/recording_seccomp.rs`: a pre-built nested BPF filter installed last in the browser `pre_exec` (after `setpgid` + browser Landlock; one `seccomp(SET_MODE_FILTER, 0)` call, no allocation): `socket()` only `AF_INET`/`AF_INET6` + `SOCK_STREAM` (`SOCK_NONBLOCK|SOCK_CLOEXEC` masked) + protocol 0/`IPPROTO_TCP`, `socketpair()` only `AF_UNIX` stream/seqpacket, `sendto`/`sendmsg`/`sendmmsg` with `MSG_FASTOPEN` EPERM, `setsid`/`setpgid` EPERM, default ALLOW (only narrows F_L ∩ F_H), foreign arch/x32 killed; residual = pre-ABI-4 loopback TCP). confirm Chromium starts under the nested filter (no `AF_NETLINK`/`AF_UNIX` `socket()`, no datagram `socketpair`, no `setsid` — crashpad/zygote behaviour) and under the production F_H (self-only `prctl` list, refused `chmod`/xattr/SysV calls);
