a dedicated probe run would need a probe account/storage lease (now documented in the HX doc §4 gate + T5 row, see round 3); (b) ~~a failed probe stays failed until spawnd restarts~~ — round 3: only hard failures (exit 78 / other exit / wrong output / stderr / start failure) are final, and so is the third inconclusive attempt; (c) ~~verdict only `eprintln`~~ — round 3: dedicated spawnd audit events; (d) `Cargo.lock` gains the `duduclaw-run` dev-dependency edge of `duduclaw-execution` on the next build. **Security round 3 (2026-11-09, Low/Info, tests `hx6r3_*`; the production gate stays off)**: (N1, Low, blocked the gate flip) `host_probe.rs` `ProbeSchedule`: a run is eligible only with remaining run time ≥ `PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS` (60 s) + its `termination_grace_ms` (checked again in `start_recording_probe`, else `NotReady`), so the deadline can never clamp the probe into a false timeout; `Verdict` = `Passed` / `Failed` (exit 78, any other exit, wrong/oversized output, stderr, `StartFailed`: final) / `Inconclusive` (`TimedOut`, `OomKilled`, `Signalled`: retried in a run of a **different** employee — the inconclusive employees are excluded — after a 30 s then 120 s backoff; the 3rd (`MAX_INCONCLUSIVE`) inconclusive attempt is a final failure, audited `exhausted`) / `RunEnded` (not counted; the run is not reused). (N5) a decided probe resets the schedule (tried runs, employees, backoff); while idle the tried-runs set is pruned to live runs. (N2, Info) the probe borrows an employee's run (rec slot ≤ 60 s + watchdog, slot memory/tasks, the run's egress forwarder; no request, network or artifacts): documented in the HX doc §4 gate and T5; new spawnd audit events `recording_probe_started` / `recording_probe_verdict` (`Detail::RecordingProbe{call, attempt, landlock_abi, outcome?, verdict?}`, `audit::ProbeVerdict` = passed/failed/inconclusive/exhausted/run_ended) with the run's employee and run ids through the audit worker; the `eprintln!` is gone. (N3, Info) `duduclaw_run::helper_policy::{RECORDING_ONLY_PRCTL = [36, 37], recording_only_violation(slot, self_only)}`: options 36/37 stay in the global `PRCTL_SELF_ONLY` but spawnd `validate_launcher` refuses them (and `NestedSandbox`) for every non-recording binding (`NotNarrowing`), and `LauncherPlan::validate` (`runner.rs`) refuses them like `NestedSandbox` (`Error::Seccomp`). (N4, Low) `host_state::RecordingGate` (shared `AtomicBool`): `Core::refresh` stores `helper_recording()` of the report it just published (false when poisoned, and on `Core` drop); `LinuxHost::recording_ready` reads it without the view lock, so `try_lock` contention no longer refuses with `helper_recording_unsupported`. (Audit gap, prev 6) `RecordingAuditKind::{StartRefused, Failed}` → `isolation_recording_start_refused` (no call: `RecordingAuditEvent.call` is now `Option`, `fields()` writes `call: null`; `reason` = the returned helper status — `helper_launcher_unavailable`, `helper_recording_unsupported` with `gate: gateway|spawnd` (spawnd's `HelperRecordingUnsupported`), `helper_parent_offline`, other `HelperError::code()`s) and `isolation_recording_failed` (terminal outcomes stored only as an `uncertain` reason: `browser_tree_survived`, `helper_recording_unsupported` (exit 78), `helper_timed_out`, `helper_oom_killed`, `helper_failed` + `exit_code`, `helper_unavailable`, `run_killed`; with the root `outcome`), both warnings, through the gateway's `GatewayRecordingAudit` (`boot_recording.rs`). Authorization refusals (`refused()`) are not recording facts and stay unaudited here. (Tripwire, prev 7) `recording_jobs::verify_artifact(digests, name, bytes: Vec<u8>) -> Result<VerifiedArtifact, ArtifactError>`: `VerifiedArtifact` (private fields, `#[must_use]`, no `Clone`/`Default`/`Deserialize`) is its only constructor and the only way back to the bytes, so H12d's distill step must take `VerifiedArtifact` as input; the CLI tripwire now scans `mcp_recording_distill.rs` with comments and string literals removed (`strip_comments_and_strings`, self-tested) and pins the seam's signature at compile time. Tests (not compiled or run here: macOS worktree, no shell): spawnd `host::probe::tests::{hx6r3_probe_timeout_oom_signal_are_inconclusive, hx6r3_probe_needs_remaining_time_for_timeout_and_grace, hx6r3_probe_retries_inconclusive_across_employees_with_backoff}` (+ `hx6fix_recording_probe_verdict_is_exact_and_fails_closed` updated: timeout/OOM/signal no longer fail), `audit::tests::hx6r3_recording_probe_audit_records_are_bounded`, `host_state::tests::hx6r3_recording_gate_follows_published_report_without_lock`, `helper_policy::tests::hx6r3_subreaper_prctl_refused_for_non_recording_bindings`; run `helper_policy::tests::hx6r3_subreaper_prctl_options_are_recording_only`, `runner::hx6fix_tests::hx6r3_launcher_plan_refuses_subreaper_outside_the_recording_slot`; mcp-boundary `recording_audit::tests::hx6r3_recording_audit_refusal_and_failure_facts`, `execution::recording::tests::{hx6r3_recording_terminal_failures_are_audited, hx6r3_recording_start_refusals_are_audited}` (+ `artifact_hash_mismatch_refused`, `recording_stop_same_run_returns_artifacts`, `hx6fix_recording_audit_covers_…` adapted); CLI `hx6r3_tripwire_scan_ignores_comments_and_strings`. **Open (round 3)**: with a single helper-entitled employee, an inconclusive probe cannot be retried (fails closed: recording stays unsupported until another employee's run or a spawnd restart). **URL authority (2026-11-09, tests `hx6url_*`)**: `browser_record_start` requires `url`, and the generic boundary rule turned any `url` into a required exact `UrlOrigin` that the selector cannot sign (the URL is unknown at issuance), so every real start would have been refused once recording is enabled. Per D-H2 and Q3 = A the authority is now the MAC-covered `PublicOrigins` grant: `tool_policy::RECORDING_URL_TOOLS = ["browser_record_start"]`; `required_for_call` makes its `url` (or an `http(s)://` `url_or_path`) require `EmployeeScope` + `PublicOrigins` instead of `UrlOrigin`; `authorize` takes both grants from the verified record itself (`recording_url_grants`; no grant ⇒ refused; a resolver answer containing any `UrlOrigin`/`PublicOrigins`/`EmployeeScope` ⇒ refused, never merged) and `check_selected_resources` requires `resources::permitted_public_origin` (the same check as the web tools: https, default port, no userinfo, a public DNS name, never an IP literal, no `localhost`/`.local`/`.internal`/… names; **http is refused**, the grant is HTTPS-only by contract); egress stays public-address-only through the run's forwarder. Every other tool's `UrlOrigin` rule is unchanged, and the tool is **not** added to `PUBLIC_ORIGIN_TOOLS` (no web-fetch path). Selector: `Selection::apply` pushes the single `PublicOrigins { * }` when `public_web || recording` (the `recording` conditions are unchanged: `helper_calls` + `helper_recording`, `[isolation] public_web`, no task, profile allows); `Selection::signed_tools` drops `web_fetch_cached`/`web_extract` when the grant exists only for the recording (`recording && !public_web`, e.g. the profile denies the web tools), so it never widens which tools may make web calls. Gateway resolver (`isolation_resources.rs`) adds no `UrlOrigin` for `browser_record_start`. Tests (not compiled or run here: macOS worktree, no shell): mcp-boundary `tool_policy::tests::{hx6url_recording_start_is_authorized_by_public_origins_without_url_origin, hx6url_recording_start_refused_without_public_origins, hx6url_recording_start_refuses_private_loopback_and_credentialed_urls, hx6url_other_tools_keep_url_origin_semantics}`, `execution::recording::tests::hx6url_recording_start_with_public_origins_starts_and_without_is_refused` (fixture now signs EmployeeScope + `PublicOrigins { * }` instead of `UrlOrigin`; `recording_start_unsigned_origin_is_refused` = refused without PublicOrigins and for private/loopback/credentialed/http URLs); gateway `isolation_resources::tests::hx6url_gateway_resolver_adds_no_url_origin_for_recording_start`, `selectors_tests::hx6url_recording_signs_public_origins_only_with_public_web`. **Low-severity cleanups (2026-11-09, tests `hx6c_*`; the production gate stays off)**: (N1) a recording is authorized only by the all-public grant (`PublicOrigins` = exactly `["*"]`, `tool_policy::all_public_grant`): a narrow grant bounds only the start URL (the page can then reach any public host the forwarder allows), so `recording_url_grants` and `check_selected_resources` refuse a non-`*` grant for `browser_record_start` (documented on `RECORDING_URL_TOOLS`; the same narrow grant still serves `web_fetch_cached`/`web_extract`); the `hx6url_recording_start_is_authorized_…` narrow-grant part now asserts refusal. (N2) spawnd `host_probe.rs`: `Signalled(31)` (SIGSYS, the seccomp kill) is a hard `Failed` (deterministic, not host load); other signals/timeouts/OOM stay inconclusive. Retry scheduling: another employee's run first; only when none is eligible and an inconclusive result exists, `ProbeSchedule::eligible_retry` lets the same employee (its inconclusive run is `untry`'d) go again once the backoff has passed, still bounded by `MAX_INCONCLUSIVE` — this closes the round-3 single-employee **Open** item. (N3) `recording_audit::StartRefusedLimiter` (used by `GatewayRecordingAudit`): `StartRefused` records are merged per (employee, run, reason): the first is written, further ones in a 60 s window only count, and the next refusal after the window (or a sweep at the 1024-key bound) writes one summary with `suppressed` and `window_secs`; at the bound a new key's record is dropped; other kinds are never limited. A pending count with no later refusal is not flushed. Tests (not compiled or run here: macOS worktree, no shell): spawnd `host::probe::tests::{hx6c_probe_sigsys_is_a_hard_failure, hx6c_probe_single_employee_retries_after_backoff}` (+ `hx6r3_probe_timeout_oom_signal_are_inconclusive` now uses signal 15, not 31); mcp-boundary `recording_audit::tests::{hx6c_start_refused_audit_is_rate_limited_per_employee_run_reason, hx6c_start_refused_limiter_is_bounded}`, `tool_policy::tests::hx6url_recording_start_is_authorized_by_public_origins_without_url_origin` (narrow-grant part); gateway `boot::recording::tests::hx6c_gateway_sink_merges_start_refused_records`.

- From F6c2 (approval rule R1/R2/S1/S2/S12/S16, F6 design §3.4): **Log** — the users.db `admin_eligibility_log` and its same-statement SQLite triggers already landed with F6a/F6b; F6c2 adds no schema, only `admin_eligibility::log_intact` (schema version == 1 and all 15 triggers present, `TRIGGERS`) and `eligible_user_ids`. One `eligibility_log_written_in_same_tx_*` test per writer path (`admin_eligibility_writer_tests.rs`: break the log with a `RAISE(ABORT)` trigger → the writer fails and its change is absent; heal → it succeeds and appends): `create_user`, `create_user_as`/invited, `create_member_at_epoch`, `ensure_default_admin`, `claim_setup_token`, `migration_invite_user`/`migration_user_disable`, `apply_user_update_as`/`update_user` (role; password clears the forced flag), `set_user_status_as` (also gateway `users.remove`), `offboard_user`, `claim_default_admin_with_email`, `change_email`, `consume_user_invite`, `mfa_confirm`, `mfa_change` (disable), `mfa_admin_reset`, `mfa_admin_reset_reauth`, `mfa_operator_reset`, `mfa_email_verify_and_issue` (add), `mfa_email_remove_and_issue`, `mfa_passkey_delete`, lockout via `mfa_verify` (counter and lockout row land together or not at all; eligibility unchanged), plus statement-level tests for the paths without a drivable API (passkey register insert, forced-password flag set, account delete — no auth API sets the flag on an existing account or deletes a user). **Rule** — new `duduclaw-auth/src/admin_approval_rule.rs`: `decide(users_tx: &Connection, &ApprovalRequest{kind: ApprovalKind (personal_restore|shared_restore|personal_adopt|shared_adopt|early_purge|early_purged_retire), requester_user_id, owner_user_id: Option (required and == requester for Personal kinds), approver_user_id, fresh_step_up, now: unix s}) -> ApprovalDecision::{Allowed{self_approved, notice_addresses} | Refused(ApprovalRefusal)}`; trait `AdminApprovalRule` + `EligibilityLogRule` for the gateway (F6c/F6i/F6g/F6l take it as a dependency). Refusal codes: `INVALID_REQUEST`, `STEP_UP_REQUIRED` (every approval), `APPROVAL_NOT_IN_TRANSACTION` (autocommit connection: the count must be read in the apply tx), `SECOND_ADMIN_REQUIRED` (approver is requester/owner while another qualifying Admin exists), `APPROVER_NOT_QUALIFIED` (`NotQualified::{NotEligibleNow, PromotedByParty, EligibleUnderCooling}`), `SELF_APPROVAL_COOLING`, `ADMIN_LOG_UNAVAILABLE`. Qualifying = eligible now ∧ eligible for the whole 7-day window by the log ∧ no `admin_created`/`role` row in the window by the requester/owner or unattributed. Cooling: any other account with an eligible→ineligible row in the window (even if eligible again — §5 "counts every loss") or eligible in the window and not now. Fail closed: log missing/unreadable, creation marker missing, schema ≠ 1, a trigger missing, or latest-row-per-account ≠ live eligibility ⇒ `ADMIN_LOG_UNAVAILABLE` for **every** path (qualification is read from the log), so until the log covers 7 days nothing that needs the rule can be approved, not only self-approval (stricter reading of §7). Window membership by `seq` from the first row with `at > now − 7 d` (a clock step back only moves rows into the window). Notices: `notice_addresses(conn, now)` = every logged address of an account eligible in the last 30 days (deleted accounts included); `promotion_notices_since(conn, after_seq, now)` / `UserDb::admin_eligibility_high_water` + `admin_promotion_notices_since` for S2; `record_self_approval(tx, &request, subject)` writes the users.db `isolation.employee_restore_self_approved` audit row in the caller's tx; `audit_fields` gives the §3.8 fields. Tests (not compiled or run here): rule `owner_admin_cannot_self_approve_with_other_admin`, `shared_requester_cannot_self_approve`, `sole_admin_self_approves_with_step_up_and_audit`, `inactive_or_no_mfa_admin_not_counted`, `self_approval_refused_within_cooling_window`, `admin_count_read_inside_apply_tx`, `self_approval_refused_after_forced_pw_or_mfa_reset_within_7d`, `self_approval_refused_after_admin_delete_within_7d`, `lockout_does_not_reduce_admin_count`, `self_approval_refused_when_log_unreadable_or_under_7d`, `recently_promoted_admin_cannot_approve`, `admin_promoted_by_requester_cannot_approve`, `admin_promotion_notifies_all_admins`, `notices_use_log_addresses_after_account_delete`, `malformed_requests_refused_before_any_read`. **Deferred:** gateway wiring (F6c restore decide, F6i early purge, F6g adoption card, F6l retire card call `EligibilityLogRule` inside their users.db tx and persist `self_approved`); S2 promotion e-mails from `users.create`/`users.update`/`members.create` (gateway handlers must read the high-water mark before the write and send `admin_promotion_notices_since` after commit); delivery of S16 notices; UI button states (§4). The test-only `mfa::test_totp_prepare`/`test_totp_code` helpers are `#[cfg(test)]`.
- From F6h (F6 design r5 §3.3, §3.10 step 7, §3.12; H4, H5, R7, S7, S9, S10): new `duduclaw-gateway/src/isolation_label_stores.rs` (+ `_tests.rs`, and `isolation_label_stores_lifecycle_tests.rs` as a child of the lifecycle tests for the fixtures). **Registry** `LABEL_KEYED_STORES` (S9): `org_entry` (org.toml, action Digest), `memory` (memory.db), `sessions` (sessions.db), `tasks` (tasks.db), `cron` (cron_tasks.db, action Pause), `website_credentials` (`secrets/website_credentials.enc`, Strip), `channel_routes` (`agent_bindings.json` channel-user bindings and bind tokens, Strip), `delegation_targets` (org.toml rows whose `reports_to` is the label, Strip: re-rooted, department kept). Each entry: `count_under` (missing store = positive 0, unreadable = `Err`, never zero), idempotent `rekey(old, new)` and `purge(label)`; `label_free`, `rekey_all`, `purge_all`, `counts_under`, `quarantine_key(employee, incarnation)` = `quarantine:<hex>-<inc>`. SQLite stores discover their label columns by exact name (`agent_id`, `assigned_to`, `owner_agent_id`, `agent`, `agent_name`, `agent_label`) in every table on every call, so later tables are covered without editing the registry; purge first deletes child rows by `session_id`/`task_id`/`cron_id` of the label's parent rows (append-only tables whose triggers refuse deletes, e.g. `task_authority_epochs`, are kept), all in one IMMEDIATE tx per store. Config maps cannot be sealed under the row, so `purge` detaches the label and `rekey` detaches it too, except stores with a `hold` (website grants are held under the row key; see Restore / purge below); pointing routes at a new id stays an explicit admin edit. `[channels.*]` and `[capabilities] db_sources` are in the tree (F6a strip, `IN_TREE_STRIPPED`). **Writer gate** (H4): `check_label_writable(home, label)` refuses while an `employee_quarantine` row on the exact label is not closed (`LABEL_QUARANTINED`) or the confirmed row is disabled; an unreadable/unsafe/future-schema apply.db refuses (`LABEL_STATE_UNAVAILABLE`, fail closed); no apply.db or no row = allowed. Active only when `protected_active()` (= `runtime_readiness::is_protected_linux()`; tests force it per thread), so unprotected installs do no extra I/O. Call sites: sessions `session_origin/store.rs::create_inner` and `session.rs::append_delivery_message` (session's agent), tasks `task_store/tasks.rs::insert_task_tx` (every task insert), cron `cron_store.rs::insert` and `edit_scoped`, memory via a new process-wide gate in `duduclaw-memory` (`label_gate.rs`, `install_label_write_gate(fn)`; called in `SqliteMemoryEngine::store`, `store_temporal_outcome_checked`, `store_fact_outcome`; the gateway installs `memory_write_gate` at boot in `server.rs`; `agent:<label>` namespaces are the same label, exact prefix token). The gate derives the home from the store connection's database file (`Connection::path`), so an in-memory/unknown file refuses in protected mode. **Tombstone hook** `tombstone_effects` (§3.2 step 4), called by `isolation_lifecycle::tombstone_employee` after the run/route fences: Strip stores detached with names merged into `stripped.global_routes.<store>`, cron rows of the label set `enabled = 0` (`last_error = employee_tombstoned`, ids in `stripped.paused_work.cron`), Gate/Pause stores counted into `employee_quarantine.store_marks` (first readable mark kept on retry), `stripped.deferred` now lists the unmodelled §3.3 routes, failures become `stores:` pending fences (block restore until a retry clears them); summary in the tombstone audit (`label_stores`). New hooks: `agent_binding::{label_route_count, strip_label_routes}` (under `with_file_lock`, never creates the file, names `binding:<channel>:<user>` / `token:<channel>`), `website_credentials::{agent_grant_count, hold_agent, regrant_held, purge_agent}` (fix round: **nothing is deleted at tombstone** — the label's grant on each record moves to a new `Metadata.held_grants: [{agent, key = quarantine:<hex>-<inc>}]` (serde default, omitted when empty), a held grant is never usable (`for_agent`/connectors read only `allowed_agents`), a held-only record is valid (`allowed_agents` may be empty only while a grant is held), `delete` refuses a held record, an admin `upsert` keeps the holds; `count_under(key)` counts holds, `count_under(label)` does not, so adoption's label-free check is unaffected). **Bot gate** (H5, A11): `channel_bot_allowed(home, label, channel)` in `spawn_telegram_bot`/`spawn_discord_bot`/`spawn_slack_bot` for per-agent bots (global bots untouched); protected mode requires no open quarantine row, a live `read_lifecycle` and an active catalog route with provider `channel` for the lifecycle binding whose C6 detail names this employee id, this label, this channel and `token = agent`; any read failure refuses; outside protected mode unchanged. **Running bots (fix round)**: new `isolation_label_bots.rs` keeps the `AbortHandle` of every started per-agent bot under `(home, channel, exact label)` (`register`, from the three spawn functions); `tombstone_employee` calls `stop_label(home, label)` right after the apply.db commit (row disabled + quarantine row open; also on a retry; audit `channel_bots_stopped`), and the tombstone RPC additionally `hot_stop_channel`s `<channel>:<label>` (handle map + status). Race: a start registers and then re-runs the gate (`admit`; refused ⇒ aborted, `None`), and a gate refusal at start also stops any older bot of that label/channel (`refused`). **Restore / purge (fix round)**: new `isolation_label_restore.rs`. Config stores may have a `hold(home, label, key)` (registry field `Backend::Config.hold`): `website_credentials` (`website_hold`: held grants, entries `{id, credential, agent, key}`) and `delegation_targets` (`delegation_hold`: re-root, entries `{agent, parent, department}`; a failed write stops, keeps what moved recorded and raises `stores:delegation_targets:strip_unavailable`). `tombstone_effects` writes the entries to `stripped.restore.<store>` (merged without duplicates on retry; website entries are re-derived from the store's holds, so a failed row write is repaired by the retry); the audit gets counts only (`restorable`). `rekey` uses `hold` (website grants held under the row key; purged with it). **For F6c:** after the restore commit call `restore_label_stores(home, label, &row.stripped) -> RestoreReport { regranted, reapplied, skipped, listed }`: website grants held under the recorded key on the recorded id are moved back (`Missing` ⇒ `skipped …:not_held`); a `reports_to` edge is re-applied only if the label is in the org, the child exists and still reports to the root (department: the current one is kept); else `skipped` with `parent_missing`/`child_missing`/`reparented`/`refused` (cycle); entries for another label ⇒ `label_mismatch`; channel routes, `deferred`, `in_tree` and paused work are only `listed` (§3.3 never automatic, §3.7 work stays paused; channel bots need a new `connector_provision`). Unreadable store ⇒ `Err`, idempotent retry. **Spec divergence to confirm (owner):** design §3.3 says website grants and other employees' delegation targets are "listed for admin re-grant / re-enable (never automatic)"; this fix round re-applies them automatically per the task owner's instruction (2026-11-09) — the §3.3 table should be amended, or F6c should skip `restore_label_stores` for those two stores. **For F6i:** `purge_row_stores(home, employee, incarnation)` = `purge_all` under the row key: held website grants deleted for good, a credential left with no grant deleted; `purge_all(home, label)` never touches a row's holds. **R7**: `check_label_not_quarantined(home, label, adopting)` refuses `Quarantined` while any row on the label is not closed (S7: `purged`/`adopted`/`restored` are closed), except the row `adopting` names (F6g passes it; everyone else `None`); a label with a `purged` row and no live confirmed row additionally needs a positive zero count from every non-Digest store (the propose flow writes the new org entry before staging). Called in `prepare_propose` and `stage_prepared` (`isolation_employee_rpc.rs`, `proposal_refusal` → `LABEL_QUARANTINED`) and in `EmployeeAuthority::validate_current` (`migration_authority_employee.rs`, so approval validation, stage/Allocate and publish/read-back refuse `Denied`; unreadable → `Storage`). **For F6i:** call `purge_all(home, label)` (and the label-free check `label_free`) inside the purge flow after the tree removal; the row's sealed stores live under `quarantine_key(..)`, purge those with `purge_all(home, &quarantine_key(..))`. **For F6g:** call `rekey_all(home, label, &quarantine_key(employee, incarnation))` before the adoption commit and in `adopting` recovery, then require `label_free(home, label) == Ok(true)`; pass `Some((employee, incarnation))` to `check_label_not_quarantined` for the adoption proposal (and add the `adopt_quarantine` field to `validate_current`'s allowlist). **For F6c:** compare the restore-time counts with `store_marks` (`STORES_CHANGED`) and refuse while a `stores:` fence is pending. Open: (1) unmodelled §3.3 global routes: webhook/automation routes, global heartbeat definitions and channel-settings agent keys in sessions.db (`DEFERRED_ROUTES`); (2) `tasks.created_by`/`plan_steps.assignee` and causal `tenant_id` rows that equal the label are not counted (attribution or keyed by another column name; the memory UPDATE/DELETE triggers already blank causal artifacts of moved/deleted memories); ephemeral `eph-<label>-…` sub-agent namespaces are not gated; (3) memory tables with update-refusing triggers (e.g. `memory_connector_stamps`) make `rekey` of a label that has such rows fail closed, and FTS rows of `key_facts_fts` (no agent column) survive a purge; (4) a store marks entry is a row count, not a digest; (5) ~~running channel bots not torn down~~ fixed (see Running bots); bots of other kinds (Google Chat / webhook channels, global bots) are not per-employee tasks and are untouched; (6) if the quarantine row write fails after `delegation_hold` re-rooted edges, a retry cannot recover those edges (the org no longer points at the label): they appear in the tombstone audit names only (`recorded: false`); (7) the tombstone RPC test fixture has no live `MethodHandler` bot map, so the RPC-level `hot_stop_channel` is covered only by the registry test. Tests added (not compiled or run here; Linux builder): `label_keyed_stores_registry_complete`, `rekey_idempotent_per_store`, `label_free_requires_positive_zero_count`, `unreadable_store_is_not_zero`, `purge_removes_label_stores`, `memory_writer_refused_for_quarantined_label`, `session_writer_refused_for_quarantined_label`, `task_writer_refused_for_quarantined_label`, `cron_writer_refused_for_quarantined_label`, `writer_gate_fails_closed_when_lifecycle_unreadable`, `writer_gate_unchanged_outside_protected_mode`, `global_channel_route_to_label_paused`, `delegation_target_to_label_paused`, `proposal_on_quarantined_label_refused` (store level and, in `handlers/isolation_employee_rpc_f6h_tests.rs`, through `prepare_propose`/`stage_prepared`), `restored_row_counts_as_closed`, `channel_bot_needs_live_lifecycle_and_route`, `tombstone_strips_global_routes_and_gates_writers`; fix round: `tombstone_stops_running_label_bot` (lifecycle child), `tombstone_keeps_website_credentials_for_restore`, `restore_label_stores_regrants_credentials_and_edges`, `purge_deletes_quarantined_website_credentials`, and `session_writer_refused_for_quarantined_label` no longer calls `unwrap_err` on `Result<Session, _>` (`Session` has no `Debug` by design).
- From F6d (RPCs + production wiring, F6 design r5 §3.4–§3.8, §3.11, §5): **RPCs** (dashboard-only, `owns()` of `isolation_employee_rpc`, Enterprise-only in `gates.rs`, `user_id == "system"` refused, not MCP tools): handler child `handlers/isolation_employee_restore.rs` (gates + dispatch; refusals are `{code, message}` objects) over the blocking bodies in new `isolation_restore_rpc.rs`: `me.isolation.list_restorable` `{}` → `{restorable: [{employee_id, incarnation, label, kind, owner_user_id, seal, tombstoned_at, restore_deadline, restorable, root_state, root_flag: null|ROOT_GONE|ROOT_RETIRED|ROOT_INCONSISTENT, open_request: null|{request_id, state, requester_user_id, created_at, expires_at}}]}` (own Personal rows only, exact owner id); `me.isolation.restore_challenge` `{employee_id, incarnation}` (a cancel names its request's row) → the owner's **strongest** factor's challenge, bound to purpose `restore:<employee_id>:<incarnation>` and the user, single use, `expires_in` = 300 s: passkey `{factor: passkey, challenge_id, publicKey, expires_in}` (WebAuthn request options for `navigator.credentials.get`), email `{factor: email, challenge_id, sent: true, expires_in}` (code mailed by the email-factor sender, never in the reply), TOTP `{factor: totp, challenge_id: null}` (no challenge); refusals `NOT_FOUND` (not the caller's row, checked first), `FACTOR_NOT_ENROLLED`, `FACTOR_FAILED` (locked out), `FACTOR_RATE_LIMITED` (email budget: ≤ 5 per hour, ≥ 60 s apart, shared with login codes), `FACTOR_UNAVAILABLE` (no passkey origin configured, mail unavailable or delivery failed — never a weaker factor); `me.isolation.request_restore` `{employee_id, incarnation, factor: passkey|totp|email, code?, challenge_id?, credential?}` (TOTP: `code`; email: `challenge_id` + `code`; passkey: `challenge_id` + `credential` = the assertion JSON) → `{request_id, state, label, expires_at, approval_required, notified}`; `me.isolation.cancel_restore` `{request_id, factor, code?, challenge_id?, credential?}` → `{request_id, state}`; `isolation.employee_restore_decide` `{request_id, decision: approve|deny, csrf_token}` → deny `{request_id, state: denied}` / approve `{request_id, state: done, label, incarnation, self_approved, label_state: {regrant, skipped, listed}, work_paused, routes_need_provisioning}`; `isolation.employee_restore_cancel` `{request_id, csrf_token}`; `isolation.employee_restore_regrant` `{employee_id, incarnation, item, csrf_token}` → `{item, state: regranted|skipped, reason}`; `isolation.employee_retire_propose` `{employee_id, csrf_token}` → `{state: staged|root_absent|retired, proposal_id?, revision?, early?}`; `isolation.employee_list` gains `restores: {quarantine: [row as above], requests: [{request_id, kind, label, employee_id, incarnation, requester_user_id, state, created_at, expires_at, approver_user_id, self_approved, failure}]}`. Owner paths: ownership first (`NOT_FOUND` for every other row, no factor oracle), then the **strongest factor** (new `duduclaw-auth` `mfa/owner_factor.rs`: passkey > TOTP > email; a weaker one ⇒ `FACTOR_NOT_STRONGEST` before any counter or challenge is touched, never a fallback; the purpose is derived by the server from the row, never a request field; passkey = an assertion over the `restore_challenge` ceremony in `mfa_passkey_ceremonies` (purpose + binding = hash(purpose), no session, operator-configured origin `server_mfa_passkey::configured_origin` as relying party, credential epoch, 300 s, consumed before verification; verified by the passkey login's own `verify_assertion` with UV required and the live signature-counter check/update); email = a six-digit code in `mfa_restore_email_codes` (hash over challenge id + purpose + code, current verified address, epoch, 300 s, consumed by any presentation, one live code per purpose); a wrong TOTP goes through `check_factor` and a failed passkey/email proof is counted the same way, i.e. the login lockout counters `mfa_credentials.failed_attempts` / `mfa_factor_failures`, committed and audited `isolation.restore_factor_failed`), then F6c's `file_personal_request`. Admin paths: Admin + current MFA + migration step-up with its CSRF; decide approve runs F6c `approve` with `LogRule { fresh_step_up: true }` (F6c2 `EligibilityLogRule` on the users.db transaction F6c opens inside its apply.db tx; `self_approved` persisted by F6c; a self-approval writes F6c2's users.db row `isolation.employee_restore_self_approved` via `record_self_approval` exactly once, in that same users.db write transaction (`restore_users`), never in tx1's read-only check; a failed write refuses the apply `SELF_APPROVAL_AUDIT_UNAVAILABLE`); the request/cancel paths pass `fresh_step_up: false`. F6l's early-purged retire card (`RetireLogRule`) writes the same row once per proposal at the stage step, deciding again in its own users.db IMMEDIATE transaction before the root retire (a retried stage finds the row and writes nothing). **Tombstone** accepts Shared (`NOT_PERSONAL` dropped; unknown label ⇒ `EMPLOYEE_NOT_FOUND`). **`RESTORE_IN_PROGRESS`** (`isolation_restore_rpc::refuse_while_restoring`, read on the caller's own apply.db connection, never writes schema) in tombstone, `prepare_propose`, `stage_prepared`, regrant and retire propose (F6l's own R11 check also refuses). **Wiring** (new `isolation_restore_wiring.rs`): `duduclaw-execution` `spawnd::employee_control::{employee_idle, stop_employee_runs}` (own authenticated control connection per call; `transport::work_request`/`validate_response` accept the two ops via `check_work_reply`); `SpawndRootProbe::employee_idle` and `SpawndRunStopper` call them (transport error ⇒ `IsolationUnavailable` / unconfirmed fence, never gone/idle; root `NotFound` ⇒ `RootError::NotFound` ⇒ precheck `Inconsistent`); `RegistryMarks` (F6c `LabelStores`: the Gate/Pause counts `tombstone_effects` records) and `RegistryCounts` (F6l `LabelStores`: sum over every store); `RetireLogRule` (F6l `ApprovalRule`, `EarlyPurgedRetire`); `OutboxNotifier` (F6l `RetireNotifier`) and `queue_notice` (apply.db `employee_restore_notices`; L1 cap ≤ 5 capped notices per owner/tenant per rolling day for request/cancel/deny, suppression audited; approval/restore/self-approval/retire notices never dropped; recipients = log-eligible Admin addresses (S16) + tombstone-time address). `handlers/migration_authority_retire.rs`: `CompositeBackend` (one backend per category, `Denied` when none or two) + `production_backend`, used by `migration_confirmations_rpc` list/approve/retry/apply. **Label-keyed state (§3.3/§3.7)**: `isolation_label_restore::restore_label_stores` now **only lists** (`regrant` = `website_credentials:<id>` / `reports_to:<child>`, held grants stay held; `listed` = channel routes, deferred, in-tree, paused work); new `regrant_item` re-applies one exactly-listed entry (Admin RPC above, audited `isolation.employee_restore_regranted`; only while the restored employee is live at incarnation + 1). **Owner credential stamps (Q3)**: `stamp_owner` inside the writer's own transaction in `mfa_confirm_and_issue`, `mfa_change_and_issue`, `mfa_passkey_register_finish`, `mfa_passkey_delete`, `mfa_email_verify_and_issue` (enrolment), `mfa_email_remove_and_issue`, and new `UserDb::change_own_password` / `change_own_email`, which `users.change_password` / `users.change_email` (always `ctx.user_id`) now call; admin resets stay unattributed (refuse a restore). **Lock order (M6, S17)**: the factor proof commits its users.db tx before any apply.db lock; F6c's approve/request take users.db only inside or after their apply.db tx. **Deviations**: SSO-only owners without MFA (Q2 fresh Google re-auth) are refused `FACTOR_NOT_ENROLLED`; `isolation.quarantine_purge` / `retire` tick and the Shared restore request RPC are not here (F6i/F6k); the F6g adoption request RPCs were added at the F6g merge (see the F6g merge bullet below); notices are queued, not delivered (no mailer wired); provider-cache eviction (L3) after restore not done (registry rescan only); F6l's HostBusy retry/backoff and boot recovery of `retiring` not wired. F6c fixture fix: `tombstone_with` now overwrites the store marks F6h records at tombstone. Tests (not compiled or run here; Linux builder): auth `mfa::owner_factor::tests::{request_requires_strongest_factor, factor_failures_count_toward_lockout, owner_self_credential_change_is_stamped, restore_purpose_is_canonical, restore_request_accepts_passkey_assertion_bound_to_purpose, restore_request_accepts_email_code_bound_to_purpose, restore_challenge_single_use_and_expires, restore_never_accepts_weaker_factor}` (passkey tests drive a signed ceremony through `mfa::passkey::ceremony_tests::{Authenticator, register}`); gateway `isolation_lifecycle_tests::restore::self_approval_row_written_once_in_apply_tx`, `isolation_lifecycle_tests::f6d::restore_challenge_only_by_owner_and_bound_to_row`, `handlers::migration_authority::retire::tests::retire_early_purged_row_needs_second_admin` (now also: the users.db row requested once, at stage); gateway `isolation_lifecycle_tests::f6d::{request_only_by_owner, admin_cannot_file_personal_request, request_requires_strongest_factor, request_rate_limits, notice_caps, request_expires, cancel_by_requester_or_admin, regrant_requires_admin_step_up_and_audits, lock_order_apply_db_before_users_db}`, `handlers::isolation_employee_rpc::restore::tests::{restore_rpcs_not_listed_to_ai, decide_requires_admin_step_up, regrant_requires_admin_step_up_and_audits_gate, owner_request_schema_has_no_owner_field}`, `handlers::isolation_employee_rpc::tests::tombstone_accepts_shared`, `isolation_label_stores_tests::restore_lists_label_state_for_admin_regrant_never_auto` (replaces `restore_label_stores_regrants_credentials_and_edges`), `isolation_restore_wiring::tests::{log_rule_requires_step_up_and_transaction, retire_rule_fails_closed, registry_marks_match_tombstone_shape, spawnd_calls_fail_closed_without_root}`. **F6fa fix round (branch `fix/iso181-f6fa-20261109`, review NO-GO items 1, 5, 7, 8):** (1, High) the strongest-factor proof no longer reads the live account: the tombstone records `principal_snapshot.owner_factors` = `owner_factor::FactorBaseline` (`version` 1, `strongest`, `passkeys` = `mfa_passkeys.id` list, `totp` = digest of the stored TOTP ciphertext, `email` = digest of the email-factor address for the current account address; never a secret). `UserDb::restore_challenge` / `restore_owner_factor` take the baseline: the required factor is the baseline's strongest and must still be the credential recorded then (passkey: the ceremony's `allowCredentials` and the assertion check — `purpose_begin`/`purpose_finish` → `verify_assertion(.., allowed)` before the counter update — accept only recorded ids still present; TOTP: same secret digest, so a re-enrolment or swap refuses; email: same address digest); a factor stronger than the baseline's (enrolled afterwards, e.g. by someone with the owner's session + password, whose writes are stamped owner-authenticated) or a changed credential refuses `FACTOR_NOT_PREEXISTING`, a weaker one `FACTOR_NOT_STRONGEST`, both before any counter or challenge; a snapshot without the baseline (tombstoned before F6fa) refuses `FACTOR_BASELINE_MISSING` — no fallback. The production check and challenge issuer (`handlers/isolation_employee_restore.rs`, restore, cancel and adoption paths) read the baseline through new `isolation_restore_rpc::factor_baseline(home, caller, purpose)` (caller's own row, canonical `restore:`/`adopt:` purpose only, read before the factor's users.db tx). Consequences (owner decisions kept): the owner's own password change stays allowed; an owner's own email change makes an email-only owner's factor unusable (address changed); a password-only owner at the tombstone cannot restore (no factor then) — amended by owner decision D2 (2026-11-09, F6fa2 below): an Admin files for such an owner. (5) the self-approval notice also goes to the tombstone-time `principal_snapshot.notice_address`; `admin_notice_addresses(home, label, event, now)` audits Critical `isolation.employee_restore_notice_recipients_unavailable` when the eligibility log is unreadable; new `queue_or_alert` audits Critical `isolation.employee_restore_notice_unqueued` for an uncapped notice the outbox refuses (used by `notify`, the self-approval notice and `OutboxNotifier`). (7) verified: the migration step-up proof is short-lived (`mfa::TTL` = 300 s, checked in `migration_confirmation_session` and `ApprovalSession::authorize`) and bound to user + session hash + credential epoch + current Admin + enrolled TOTP + session CSRF, but **not single-use** (reusable within the window by the same session, as for confirmation cards) — left as is, owner may require per-decision step-up. Decide now computes `fresh_step_up` from the proof's `verified_at` (`wiring::step_up_fresh`); `RetireLogRule` gets `step_up_verified_at` from the confirmation RPC (`production_backend(.., step_up_verified_at)`: `Some(proof.verified_at)` on approve/retry/reject, the listing's optional proof otherwise — an early-purged retire card lists as not applicable until the Admin steps up) and refuses `STEP_UP_REQUIRED` (new retire `ApprovalRefusal`) without a fresh one; the retire trait's `record_self_approval` became `confirm_at_apply`: at the stage step every early-purged approval (self or another Admin) is decided again inside an apply.db IMMEDIATE tx that re-runs R11, with the rule's users.db IMMEDIATE tx inside it (M6), and must equal the validation's answer before the root retire. (8) verified: AI employees reach the gateway only through MCP tools implemented by the CLI (no tool forwards a dashboard RPC method, no `/api/internal/*` route dispatches one); no MCP tool, external-whitelist entry or tool-guide entry names `me.isolation.*`, `isolation.employee_restore_*`, `users.change_password` or `users.change_email`; the `system` identity is refused by every restore RPC and has no account for the credential writers; the on-behalf-of context only attributes MCP/bus dispatch. Tests (`f6fa_`; not compiled or run here): auth `mfa::owner_factor::tests::{f6fa_newly_enrolled_passkey_refused_for_restore, f6fa_reenrolled_totp_refused, f6fa_preexisting_factor_accepted, f6fa_legacy_snapshot_without_factor_ids_refused}` (existing owner-factor tests now pass a baseline; `ceremony_tests::{Authenticator::with_id, register_key}` added); gateway `isolation_restore_rpc_tests::{f6fa_legacy_snapshot_without_factor_ids_refused, f6fa_self_approval_notice_reaches_tombstone_address, f6fa_notice_failures_audited_critical}`, `handlers::migration_authority::retire::tests::{f6fa_retire_non_self_approval_decided_in_apply_tx, f6fa_retire_rule_uses_real_step_up}`, `handlers::isolation_employee_rpc::restore::tests::f6fa_restore_and_credential_rpcs_unreachable_from_ai`. **F6fa2 — owner decisions 2026-11-09 (same branch; F6 design §2 D1/D2, §3.4, §3.11):** **D1 "log out all devices" does not block restore**: `isolation_restore_checks::SNAPSHOT_LOG_FIELDS` no longer compares `credential_epoch` (now `email` → `email`, `factors_digest` → `totp`/`passkey`/`email_factor`); a session revocation (`remember_revoke`, `remember_admin_revoke_all`) writes no `credential_changes` row and changes no compared field, so it passes Q3 at request, tx1 and the apply transaction; password, email and factor changes by anyone but the owner still refuse `CREDENTIAL_CHANGED` (log rows after `credential_seq`), a changed Google `sub` too; the `FactorBaseline` holds factor identities only (never the epoch); the epoch stays in the snapshot, informational. **D2 Admin files for an owner without a second factor**: new RPCs (dashboard-only, `owns()`, Enterprise-only in `gates.rs`, `system` refused, Admin + current MFA at the gate, not MCP tools) `isolation.employee_restore_request_for_owner` `{employee_id, incarnation, csrf_token}` and `isolation.adoption_request_for_owner` `{employee_id, incarnation, target_label?, csrf_token}` → `{request_id, kind: personal_restore|personal_adopt, state: requested, label, target_label (adoption), owner_user_id, expires_at, approval_required: true, second_admin_required: true, filed_by_admin, owner_unverified: true, notified}`; engine `isolation_restore::file_personal_request_for_owner` / `isolation_adopt::Filer::AdminForOwner` over `isolation_restore_checks::check_admin_filing`: fresh step-up (`wiring::step_up_fresh` of the call's proof, else `STEP_UP_REQUIRED`), filer ≠ owner (`FILER_IS_OWNER`), tombstone baseline present (`SNAPSHOT_INCOMPLETE`) with `strongest = none` (`OWNER_HAD_SECOND_FACTOR`, never a fallback), then F6c2 `admin_approval_rule::check_admin_filing` in a users.db read tx: filer eligible (`FILER_NOT_ELIGIBLE`) and a third qualifying Admin exists (`NO_SECOND_ADMIN`); then every owner-filed check unchanged (owner eligible, Q3, 90 days, root, routes, stores, quota, L1). `employee_restores` gains `filed_by_admin TEXT` + `owner_unverified INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` (in-place ALTER in `ensure_restore_schema`; decode refuses a row where they disagree or the filer is not the requester); `request_digest` binds filer, requester and flag (owner-filed digests unchanged). Approval: `isolation_restore::decide_request` (restore tx1 and apply tx) and `isolation_adopt::decide` (proposal card, every validate) call F6c2 `decide_admin_filed` — filer and owner are parties, a party approver gets `SECOND_ADMIN_REQUIRED` while a third qualifying Admin exists, else `NO_SECOND_ADMIN`; never `self_approved`, no `record_self_approval`; restore approve and adoption validate re-check `admin_may_file_for_owner` on the row. New trait methods (fail closed by default): gateway `ApprovalRule::{decide_admin_filed, check_admin_filing}` (`LogRule` implements both; `ApprovalRuleUnavailable` refuses), auth `AdminApprovalRule::{decide_admin_filed, check_admin_filing}` (default = the real functions); new `ApprovalRefusal::{NoSecondAdmin, FilerNotEligible}`. Notices: the filing notice `isolation.employee_restore_requested_for_owner` / `isolation.employee_adoption_requested_for_owner` is **uncapped** to the log-eligible Admins + the tombstone-time `notice_address`; the approval notices (`isolation.employee_restored`, `isolation.employee_adopted`, already uncapped and to that address) carry `filed_by_admin`/`owner_unverified`; audits `isolation.employee_restore_requested_for_owner` / `isolation.employee_adoption_requested_for_owner` (Warning). Views: `me.isolation.list_restorable` and `isolation.employee_list` `restores.quarantine[]` gain `admin_may_file_for_owner` and `open_request.{kind, filed_by_admin, owner_unverified}`; `restores.requests[]` gain `owner_user_id, filed_by_admin, owner_unverified`. Not done: the owner cannot cancel an Admin-filed request through `me.isolation.cancel_restore` (no factor to prove; Admins cancel with `isolation.employee_restore_cancel`); the F6e UI; adoption still accepts `google_reauth` as an owner factor name (unlike restore since F6fa item 4, untouched here). Tests (`f6fa2_`; not compiled or run here): auth `admin_approval_rule::tests::{f6fa2_admin_filed_request_needs_different_admin, f6fa2_admin_filed_request_has_no_sole_admin_path}`, `mfa::owner_factor::tests::f6fa2_session_revoke_keeps_factor_baseline`; gateway `isolation_lifecycle_tests::restore::{f6fa2_session_revoke_after_tombstone_allows_restore, f6fa2_admin_password_reset_still_refuses, f6fa2_admin_filed_request_engine_rule}`, `isolation_lifecycle_tests::f6d::{f6fa2_admin_files_for_owner_without_second_factor, f6fa2_admin_cannot_file_when_owner_had_second_factor, f6fa2_admin_filed_request_needs_different_admin, f6fa2_admin_filed_request_notifies_tombstone_address, f6fa2_admin_filed_flag_in_views}`, `isolation_lifecycle_tests::restore::adopt::f6d::f6fa2_admin_files_adoption_for_owner_without_second_factor`, `handlers::isolation_employee_rpc::restore::tests::f6fa2_admin_filing_rpcs_gated` (and `restore_rpcs_not_listed_to_ai` / the edition gate table cover both new methods). **Security round 3 (F6 part a, 2026-11-09; N3, N4):** (N3) the owner can now cancel an Admin-filed request (replaces the F6fa2 "Not done" item above): `me.isolation.cancel_restore` `{request_id, password}` (no factor fields; mixing them is `INVALID_REQUEST`) → `isolation_restore_rpc::cancel_own_unverified`: only the request's owner, only an `owner_unverified` request filed by another account (anything else `NOT_FOUND`, no re-auth asked); re-auth = handler `owner_password_reauth` (account active at the session's credential epoch, no forced password change, then `UserDb::verify_password`); a failed re-auth refuses (`FACTOR_FAILED` / `FACTOR_DENIED`) and is audited `isolation.employee_restore_owner_cancel_reauth_failed` (Warning); success closes the request (`isolation.employee_restore_cancelled`), audits `isolation.employee_restore_owner_cancelled_unverified` (Warning) and sends the cancel notice uncapped. No password lockout of its own (it relies on the current session). (N4) `FactorBaseline` version 2: the TOTP identity is a domain-separated digest of the **decrypted** secret (`duduclaw-owner-factor-v2:totp-secret:…`, decrypted with the machine cipher and bound to its user by `mfa::decrypt`; an undecryptable secret gets a `totp-undecryptable` digest of its ciphertext — still TOTP-enrolled, never equal to a decrypted digest), compared in constant time (`subtle`), so a re-encryption / machine-key rotation keeps a genuine owner's baseline. Version-1 baselines (recorded before: digest of `secret_enc`) still parse and are compared the version-1 way (a rotation still breaks those; no migration). API: `FactorBaseline::read(conn, user, cipher)` and `UserDb::restore_challenge(.., baseline, cipher, origin, ..)` take the cipher; the tombstone snapshot passes `mfa::Cipher(home)`. Tests (`f6r3_`; not compiled or run here): auth `mfa::owner_factor::tests::f6r3_totp_baseline_survives_reencryption` (the legacy-snapshot mutation test now rejects version 3); gateway `isolation_lifecycle_tests::restore::adopt::f6r3::f6r3_owner_cancels_admin_filed_request_with_password`, `handlers::isolation_employee_rpc::restore::tests::f6r3_owner_cancel_password_reauth`.
- From F6h (F6 design r5 §3.3, §3.10 step 7, §3.12; H4, H5, R7, S7, S9, S10): new `duduclaw-gateway/src/isolation_label_stores.rs` (+ `_tests.rs`, and `isolation_label_stores_lifecycle_tests.rs` as a child of the lifecycle tests for the fixtures). **Registry** `LABEL_KEYED_STORES` (S9): `org_entry` (org.toml, action Digest), `memory` (memory.db), `sessions` (sessions.db), `tasks` (tasks.db), `cron` (cron_tasks.db, action Pause), `website_credentials` (`secrets/website_credentials.enc`, Strip), `channel_routes` (`agent_bindings.json` channel-user bindings and bind tokens, Strip), `delegation_targets` (org.toml rows whose `reports_to` is the label, Strip: re-rooted, department kept). Each entry: `count_under` (missing store = positive 0, unreadable = `Err`, never zero), idempotent `rekey(old, new)` and `purge(label)`; `label_free`, `rekey_all`, `purge_all`, `counts_under`, `quarantine_key(employee, incarnation)` = `quarantine:<hex>-<inc>`. SQLite stores discover their label columns by exact name (`agent_id`, `assigned_to`, `owner_agent_id`, `agent`, `agent_name`, `agent_label`) in every table on every call, so later tables are covered without editing the registry; purge first deletes child rows by `session_id`/`task_id`/`cron_id` of the label's parent rows (append-only tables whose triggers refuse deletes, e.g. `task_authority_epochs`, are kept), all in one IMMEDIATE tx per store. Config maps cannot be sealed under the row, so `purge` detaches the label and `rekey` detaches it too, except stores with a `hold` (website grants are held under the row key; see Restore / purge below); pointing routes at a new id stays an explicit admin edit. `[channels.*]` and `[capabilities] db_sources` are in the tree (F6a strip, `IN_TREE_STRIPPED`). **Writer gate** (H4): `check_label_writable(home, label)` refuses while an `employee_quarantine` row on the exact label is not closed (`LABEL_QUARANTINED`) or the confirmed row is disabled; an unreadable/unsafe/future-schema apply.db refuses (`LABEL_STATE_UNAVAILABLE`, fail closed); no apply.db or no row = allowed. Active only when `protected_active()` (= `runtime_readiness::is_protected_linux()`; tests force it per thread), so unprotected installs do no extra I/O. Call sites: sessions `session_origin/store.rs::create_inner` and `session.rs::append_delivery_message` (session's agent), tasks `task_store/tasks.rs::insert_task_tx` (every task insert), cron `cron_store.rs::insert` and `edit_scoped`, memory via a new process-wide gate in `duduclaw-memory` (`label_gate.rs`, `install_label_write_gate(fn)`; called in `SqliteMemoryEngine::store`, `store_temporal_outcome_checked`, `store_fact_outcome`; the gateway installs `memory_write_gate` at boot in `server.rs`; `agent:<label>` namespaces are the same label, exact prefix token). The gate derives the home from the store connection's database file (`Connection::path`), so an in-memory/unknown file refuses in protected mode. **Tombstone hook** `tombstone_effects` (§3.2 step 4), called by `isolation_lifecycle::tombstone_employee` after the run/route fences: Strip stores detached with names merged into `stripped.global_routes.<store>`, cron rows of the label set `enabled = 0` (`last_error = employee_tombstoned`, ids in `stripped.paused_work.cron`), Gate/Pause stores counted into `employee_quarantine.store_marks` (first readable mark kept on retry), `stripped.deferred` now lists the unmodelled §3.3 routes, failures become `stores:` pending fences (block restore until a retry clears them); summary in the tombstone audit (`label_stores`). New hooks: `agent_binding::{label_route_count, strip_label_routes}` (under `with_file_lock`, never creates the file, names `binding:<channel>:<user>` / `token:<channel>`), `website_credentials::{agent_grant_count, hold_agent, regrant_held, purge_agent}` (fix round: **nothing is deleted at tombstone** — the label's grant on each record moves to a new `Metadata.held_grants: [{agent, key = quarantine:<hex>-<inc>}]` (serde default, omitted when empty), a held grant is never usable (`for_agent`/connectors read only `allowed_agents`), a held-only record is valid (`allowed_agents` may be empty only while a grant is held), `delete` refuses a held record, an admin `upsert` keeps the holds; `count_under(key)` counts holds, `count_under(label)` does not, so adoption's label-free check is unaffected). **Bot gate** (H5, A11): `channel_bot_allowed(home, label, channel)` in `spawn_telegram_bot`/`spawn_discord_bot`/`spawn_slack_bot` for per-agent bots (global bots untouched); protected mode requires no open quarantine row, a live `read_lifecycle` and an active catalog route with provider `channel` for the lifecycle binding whose C6 detail names this employee id, this label, this channel and `token = agent`; any read failure refuses; outside protected mode unchanged. **Running bots (fix round)**: new `isolation_label_bots.rs` keeps the `AbortHandle` of every started per-agent bot under `(home, channel, exact label)` (`register`, from the three spawn functions); `tombstone_employee` calls `stop_label(home, label)` right after the apply.db commit (row disabled + quarantine row open; also on a retry; audit `channel_bots_stopped`), and the tombstone RPC additionally `hot_stop_channel`s `<channel>:<label>` (handle map + status). Race: a start registers and then re-runs the gate (`admit`; refused ⇒ aborted, `None`), and a gate refusal at start also stops any older bot of that label/channel (`refused`). **Restore / purge (fix round)**: new `isolation_label_restore.rs`. Config stores may have a `hold(home, label, key)` (registry field `Backend::Config.hold`): `website_credentials` (`website_hold`: held grants, entries `{id, credential, agent, key}`) and `delegation_targets` (`delegation_hold`: re-root, entries `{agent, parent, department}`; a failed write stops, keeps what moved recorded and raises `stores:delegation_targets:strip_unavailable`). `tombstone_effects` writes the entries to `stripped.restore.<store>` (merged without duplicates on retry; website entries are re-derived from the store's holds, so a failed row write is repaired by the retry); the audit gets counts only (`restorable`). `rekey` uses `hold` (website grants held under the row key; purged with it). **For F6c:** after the restore commit call `restore_label_stores(home, label, &row.stripped) -> RestoreReport { regranted, reapplied, skipped, listed }`: website grants held under the recorded key on the recorded id are moved back (`Missing` ⇒ `skipped …:not_held`); a `reports_to` edge is re-applied only if the label is in the org, the child exists and still reports to the root (department: the current one is kept); else `skipped` with `parent_missing`/`child_missing`/`reparented`/`refused` (cycle); entries for another label ⇒ `label_mismatch`; channel routes, `deferred`, `in_tree` and paused work are only `listed` (§3.3 never automatic, §3.7 work stays paused; channel bots need a new `connector_provision`). Unreadable store ⇒ `Err`, idempotent retry. **Spec divergence to confirm (owner):** design §3.3 says website grants and other employees' delegation targets are "listed for admin re-grant / re-enable (never automatic)"; this fix round re-applies them automatically per the task owner's instruction (2026-11-09) — the §3.3 table should be amended, or F6c should skip `restore_label_stores` for those two stores. **For F6i:** `purge_row_stores(home, employee, incarnation)` = `purge_all` under the row key: held website grants deleted for good, a credential left with no grant deleted; `purge_all(home, label)` never touches a row's holds. **R7**: `check_label_not_quarantined(home, label, adopting)` refuses `Quarantined` while any row on the label is not closed (S7: `purged`/`adopted`/`restored` are closed), except the row `adopting` names (F6g passes it; everyone else `None`); a label with a `purged` row and no live confirmed row additionally needs a positive zero count from every non-Digest store (the propose flow writes the new org entry before staging). Called in `prepare_propose` and `stage_prepared` (`isolation_employee_rpc.rs`, `proposal_refusal` → `LABEL_QUARANTINED`) and in `EmployeeAuthority::validate_current` (`migration_authority_employee.rs`, so approval validation, stage/Allocate and publish/read-back refuse `Denied`; unreadable → `Storage`). **For F6i:** call `purge_all(home, label)` (and the label-free check `label_free`) inside the purge flow after the tree removal; the row's sealed stores live under `quarantine_key(..)`, purge those with `purge_all(home, &quarantine_key(..))`. **For F6g:** call `rekey_all(home, label, &quarantine_key(employee, incarnation))` before the adoption commit and in `adopting` recovery, then require `label_free(home, label) == Ok(true)`; pass `Some((employee, incarnation))` to `check_label_not_quarantined` for the adoption proposal (and add the `adopt_quarantine` field to `validate_current`'s allowlist). **For F6c:** compare the restore-time counts with `store_marks` (`STORES_CHANGED`) and refuse while a `stores:` fence is pending. Open: (1) unmodelled §3.3 global routes: webhook/automation routes, global heartbeat definitions and channel-settings agent keys in sessions.db (`DEFERRED_ROUTES`); (2) `tasks.created_by`/`plan_steps.assignee` and causal `tenant_id` rows that equal the label are not counted (attribution or keyed by another column name; the memory UPDATE/DELETE triggers already blank causal artifacts of moved/deleted memories); ephemeral `eph-<label>-…` sub-agent namespaces are not gated; (3) memory tables with update-refusing triggers (e.g. `memory_connector_stamps`) make `rekey` of a label that has such rows fail closed, and FTS rows of `key_facts_fts` (no agent column) survive a purge; (4) a store marks entry is a row count, not a digest; (5) ~~running channel bots not torn down~~ fixed (see Running bots); bots of other kinds (Google Chat / webhook channels, global bots) are not per-employee tasks and are untouched; (6) if the quarantine row write fails after `delegation_hold` re-rooted edges, a retry cannot recover those edges (the org no longer points at the label): they appear in the tombstone audit names only (`recorded: false`); (7) the tombstone RPC test fixture has no live `MethodHandler` bot map, so the RPC-level `hot_stop_channel` is covered only by the registry test. Tests added (not compiled or run here; Linux builder): `label_keyed_stores_registry_complete`, `rekey_idempotent_per_store`, `label_free_requires_positive_zero_count`, `unreadable_store_is_not_zero`, `purge_removes_label_stores`, `memory_writer_refused_for_quarantined_label`, `session_writer_refused_for_quarantined_label`, `task_writer_refused_for_quarantined_label`, `cron_writer_refused_for_quarantined_label`, `writer_gate_fails_closed_when_lifecycle_unreadable`, `writer_gate_unchanged_outside_protected_mode`, `global_channel_route_to_label_paused`, `delegation_target_to_label_paused`, `proposal_on_quarantined_label_refused` (store level and, in `handlers/isolation_employee_rpc_f6h_tests.rs`, through `prepare_propose`/`stage_prepared`), `restored_row_counts_as_closed`, `channel_bot_needs_live_lifecycle_and_route`, `tombstone_strips_global_routes_and_gates_writers`; fix round: `tombstone_stops_running_label_bot` (lifecycle child), `tombstone_keeps_website_credentials_for_restore`, `restore_label_stores_regrants_credentials_and_edges`, `purge_deletes_quarantined_website_credentials`, and `session_writer_refused_for_quarantined_label` no longer calls `unwrap_err` on `Result<Session, _>` (`Session` has no `Debug` by design). **Fix round F6fb (2026-11-09, review NO-GO items 1, 2, 3, 9)**: (a) **Registry**: new store `member_bindings` (`users.db` `user_agent_bindings` + the `isolation_personal_agents` mirror, new `TombstoneAction::Principal`): counted by every positive zero count (`label_free`, `check_label_not_quarantined`, adoption's `require_zero`, F6l `RegistryCounts`), never re-keyed (`rekey` is a no-op for it: a new id's own bindings may already be on the label during adoption recovery), no tombstone action (the tombstone's users.db step 5 removes it), `purge` = `remove_shared_bindings_in_transaction` (ACL bump; a Personal mirror refuses, it is only removed by the lifecycle's own paths). (b) **R7**: `check_label_not_quarantined` requires the positive zero count (non-Digest stores) whenever the label has **any** closed row (`LabelHold.closed`: purged, adopted, restored) and no live confirmed row — no longer only after a purge. (c) **Writer gate on every write** (H4): `duduclaw-memory` `label_gate::install_write_triggers` registers a per-connection SQL function `duduclaw_label_writable` and TEMP triggers on every table with a label column (`LABEL_COLUMNS` = `AGENT_COLUMNS`, a test keeps them equal) and, for a store with a registered parent, on every child table through the parent lookup: INSERT of a refused label `RAISE(ABORT, REFUSED)`; UPDATE whose old or new label is refused and that widens — sets a non-closing column to a new non-NULL value, or raises a lowering-only column — is skipped (`RAISE(IGNORE)`, so an all-agent sweep still runs for the others; nothing is re-keyed into or out of the label); narrowing updates keep working (memory `GateRules`: closing columns `valid_until`, `invalidated_by_event`, `invalidated_at`, `superseded_by`, `access_count`, `last_accessed`; lowering-only `origin_trust`, `confidence`; clearing any column to NULL); DELETE is not gated (cannot plant state, right-to-erasure must not be skipped; it changes the store marks, which a restore refuses), nor are memory's narrowing tables (`MEMORY_NARROWING_TABLES`: forget tombstones/plans/steps/receipts, fences, connector disconnect and CCR revocation records), whose inserts revoke rather than plant. Installed on the memory engine's connection (`SqliteMemoryEngine::new`/`in_memory`, rusqlite `functions` feature added to `duduclaw-memory`) and on every `SessionManager` pool connection (`isolation_label_stores::install_store_gate(conn, "sessions")`, parent `session_id → sessions.agent_id`); explicit refusals added to `record_webchat_failure`, `compress` (before anything is deleted), `set_pinned`, `set_title` and `migration_import::import_legacy_message`. The answer is cached per connection and label for 250 ms. Tasks and cron keep their insert-time gates only: their status / authority-revision updates of a tombstoned label's rows must keep working (a stop or revocation is a narrowing write). (d) **Isolated MCP** (`duduclaw-cli` `mcp_isolation.rs`), which opens the home's memory.db itself: installs `duduclaw_gateway::install_isolated_process_writer_gate()` before the engine opens — always active there (that process exists only in protected mode and need not inherit the env flag); an in-memory database is allowed. (e) **Bots** (`isolation_label_bots.rs`): each per-employee bot task runs in a `BotScope` (`scoped`, stopped when the bot future is dropped before completion, i.e. any abort); `spawn_scoped` / `inherit` track every task the bot starts — Discord message/interaction/guild/thread handlers, heartbeat, typing, progress, cleanup and guide tasks, Telegram callback-query and progress tasks, Slack progress tasks, and every `BoundedChannelExecutor` job (the job carries the submitter's scope) — and `stop`/`stop_label`/a refused `admit` also stop the scope, so no handler of a stopped bot can reply. Open: tables a store creates lazily after open (e.g. `memories_archive`, `legacy_import_messages`) carry no trigger (their writes move or copy rows of already-gated tables); the trigger gate's apply.db read runs inside the SQLite call (as the explicit checks did); the isolated MCP must run as the gateway uid to read apply.db (verify on the Linux box), else its memory writes fail closed. Tests (not compiled or run here): `f6fb_memory_every_label_write_gated`, `f6fb_session_every_label_write_gated` (both also assert, registry-based, that every label-keyed table and child table of the connection carries the gate triggers), `f6fb_zero_count_required_for_any_closed_row`, `isolation_label_bots::tests::{f6fb_stop_label_stops_per_update_handlers, f6fb_aborted_bot_task_stops_its_handlers}`; `rekey_idempotent_per_store`, `purge_removes_label_stores` and `label_keyed_stores_registry_complete` cover `member_bindings`.
- From F6h (F6 design r5 §3.3, §3.10 step 7, §3.12; H4, H5, R7, S7, S9, S10): new `duduclaw-gateway/src/isolation_label_stores.rs` (+ `_tests.rs`, and `isolation_label_stores_lifecycle_tests.rs` as a child of the lifecycle tests for the fixtures). **Registry** `LABEL_KEYED_STORES` (S9): `org_entry` (org.toml, action Digest), `memory` (memory.db), `sessions` (sessions.db), `tasks` (tasks.db), `cron` (cron_tasks.db, action Pause), `website_credentials` (`secrets/website_credentials.enc`, Strip), `channel_routes` (`agent_bindings.json` channel-user bindings and bind tokens, Strip), `delegation_targets` (org.toml rows whose `reports_to` is the label, Strip: re-rooted, department kept).
