Race: a start registers and then re-runs the gate (`admit`; refused ⇒ aborted, `None`), and a gate refusal at start also stops any older bot of that label/channel (`refused`). **Restore / purge (fix round)**: new `isolation_label_restore.rs`. Config stores may have a `hold(home, label, key)` (registry field `Backend::Config.hold`): `website_credentials` (`website_hold`: held grants, entries `{id, credential, agent, key}`) and `delegation_targets` (`delegation_hold`: re-root, entries `{agent, parent, department}`; a failed write stops, keeps what moved recorded and raises `stores:delegation_targets:strip_unavailable`). `tombstone_effects` writes the entries to `stripped.restore.<store>` (merged without duplicates on retry; website entries are re-derived from the store's holds, so a failed row write is repaired by the retry); the audit gets counts only (`restorable`). `rekey` uses `hold` (website grants held under the row key; purged with it). **For F6c:** after the restore commit call `restore_label_stores(home, label, &row.stripped) -> RestoreReport { regranted, reapplied, skipped, listed }`: website grants held under the recorded key on the recorded id are moved back (`Missing` ⇒ `skipped …:not_held`); a `reports_to` edge is re-applied only if the label is in the org, the child exists and still reports to the root (department: the current one is kept); else `skipped` with `parent_missing`/`child_missing`/`reparented`/`refused` (cycle); entries for another label ⇒ `label_mismatch`; channel routes, `deferred`, `in_tree` and paused work are only `listed` (§3.3 never automatic, §3.7 work stays paused; channel bots need a new `connector_provision`). Unreadable store ⇒ `Err`, idempotent retry. **Spec divergence to confirm (owner):** design §3.3 says website grants and other employees' delegation targets are "listed for admin re-grant / re-enable (never automatic)"; this fix round re-applies them automatically per the task owner's instruction (2026-11-09) — the §3.3 table should be amended, or F6c should skip `restore_label_stores` for those two stores. **For F6i:** `purge_row_stores(home, employee, incarnation)` = `purge_all` under the row key: held website grants deleted for good, a credential left with no grant deleted; `purge_all(home, label)` never touches a row's holds. **R7**: `check_label_not_quarantined(home, label, adopting)` refuses `Quarantined` while any row on the label is not closed (S7: `purged`/`adopted`/`restored` are closed), except the row `adopting` names (F6g passes it; everyone else `None`); a label with a `purged` row and no live confirmed row additionally needs a positive zero count from every non-Digest store (the propose flow writes the new org entry before staging). Called in `prepare_propose` and `stage_prepared` (`isolation_employee_rpc.rs`, `proposal_refusal` → `LABEL_QUARANTINED`) and in `EmployeeAuthority::validate_current` (`migration_authority_employee.rs`, so approval validation, stage/Allocate and publish/read-back refuse `Denied`; unreadable → `Storage`). **For F6i:** call `purge_all(home, label)` (and the label-free check `label_free`) inside the purge flow after the tree removal; the row's sealed stores live under `quarantine_key(..)`, purge those with `purge_all(home, &quarantine_key(..))`. **For F6g:** call `rekey_all(home, label, &quarantine_key(employee, incarnation))` before the adoption commit and in `adopting` recovery, then require `label_free(home, label) == Ok(true)`; pass `Some((employee, incarnation))` to `check_label_not_quarantined` for the adoption proposal (and add the `adopt_quarantine` field to `validate_current`'s allowlist). **For F6c:** compare the restore-time counts with `store_marks` (`STORES_CHANGED`) and refuse while a `stores:` fence is pending. Open: (1) unmodelled §3.3 global routes: webhook/automation routes, global heartbeat definitions and channel-settings agent keys in sessions.db (`DEFERRED_ROUTES`); (2) `tasks.created_by`/`plan_steps.assignee` and causal `tenant_id` rows that equal the label are not counted (attribution or keyed by another column name; the memory UPDATE/DELETE triggers already blank causal artifacts of moved/deleted memories); ephemeral `eph-<label>-…` sub-agent namespaces are not gated; (3) memory tables with update-refusing triggers (e.g. `memory_connector_stamps`) make `rekey` of a label that has such rows fail closed, and FTS rows of `key_facts_fts` (no agent column) survive a purge; (4) a store marks entry is a row count, not a digest; (5) ~~running channel bots not torn down~~ fixed (see Running bots); bots of other kinds (Google Chat / webhook channels, global bots) are not per-employee tasks and are untouched; (6) if the quarantine row write fails after `delegation_hold` re-rooted edges, a retry cannot recover those edges (the org no longer points at the label): they appear in the tombstone audit names only (`recorded: false`); (7) the tombstone RPC test fixture has no live `MethodHandler` bot map, so the RPC-level `hot_stop_channel` is covered only by the registry test. Tests added (not compiled or run here; Linux builder): `label_keyed_stores_registry_complete`, `rekey_idempotent_per_store`, `label_free_requires_positive_zero_count`, `unreadable_store_is_not_zero`, `purge_removes_label_stores`, `memory_writer_refused_for_quarantined_label`, `session_writer_refused_for_quarantined_label`, `task_writer_refused_for_quarantined_label`, `cron_writer_refused_for_quarantined_label`, `writer_gate_fails_closed_when_lifecycle_unreadable`, `writer_gate_unchanged_outside_protected_mode`, `global_channel_route_to_label_paused`, `delegation_target_to_label_paused`, `proposal_on_quarantined_label_refused` (store level and, in `handlers/isolation_employee_rpc_f6h_tests.rs`, through `prepare_propose`/`stage_prepared`), `restored_row_counts_as_closed`, `channel_bot_needs_live_lifecycle_and_route`, `tombstone_strips_global_routes_and_gates_writers`; fix round: `tombstone_stops_running_label_bot` (lifecycle child), `tombstone_keeps_website_credentials_for_restore`, `restore_label_stores_regrants_credentials_and_edges`, `purge_deletes_quarantined_website_credentials`, and `session_writer_refused_for_quarantined_label` no longer calls `unwrap_err` on `Result<Session, _>` (`Session` has no `Debug` by design). **Fix round F6fb (2026-11-09, review NO-GO items 1, 2, 3, 9)**: (a) **Registry**: new store `member_bindings` (`users.db` `user_agent_bindings` + the `isolation_personal_agents` mirror, new `TombstoneAction::Principal`): counted by every positive zero count (`label_free`, `check_label_not_quarantined`, adoption's `require_zero`, F6l `RegistryCounts`), never re-keyed (`rekey` is a no-op for it: a new id's own bindings may already be on the label during adoption recovery), no tombstone action (the tombstone's users.db step 5 removes it), `purge` = `remove_shared_bindings_in_transaction` (ACL bump; a Personal mirror refuses, it is only removed by the lifecycle's own paths). (b) **R7**: `check_label_not_quarantined` requires the positive zero count (non-Digest stores) whenever the label has **any** closed row (`LabelHold.closed`: purged, adopted, restored) and no live confirmed row — no longer only after a purge. (c) **Writer gate on every write** (H4): `duduclaw-memory` `label_gate::install_write_triggers` registers a per-connection SQL function `duduclaw_label_writable` and TEMP triggers on every table with a label column (`LABEL_COLUMNS` = `AGENT_COLUMNS`, a test keeps them equal) and, for a store with a registered parent, on every child table through the parent lookup: INSERT of a refused label `RAISE(ABORT, REFUSED)`; UPDATE whose old or new label is refused and that widens — sets a non-closing column to a new non-NULL value, or raises a lowering-only column — is skipped (`RAISE(IGNORE)`, so an all-agent sweep still runs for the others; nothing is re-keyed into or out of the label); narrowing updates keep working (memory `GateRules`: closing columns `valid_until`, `invalidated_by_event`, `invalidated_at`, `superseded_by`, `access_count`, `last_accessed`; lowering-only `origin_trust`, `confidence`; clearing any column to NULL); DELETE is not gated (cannot plant state, right-to-erasure must not be skipped; it changes the store marks, which a restore refuses), nor are memory's narrowing tables (`MEMORY_NARROWING_TABLES`: forget tombstones/plans/steps/receipts, fences, connector disconnect and CCR revocation records), whose inserts revoke rather than plant. Installed on the memory engine's connection (`SqliteMemoryEngine::new`/`in_memory`, rusqlite `functions` feature added to `duduclaw-memory`) and on every `SessionManager` pool connection (`isolation_label_stores::install_store_gate(conn, "sessions")`, parent `session_id → sessions.agent_id`); explicit refusals added to `record_webchat_failure`, `compress` (before anything is deleted), `set_pinned`, `set_title` and `migration_import::import_legacy_message`. The answer is cached per connection and label for 250 ms. Tasks and cron keep their insert-time gates only: their status / authority-revision updates of a tombstoned label's rows must keep working (a stop or revocation is a narrowing write). (d) **Isolated MCP** (`duduclaw-cli` `mcp_isolation.rs`), which opens the home's memory.db itself: installs `duduclaw_gateway::install_isolated_process_writer_gate()` before the engine opens — always active there (that process exists only in protected mode and need not inherit the env flag); an in-memory database is allowed. (e) **Bots** (`isolation_label_bots.rs`): each per-employee bot task runs in a `BotScope` (`scoped`, stopped when the bot future is dropped before completion, i.e. any abort); `spawn_scoped` / `inherit` track every task the bot starts — Discord message/interaction/guild/thread handlers, heartbeat, typing, progress, cleanup and guide tasks, Telegram callback-query and progress tasks, Slack progress tasks, and every `BoundedChannelExecutor` job (the job carries the submitter's scope) — and `stop`/`stop_label`/a refused `admit` also stop the scope, so no handler of a stopped bot can reply. Open: ~~tables a store creates lazily after open carry no trigger~~ fixed in F6fb2 (below); the trigger gate's apply.db read runs inside the SQLite call (as the explicit checks did); ~~the isolated MCP must run as the gateway uid to read apply.db~~ verified in F6fb2 (below). Tests (not compiled or run here): `f6fb_memory_every_label_write_gated`, `f6fb_session_every_label_write_gated` (both also assert, registry-based, that every label-keyed table and child table of the connection carries the gate triggers), `f6fb_zero_count_required_for_any_closed_row`, `isolation_label_bots::tests::{f6fb_stop_label_stops_per_update_handlers, f6fb_aborted_bot_task_stops_its_handlers}`; `rekey_idempotent_per_store`, `purge_removes_label_stores` and `label_keyed_stores_registry_complete` cover `member_bindings`. **Follow-up round F6fb2 (2026-11-09)**: (1) **GDPR erasure under the gate**: `gdpr_erase`'s erasure record (the INSERT into `memories` after its deletes) was refused by the INSERT trigger for a tombstoned/quarantined label. New crate-private scope `label_gate::narrowing_write(kind, label, write)`: a thread-local context around exactly one synchronous statement (the trigger's SQL function runs on the stepping thread), optionally limited to one label; inside it a refused label passes (never cached as writable), every admission is counted and logged once per scope (`tracing` target `duduclaw_memory::label_gate`, `kind`, `label`, `rows`). Kinds: `ErasureRecord` (gdpr, limited to the erased label) and `ArchiveMove` (the archive copy of a row deleted in the same transaction: `forget` limited to its label, decay any label, so the all-agent sweep never aborts). No row shape is trusted (a look-alike `source_event = 'gdpr_erasure'` insert is still refused) and no other writer can enter the scope. (2) **Lazily created tables**: `label_gate::refresh_on_engine(conn)` (keeps the gate function already registered on the connection, creates the missing triggers; idempotent) right after the lazy `CREATE TABLE memories_archive` in `forget` and in `run_decay` (inside the decay tx, rolled back with it); sessions.db `legacy_import_messages` re-runs `install_store_gate(conn, "sessions")` after its `CREATE` in `import_legacy_message` (its only writer; the gate is per connection, so other pool connections get it only when they create/write it). `install_write_triggers` is split into function registration + `create_triggers`. (3) **Cache invalidation**: new `label_gate::invalidate_cached_answers()` (process-wide epoch; each connection's 250 ms cache entries carry the epoch), called by `isolation_quarantine::insert_quarantine` and after the tombstone's apply.db commit, so a write right after a tombstone cannot reuse a "writable" answer read before it (this also made `f6fb_memory_every_label_write_gated` order-dependent). (4) **FTS in step with base rows**: `reassign_agent` (in-engine re-key, engine connection with triggers) re-keys a `memories_fts` row only when its base row now carries the new label (`memory_id IN (SELECT id FROM memories WHERE agent_id = new)`), so rows the gate skipped keep their FTS row and search under either label never sees the other's content; the store purge (`sqlite::purge`, `FTS_COMPANIONS`) deletes `key_facts_fts` (rowid-linked, no label column — open item 3 above, fixed) and `memories_fts` rows of the label's base rows first, in the same IMMEDIATE tx. The gateway re-key needs no change (its own connection has no triggers; `memories_fts.agent_id` is a keyed column and moves with the base rows). (5) **Isolated MCP uid (verified, no capability change needed)**: `IsolationDispatcher` is not a separate process — `cmd_run_server` installs `CliDispatcherFactory` into the gateway, the protected boot calls `create` (`isolation_mcp/boot.rs`) and `McpBoundaryService::attach` serves it on a gateway task; the employee runtime only holds the socket end delivered by spawnd's `TakeIo`. So the gate runs as the gateway uid that owns `migration-authority/apply.db` (0600). The boot gate `memory_write_gate` (`server.rs`) is installed first, so `install_isolated_process_writer_gate` returned `false` and its "always active" gate was never used: it now also sets `ISOLATED_DISPATCHER`, after which `memory_write_gate` is active regardless of the protected-mode env flag. Not done: cross-database hand-off (`reassign_agent_cross_db`, ATTACHed destination) is not gated (inserts into `hdst.*` and deletes are untriggered; FTS stays consistent); an engine opened on a per-agent `agents/<id>/memory.db` derives the home from the file's parent and so finds no apply.db (allowed) — pre-existing, only home `memory.db` is gated. Tests (not compiled or run here): memory `label_gate::tests::{f6fb2_gdpr_erase_of_quarantined_label_succeeds, f6fb2_narrowing_scope_is_one_statement_and_one_label, f6fb2_lazy_archive_gated_and_archive_moves_admitted}` (refusing gate installed on the engine connection only); gateway `isolation_label_stores_tests::f6fb2::{f6fb2_gdpr_erase_of_quarantined_label_succeeds, f6fb2_lazily_created_tables_gated, f6fb2_rekey_keeps_fts_in_step_with_skipped_rows, f6fb2_purge_removes_fts_rows_with_base_rows}` (real gate, quarantine row in apply.db).
- From F6g (adoption under a new employee id, F6 design r5 §3.10, Q8, N1, R6–R8, R12, S3, S10): new `duduclaw-gateway/src/isolation_adopt.rs` (refs, errors, journal, request filing, proposal plan/bind, card validation, recovery) + child `isolation_adopt_apply.rs` (allowlist transform, commit, rollback/compensation); tests `isolation_adopt_tests.rs` (child of `isolation_restore_tests`). Also fixed the known compile error `isolation_restore_root.rs:75` (`approver: None`, schema-1 ReadBack, as F6l's own non-retire callers). **Field** `adopt_quarantine = <hex32>:<incarnation>:<digest64>` (`AdoptRef`, canonical lowercase hex, decimal without leading zeros; one field max), allowed in `StagingCheck` and `EmployeeAuthority::validate_current`; F6h's exception is passed as `Some((employee, incarnation))` to `check_label_not_quarantined` in `prepare_propose`'s adoption branch, `stage_prepared` and `validate_current` (after the F6d merge `proposal_refusal` = `check_label_not_quarantined(.., None)` stays only in `prepare_propose`'s non-adoption branch, where no adoption can be involved). **Request** `file_adoption(home, &AdoptEnv, employee, incarnation, target, Filer::{Personal(Option<OwnerProof>), Shared{requester, members}})`: Personal by the owner only (foreign row `NOT_FOUND`, factor required), Shared by an Admin with `members ⊆ snapshot` at ≤ snapshot level (each once; empty allowed = members re-bound later through the normal flow); restore + adoption recovery first; restore's `load_fenced`, `ensure_tombstone_finished`, `check_users` (tenant, `owner_eligible`, Q3), `check_no_open_proposal` (old and target label), `check_target_absent`, `check_quota` (the last three made `pub(crate)`); target free (R6: `check_label_not_quarantined(target, Some(row))`, no live row, no marker); **root**: `isolation_retire::root_precheck` must be `ROOT_GONE` (authoritative `NotFound` or recorded `root_absent`); `Unavailable`/`Ok`/`Retired` refuse `Root(class)`, `Inconsistent` refuses with a Critical `isolation.employee_root_inconsistent`. Journal row in `employee_restores` (`personal_adopt`/`shared_adopt`, `target_label`, `member_selection`), L1 cap, digest `duduclaw-adopt-request-v1` = §3.4 request digest ‖ target. **Proposal**: `isolation.employee_propose` gains optional `adoption_request_id` (no new RPC method; F6d may move it): `prepare_adoption` runs `prepare_proposal` (all checks again, digest, `sealed_inputs` incl. the path check) **before** taking the apply.db writer lock, then label_pending / R7 / binding under it; no directory is created; `stage_prepared` adds the field and, in the same apply.db tx, `bind_proposal` (`proposal_id`, row `sealed → adopting`, `closed_by_request = request id`), audit `isolation.employee_adoption_proposed`. Card facts in `AdoptionPlan.card` (adopted_from, tombstoned_at, data_bytes, kept/sealed names, routes count, SOUL summary ≤ 280 chars). **Approval / apply** (`EmployeeAuthority`): `validate_current` → `validate_item` (Personal same owner `ADOPT_NOT_SAME_OWNER`, Shared same tenant `ADOPT_NOT_SAME_TENANT`, request bound to this proposal or `ADOPT_ALREADY_USED`, digest, adoption apply.db checks, while `requested`: users, root gone, settings == transformed sealed agent.toml; F6c2 `EligibilityLogRule` with kind `personal_adopt`/`shared_adopt`, approver = live Admin while Pending else `ConfirmationStore::approver`, `fresh_step_up = true` since the card approval is step-up gated); after the commit the item validates against its `adopted` marker + v2 snapshot instead of the registry. `stage` = validate → root `Allocate` → `apply_item`: request `applying` (+ approver, `self_approved`), verify in quarantine (M1; mismatch ⇒ row `invalid`), `sealed_inputs` again, transform, hooks (`agent_hook_installer` through `bounded` on the live runtime), `employee_dirs::snapshot` (now `pub(crate)`, re-exported as `handlers::employee_dirs`) must pass and its `settings()` equal the proposal's, `rekey_row` (every `LABEL_KEYED_STORES` entry except the `Digest` org entry — the proposal states the new entry, and the old one stays in the row digest) under `quarantine:<hex>-<inc>`, positive zero count on the old and target labels (`STORES_NOT_EMPTY`), `adopted` marker, `save_confirmed_receipt` (new id, incarnation 0, Personal mirror), Shared `restore_shared_bindings_in_transaction` for a non-empty selection, then one apply.db tx: row `adopted` + `adopted_by_employee`, request `done`, `root_state = root_absent`; audits `isolation.employee_adopted`, `isolation.employee_adoption_approved`, `isolation.employee_root_absent` (operator), `isolation.employee_restore_self_approved` when self-approved. **Transform** (descriptors, never following links): `mkdirat(agents/<target>, 0700)` (exists ⇒ `LABEL_NOT_FREE`), `renameat2(NOREPLACE)` of `SOUL.md`, `CONTRACT.toml`, `SKILLS/` with the moved inode checked against the verification's stat manifest before and after; `agent.toml` and `.claude/settings.json` are written new (originals stay sealed); `ADOPT_SETTINGS_KEYS = [permissions.deny, permissions.ask, model]` (malformed narrowing key refuses: dropping a deny list would widen); `[agent] name` rewritten on a new label by a line rewrite verified by re-parsing (only that value may change); any kept byte sequence equal to `<home>/agents/<old label>` (given and canonical home) refuses `ADOPT_PATH_REJECTED` before anything moves. **Failure / recovery**: any failure after the transform rolls back (moved entries back NOREPLACE, written files and the new dir removed, marker deleted, row `needs_reseal` → restore's reseal → `sealed`/`invalid`, request `failed: <code>`); `disable` (store compensation after the Disable of the new item) calls `compensate_item` (idempotent; also after a committed adoption whose later step failed: `adopted → needs_reseal`). `recover_adoptions(home, env, label)` (run before every request/proposal; F6d must also call it at boot): `applying` ⇒ finish the re-key first (S10); proposal Pending/Approved/Applying ⇒ wait; Applied + live new id + marker names the row ⇒ roll forward; Rejected/Rejecting ⇒ rollback `denied`, ExpiredRejected ⇒ `expired`, Failed/missing ⇒ `failed: interrupted`; Applied without the match ⇒ Critical alert, request `failed: inconsistent`, row stays `adopting`. **Marker** (R8): `ensure_marker_schema` adds `origin TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'created' CHECK (origin IN ('created','adopted'))` and `quarantine_ref` (called from the RPC's `open_requests`); `check_marker` refuses any non-`created` marker; `record_marker` never overwrites an `adopted` one. **Deviations**: (1) `agent.toml` and `settings.json` are written as new files rather than moved, so the sealed originals stay intact for a rollback; (2) `.mcp.json` is not written (it is not in the allowlist and would fail the v2 digest; it is regenerated at spawn by `prepare_mcp_config_for_spawn`); (3) the request RPCs `me.isolation.request_adoption`, `isolation.shared_adoption_request` were **not** added by F6g (F6d owns `isolation_employee_rpc.rs`; engine entry points are `file_adoption`, `prepare_proposal`/`bind_proposal`, `recover_adoptions`) — done at the F6g merge, see below; (4) A7 for adoption: the Personal mirror write and Shared binding insert bump `acl_generation` (and the Shared path the policy epoch); no extra policy-epoch bump for Personal; (5) `agent_cap` is 0 in the production env (license cap read is F6d's, as for restore); (6) the root precheck runs at every Pending validation (each card listing asks the root once). Tests (`adopt_`; not compiled or run here — macOS worktree, no shell; Linux builder): `requires_authoritative_not_found`, `refused_on_inconsistent`, `original_label_when_free`, `new_label_rewrites_agent_name_only`, `keeps_only_allowed_top`, `strips_command_keys_from_settings`, `rejects_absolute_old_label_paths`, `remainder_stays_sealed_and_purged_with_row`, `post_transform_passes_v2_digest`, `never_copies_root_workspace`, `old_id_authorities_dead` (F6m v2 token of the old id refused, new verifies; lifecycle reads the new id at incarnation 0), `label_stores_sealed_not_carried`, `crash_mid_rekey_never_goes_live_with_old_rows`, `records_root_absent`, `personal_same_owner_only`, `shared_same_tenant_only`, `normal_proposal_refuses_adopted_marker`, `digest_mismatch_refused`, `failure_compensates_new_allocation` (real confirmation store `apply_next` with a failing stage ⇒ `disable_and_read_back`), `crash_rolls_back_or_forward`, `single_use_per_row`. **For F6d** (done at the F6g merge except cache eviction and member notices, see below): the two request RPCs (owner Q2 factor / Admin step-up) calling `file_adoption`; `recover_adoptions` at boot for labels with an `adopting` row; registry rescan + cache eviction after the adoption commit (the backend runs inside the confirmation call); notices to the owner/members/approver. **For F6e**: owner card 由新員工接手 (shown only for `ROOT_GONE`), target label input (default original), Shared member checklist with levels ≤ snapshot, the proposal card section from `AdoptionPlan.card` (adopted from / tombstoned date / data size / SOUL summary / kept vs sealed lists / routes to re-provision), and the error codes above. **For F6i**: purge of an `adopted` row deletes the remainder dir and `purge_all(quarantine_key)` (the re-keyed stores). **Fix round F6fb (2026-11-09, review NO-GO items 1, 4, 5, 6, 8)**: (1) **Resume past `save()`**: `validate_item` recognises an interrupted apply of this very item — request `applying`, row `adopting`, the target's confirmed row a new id, live, under the item's binding, its `adopted` marker naming the row and the tree still passing the marker digest and the proposal's settings — as `Phase::Saved` (`check_apply_db_with` accepts that one live row; any other live row on the target is `Inconsistent` before anything moves); `apply_item` then resumes in `resume_after_save` (re-key again, zero count without the principal store, member bindings unless already exactly the selection — `bind_members` is idempotent — then `finish`), never the transform, so the live tree is not deleted and Shared `bind_members` does not fail; `roll_forward` also binds members idempotently (the crash may precede them); audit `isolation.employee_adoption_resumed`. `roll_back` of a Shared adoption removes the target's member bindings first (`lifecycle::remove_shared_bindings`; refuses when a live row on the target is not this adoption's). The zero count includes the users.db bindings (`member_bindings`, see F6h fix round) before `save()`. (4) `Phase::Adopted { employee }`: the target's live row must be this item's (binding) and its id equal `adopted_by_employee`; `apply_item` additionally requires `receipt.employee == employee` and `receipt.binding == item.binding`. (5) Compensation after the commit (row `adopted`) refuses `Inconsistent` with a Critical `isolation.employee_adoption_compensation_refused` unless the new tree's v2 digest still equals the marker. (6) The old-path check (`Needles`) normalises kept bytes and needles (`\/` → `/`, `${HOME}`/`$HOME`/leading `~/` → the process home, `//` → `/`, `/./` → `/`), covers `<home>/agents/<old label>`, `<home>/isolation-quarantine/<row>` and the old root workspace (`/var/lib/duduclaw-spawnd/images/<hex>.img`/`.mnt`), each for the given and the canonical home, and matches whole paths only (start after a non-path byte; end at a separator, quote, whitespace, end, or a final `.`). (8) Licence cap: `isolation.employee_propose` reads `effective_agent_cap` and passes it to `prepare_adoption` (`with_production_env_capped`); `migration_confirmations_rpc` builds `EmployeeAuthority::with_agent_cap(effective_agent_cap)`, and `validate_item` runs `check_quota` while the request is `requested` (approval and the stage before the transform); `with_production_env` (cap 0) is left for boot recovery only (`isolation_restore_rpc.rs` not touched); new zh-TW message for `QUOTA_EXCEEDED`. A crash between the users.db mirror write and the confirmed-row insert inside `save()` is still not resumable (the zero count sees the mirror): it rolls back and the store compensates. A refusal takes the existing failure path (`failed: <code>`, e.g. `CREDENTIAL_CHANGED`; tree back, marker deleted, row resealed; the store compensates the new allocation). New refusal codes (`SharedError`, mapped through new `RpcError::Shared`): `MEMBERS_INVALID` (not in the snapshot, duplicate, malformed, extra fields), `ACCESS_LEVEL_RAISED` (S11), `MEMBERS_REQUIRED` (no active member left), `MEMBERS_CHANGED` (approval: a selected member no longer active, or the stored selection no longer a subset of the snapshot at ≤ its level — terminal, request `failed`), `ROUTE_NOT_OFFERED`, `INVALID_REQUEST`; engine refusals keep their codes (`TENANT_MISMATCH`, `ROUTES_CHANGED`, `STEP_UP_REQUIRED`, `SECOND_ADMIN_REQUIRED`, …). **Filing** (`file_shared_request`): fresh step-up, Shared row only (others `NOT_FOUND`), snapshot `tenant_id` == row tenant, then an explicit pin check (apply.db `confirmed_employees.tenant_id` and users.db installation tenant == row tenant ⇒ else `TENANT_MISMATCH`, before the root precheck, which would only say `NOT_FOUND`), selection ⊆ snapshot at ≤ level once (canonical order by user id), inactive/deleted members dropped and reported (never bound), F6c `recover_label` / `load_fenced` / `check_outside` (all common checks incl. root, store marks, quota), ticks ⊆ the offered tenant routes at the same generation and ⊆ the engine's catalog list; one apply.db IMMEDIATE tx runs `check_apply_db(Stage::Request)`, the L1 cap, inserts the `employee_restores` row (`member_selection` with levels in the F6c request digest; `route_digest` over exactly the ticked `(account, generation)` list) and new table `shared_restore_selections(request_id PK, employee_hex, incarnation, routes, dropped, created_at)`; audited `isolation.employee_restore_requested`; L1-capped notice `isolation.shared_restore_requested` to log-eligible Admins. Tickable routes (`TenantRouteSource`, production `CatalogTenantRoutes`): active `SharedTenant`/`SharedHome` routes of exactly `Shared(tenant)`, never a `needs_details` provider or a route carrying a C6 detail record (per-employee routes need `isolation.connector_provision` again). **Approval** (`isolation_restore_shared::approve`, called by `isolation_restore_rpc::decide`; other kinds go to `engine::approve` unchanged): stored ticks must still give the request's route digest, stored selection re-validated against the snapshot and every member active (else `MEMBERS_CHANGED` + `failed`), pin re-checked; then `engine::approve` with the route source restricted to the ticked accounts (`Ticked`), so a ticked route revoked or re-generated refuses `ROUTES_CHANGED` at tx1 and in tx2 while unticked routes never matter. The approval rule is the F6c2 rule unchanged: §2 SA keeps the sole-Admin self-approval path for Shared (step-up, cooling, `self_approved` audit); tx2's `restore_shared_bindings_in_transaction` re-checks active members inside the users.db write tx (M5). **After restore** (`after_restore`): reply fields above; one never-dropped `isolation.shared_member_rebound` notice per re-bound member to their current address; audit `isolation.shared_restore_routes_reapproved`. **Use-time gate** (one call in `isolation_mcp/authority.rs::connector_with` after `route_use`, skipped for `needs_details` providers which keep their own pins): `tenant_route_gate(home, employee, incarnation, binding, account, kind)` — a Shared employee at incarnation N > 0 whose row N−1 is `restored` by a done `shared_restore` request may use a `SharedTenant`/`SharedHome` route only if that request ticked its account; missing selection ⇒ `Revoked`, unreadable store/table, missing row or a non-Shared row ⇒ `Unavailable`; Personal, incarnation 0 and other kinds pass. **Deviations / open**: ticks bind the account id at use (the generation is bound at request/approval; a later `advance` of a ticked route keeps it usable, a revoke never comes back); Shared *adoption* (F6g, new id at incarnation 0) has no tenant-route ticks yet, so this gate does not restrict it (F6g follow-up); only the per-call authority enforces the ticks — readiness / connector-plan listings may still list unticked tenant routes for a restored Shared employee (each use is refused); notices are queued, not delivered (no mailer). Shared files touched (small, separated): `isolation_restore_rpc.rs` (`RpcError::Shared` + `From`, `decide` calls the wrapper and `after_restore`), `handlers/isolation_employee_restore.rs` (`mod shared`, `handles`, one dispatch arm, gate line), `handlers/isolation_employee_rpc.rs` (`owns`, `handles`), `handlers/gates.rs` (two names), `isolation_mcp/authority.rs` (gate call), `lib.rs`, `isolation_lifecycle_tests.rs` (`mod f6k`). Tests (not compiled or run here; macOS worktree, no shell — Linux builder): `isolation_lifecycle_tests::f6k::{shared_restore_tenant_must_equal_pin, shared_restore_members_only_from_snapshot, shared_restore_inactive_member_dropped, shared_restore_no_auto_rebind, shared_restore_tenant_routes_reapproved_not_reactivated, shared_restore_detail_routes_need_provisioning, shared_restore_second_admin_required, shared_restore_cannot_raise_access_level, shared_restore_members_changed_refused}`, `isolation_restore_shared::unit_tests::{ticks_must_match_offered_route_and_generation_once, ticked_source_sees_only_ticked_accounts, gate_exits_early_only_for_unrestricted_cases_and_fails_closed, shared_error_codes_are_stable_and_distinct}`, `handlers::isolation_employee_rpc::restore::shared::tests::{shared_restore_rpcs_not_listed_to_ai, shared_restore_request_schema_has_no_authority_fields}`.
- From F6i (quarantine purge, F6 design r5 §3.6, §3.8, §3.11, §3.12; Q5, N3, R10, S12): new `duduclaw-gateway/src/isolation_purge.rs` (engine) with children `isolation_purge_tree.rs` (Unix: descriptor-relative removal) and `isolation_purge_rpc.rs` (RPC bodies); handler child `handlers/isolation_employee_purge.rs`. **RPC** `isolation.quarantine_purge` (dashboard-only, `owns()` of `isolation_employee_rpc`, Enterprise-only in `gates.rs`, `system` refused, Admin + current MFA at the gate, then the migration step-up with its CSRF token and `step_up_fresh` of its proof, else `STEP_UP_REQUIRED`; not an MCP tool) `{employee_id, incarnation, confirm_early?: false, retire?: true, csrf_token}` (`deny_unknown_fields`; `retire` = 同時釋放主機資源, checked by default) → ≥ 90 days after the tombstone `{state: purged, employee_id, incarnation, label, early: false, request_id: null, approver_user_id, self_approved, purged_at, removed: {entries, file_bytes}, row_stores: {store: n}, label_stores: {store: n}|null, retire: null|{state: staged, proposal_id, revision, early, approval_required}|{state: root_absent}|{state: retired}|{state: refused, code, message}}`; < 90 days without `confirm_early` `EARLY_PURGE_CONFIRM_REQUIRED`, with it `{state: requested, request_id, kind: early_purge, label, employee_id, incarnation, expires_at, approval_required: true, retire, lost: {seal, seal_digest, manifest: {entries, bytes}|null, store_marks, row_stores, label_stores|null}, notified}` (nothing removed). **Decide** stays `isolation.employee_restore_decide` `{request_id, decision, csrf_token}` (§3.11): the restore handler first calls `isolation_purge::decide_early` with its `LogRule` (step-up freshness of the call) and falls through for every other kind; approve → `{request_id, kind: early_purge, state: done, label, self_approved, purged: {…as above}, retire}`, deny → `{request_id, kind: early_purge, state: denied}`; cancel is the generic `isolation.employee_restore_cancel`. `isolation.employee_list` `restores.quarantine[]` gains `purge: {purgeable, purge_after, early, early_purge, purged_at}` (`isolation_restore_rpc::admin_view` → `extend_view`); the open request shows `kind: early_purge`. New refusal codes (`RpcError::Purge`, zh-TW messages): `PURGE_NOT_ALLOWED` (`pending`/`restored`/`purging`/`purged`), `PURGE_REQUEST_OPEN`, `EARLY_PURGE_CONFIRM_REQUIRED`, `PURGE_CHANGED`, `PURGE_TREE_REFUSED`, `PURGE_INCOMPLETE`; restore codes reused (`RESTORE_IN_PROGRESS` incl. an `adopted` row whose adoption proposal is not Applied yet, `RETIRE_IN_PROGRESS` for any id on the label, `RATE_LIMITED` = L1 3 requests per row per 30 days, `RESTORE_EXPIRED`, `NOT_FOUND`, the F6c2 approval codes). **Eligibility** (`check_purgeable`, outside and again in each apply.db IMMEDIATE tx): row `sealed`/`oversize`/`invalid`/`absent`/`needs_reseal`/`adopted` (`restoring`/`adopting` ⇒ `RESTORE_IN_PROGRESS`), same label/digest/root/seal as read outside, no other row on the label `restoring`/`adopting`/`purging`, no open request on the row (only the approving `early_purge` request), no `applying` request on the label, no `retiring` root of any id on the label. **Early purge (S12)**: `employee_restores` row kind `early_purge` (requester = filing Admin, `owner_user_id` = the Personal owner, `route_digest` of the empty list, `expires_at = min(created + 72 h, tombstone + 90 d)`); additive apply.db table `employee_purge_requests(request_id, retire, preview)`; digest `duduclaw-early-purge-v1` (`put` framing) over request id, kind, employee, incarnation, label, tenant, owner, requester, the preview (seal, seal digest, sealed manifest totals, recorded store marks, counts under the row key and — when they go — under the label) and the retire flag. Approval (`approve_early`): restore + purge recovery first, request `requested` and unexpired, adoption settled, tree inspected, preview recomputed outside the lock, then one apply.db IMMEDIATE tx: eligibility again, digest re-check (mismatch ⇒ request `failed: PURGE_CHANGED`, terminal, audited), the F6c2 rule in a users.db IMMEDIATE tx inside it (M6) through the new `PurgeRule` seam (production `impl PurgeRule for LogRule` = `EligibilityLogRule` with `ApprovalKind::EarlyPurge`, **parties = requester and Personal owner**, so the owner never approves while another qualifying Admin exists; the answer must be `self_approved` exactly when the approver is a party), a self-approval writes F6c2's users.db `isolation.employee_restore_self_approved` row there once per request (an existing row is not written again), request `applying` + approver + `self_approved`, row `purging` + `early_purge = request_id`, journal row; commit; then the purge; request `done`. **Purge** (`finish`, idempotent): apply.db additive table `employee_purge_journal(employee_hex, incarnation, prior_seal, request_id, preview, retire, actor_user_id, started_at, finished_at)` written in the tx that marks `purging`; the tree is removed through the private quarantine root's descriptor (`tree::remove`: `fstatat(AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW)` + `openat(O_NOFOLLOW|O_DIRECTORY)` re-checked against the stat'ed inode, `unlinkat` on the parent, symlinks unlinked never followed, nothing on another filesystem entered (`other_filesystem` refused), own dirs without `u+rwx` made 0700 through the fd, depth ≤ 128, ≤ 4 000 000 entries, raw-byte names, parent fsynced); before anything changes `tree::inspect` requires the recorded root `(dev, ino)` (an `invalid` row: any real directory; an `absent` row: nothing), else Critical `isolation.quarantine_purge_tree_refused` and `PURGE_TREE_REFUSED` with the row unchanged; then F6h `purge_row_stores` (row key; held website grants deleted for good) and, only while no live confirmed row holds the label and no other row on it is open, `purge_all(label)` (its org entry, memory, sessions, tasks, cron, website grants, channel routes, delegation edges, member bindings); positive zero (`label_free`) under the row key and, when purged, under the label (else `PURGE_INCOMPLETE`, row stays `purging`); final tx `purged`, `purged_at`, journal `finished_at`, request `done`; writer-gate cache invalidated. An `adopted` row: only its remainder directory and row-key stores go (the new employee live on the label keeps its tree and state), and only once its adoption proposal is Applied; `adopted_by_employee` stays. **Crash recovery** (§3.6): `recover_purges(home, label, now)` finishes every `purging` row (and a `purged` row whose tree or row-key stores are still present) from its journal before any purge step on the label (`purge` and `approve_early` also run restore's `recover_label` first); a row without its journal is Critical `isolation.quarantine_purge_inconsistent` and refuses; `purge` of the very row a recovery finished returns that purge. Boot: `server.rs` (protected mode, blocking pool, after the adoption recovery) runs `recover_purges_on_boot` for every label with a `purging` row. While `purging`, R7 holds the label, writers are gated and no request can be filed (row not `sealed`). **Retire (§3.12)**: after the purge commits and `retire` is set, the RPC calls F6l `purge_hook` with the production root and `OutboxNotifier`, requester = the Admin of the call (for an early purge: the approver); a refusal never undoes the purge (reported in `retire`). **Audit / notices (M7, S16)**: `isolation.quarantine_purge_requested`, `_refused`, `_failed`, `_approved`, `_self_approved`, `_tree_refused`, `_recovered`, `_inconsistent`, `isolation.quarantine_purged` (employee, incarnation, label, kind, owner, `early`, request id, requester, approver, `self_approved`, prior seal, **full seal digest** + prefix, **sealed manifest totals** `{entries, bytes}` (bytes = the stat manifest's size column, directories included), **store marks**, `removed {entries, file_bytes}`, per-store counts under the row key and the label, the request preview, retire flag, `purged_at`, `recovered`); deny/cancel/expiry use the generic restore events. Uncapped notices (`queue_or_alert`) to every log-eligible Admin address and the tombstone-time `notice_address`: `isolation.quarantine_purge_requested`, `isolation.quarantine_purged`, `_self_approved`, `_denied`. **Shared-file edits** (small, separated): `lib.rs` (module), `isolation_restore_rpc.rs` (`RpcError::Purge` + code/message arms, `admin_view` row map), `handlers/isolation_employee_restore.rs` (DECIDE arm: `decide_early` first), `handlers/isolation_employee_rpc.rs` (`mod purge`, `owns()`, dispatch line), `handlers/gates.rs` + `edition_rpc_gate_table_tests.rs`, `server.rs` (boot block), `isolation_adopt_tests.rs` (`mod purge`). **Deviations**: (1) one RPC `isolation.quarantine_purge` as §3.8/§3.11 name it (no separate `_request`/`_now` methods: age decides); (2) approval of an early purge reuses `isolation.employee_restore_decide` (§3.11), no dedicated decide; (3) the Personal owner is a party of the early-purge approval (same as F6l's early-purged retire card); (4) a purge finished by boot recovery never stages the retire (the Admin proposes it with `isolation.employee_retire_propose`); one finished by the next `isolation.quarantine_purge` of that row returns the recovered purge and stages it when its journal says `retire`; (5) the users.db self-approval row commits before the apply.db tx that starts the purge — a failed apply.db commit leaves the audit row of a decision whose purge did not start (a retry writes none); (6) no preview RPC for the dialog: the request reply's `lost` and the listing's `purge` block are what the UI has. Tests (not compiled or run here: macOS worktree, no shell; Linux builder): gateway `isolation_lifecycle_tests::restore::adopt::purge::{purge_early_files_request_not_purge, purge_early_needs_second_qualifying_admin, purge_early_sole_admin_self_approves_with_cooling_and_audit, purge_early_digest_rechecked_at_approval, purge_early_notifies_owner_and_admins, purge_after_90_days_purges_in_call, purge_refused_with_open_request, purge_no_symlink_follow, purge_removes_tree_and_label_stores, purge_adopted_row_removes_remainder_and_sealed_stores, purge_crash_finishes_first, purge_audit_keeps_digest_and_totals}` (real stores; the F6c2 rule is a stand-in with F6c2's answers, the production `LogRule` mapping is asserted fail closed), `handlers::isolation_employee_rpc::purge::tests::{quarantine_purge_not_listed_to_ai_and_admin_gated, quarantine_purge_schema}`, `edition_rpc_gate_table_tests` (method Enterprise-only). - From SFR (`skill_from_recording` routed in protected mode, branch `feat/iso181-sfr-20261109`; security rounds honoured: artifacts are **untrusted even when the hash matches**, `VerifiedArtifact` is the type seam, recording stays gated off by `RECORDING_BUILD_ENABLED` + the per-host probe): **Source**: gateway `isolation_skill_jobs::StoredRecordings` implements H12d's `tool_policy::RecordingJobs` over HX6's `recording_jobs::RecordingJobs` (`SqliteRecordingJobs`): `parse_id` → owner `read` (employee + principal incl. generation + binding of the record) → row must be the exact `(run, call)` of the id and the record's employee, `complete` (live / `stop_requested` / `uncertain` refuse), the stored row must be the helper's strict `decode_result` shape plus exactly one collector `ended_by` (`stop|revoked|admin|run_end|deadline`), and H12d's `RecordingManifest::from_result` must equal HX6's `artifacts_from_result` (`RecordingManifest::digests`). A recording made in an earlier run of the same employee (flushed at run end) is readable; another employee's, another person's or an older credential generation's is not found. `install_recording_source` is called by `isolation_mcp::boot::assemble` after every fallible producer (one-shot). `ExecutionJobRecordings` (the H12d adapter over `ExecutionJobs`, never installed; recordings are no longer execution jobs since HX6) and `recording_summary_input` were removed. **Read**: `ResourceRoots::read_recording_artifact` now returns `recording_jobs::VerifiedArtifact` — after the bounded brokered read the only digest check is `verify_artifact(&manifest.digests(), …)`; a size or byte mismatch is the new `FileRefusal::Artifact(ArtifactError)` (`verification()` = `BindingMismatch`, `artifact()` accessor; CLI message names `artifact_modified`). Gateway `recording_artifacts(call, roots) -> (RecordingManifest, VerifiedRecording)` reads `trace.zip`, `actions.json`, `network.json` in layout order; `VerifiedRecording::new(id, [VerifiedArtifact; 3])` (private fields, no `Clone`, names must be `recording_layout::ARTIFACTS` in order) is its only constructor. **Parse (E only)**: `summarize_recording(VerifiedRecording, slug)` frames the three verified artifacts with `recording_layout::bundle` (magic `DDCREC1\n` + three LE `u32`-length parts; `unbundle` is strict; `MAX_BUNDLE_BYTES` ≈ 48 MiB < the worker's 64 MiB) — framing only, the host parses nothing — and calls the new `parse_worker::adapters::summarize_recording_artifacts` (`ParseOperation::SummarizeRecording` + new `ParseOptions::RecordingArtifacts { name }`, `ContentFormat::Archive`; contract validation shares the slug rule; result kind `Recording` only). Worker `parsers/recording_artifacts.rs`: `open_trace` checks every entry before reading any (≤ 4096 entries, declared total ≤ 64 MiB, ratio ≤ 100 for entries ≥ 1 KiB, UTF-8 names, `enclosed_name` + no `\\`/`:`/controls/absolute/empty/`.`/`..` components, depth ≤ 8, no symlink/special/mismatched-dir modes, no case-folded duplicates, encrypted entries fail in `by_index`), then decompresses only `trace.trace` (required) and `trace.network` with a hard `take(declared + 1)` bound (≤ 16 MiB each); JSONL lines ≤ 256 KiB, ≤ 20 000 per part. Steps come from the trace's `before` (or legacy `action`) events — fixed method names only, selectors ≤ 120 chars, URLs through `redact_url_query`, typed values never kept (`value=<masked>`) — else from the helper's `actions.json` `{kind, tag}` (tag reduced to ASCII alphanumerics); API calls from the trace's `resource-snapshot` lines as a HAR through the existing `redact_har` / `extract_api_calls`; `network.json` must be `{responses: [...]}` and is otherwise unused; env placeholders only `<env:[A-Z0-9_]{1,100}>`; every page-derived string is `fence_safe` (`<`/`>` → `‹`/`›` except well-formed placeholders, controls → space) so it cannot close or open the prompt's `<recording>` data fences. **Publish**: CLI `mcp_skill_jobs::handle_protected` (the isolated branch of `handle_skill_from_recording` now returns into it; the old isolated sub-branches of the legacy handler are gone; the shared model step is `mcp_recording_distill::distill_with_model`): `recording_input` (same current-call / record checks as H12d) → `summarize` (E) → the employee's own utility model → `admit_background_identity` → gateway `publish_owned`: exact protected arguments, valid non-reserved label (bound by the CLI to the current isolated call), slug `[a-z0-9_-]{1,64}`, ≤ 64 KiB, E `ScanSkill` on the exact bytes (`scan_admits`: High/Critical refuse, nothing written), live check, `io_authority::scope(call.context())` capture, live check again, `write_host_file` no-clobber into `agents/<label>/SKILLS/<slug>.md` (H12c rules; no custom-skill draft or approval card on the protected path, as H12c). **Selector**: `workspace_selection::RECORDING_DISTILL_TOOLS = ["skill_from_recording"]`, `WorkspaceSelectors { helpers, recording, distill }`, `drops_from_signed_selected` (`drops_from_signed_with` delegates with `distill: false`); `skill_from_recording` removed from `PENDING_WORKSPACE_SELECTOR_TOOLS` (now the eight fork/discovery readers only). Gateway `selectors.rs` `Selection.distill` = listed ∧ interactive ∧ `recording_ready()` (spawnd `helper_calls` + `helper_recording`) ∧ `[isolation] public_web` ∧ profile allows the tool; it selects the brokered `Workspace{employee-work}`, signs no `PublicOrigins`, and is part of the B2 re-read. Production spawnd never advertises `helper_recording` while `RECORDING_BUILD_ENABLED = false`, so the tool stays hidden although the store is installed. The CLI protected schema description now says own SKILLS / verified / parsed only in the isolated parse service. **Parity**: the child process installs the source with the production `install_recording_source`; `skill_from_recording` is eligible with exactly `[own Employee, Workspace{employee-work}]` and is in the compared tool inventory; `pending_production_selectors` asserts it is not pending and that every remaining pending tool is one of `FORK_DISCOVERY_READERS` (the list is not empty on this branch: the fork/discovery readers still need their own selectors — once they land the list and that constant are empty). Tripwire `hx6fix_skill_from_recording_leaves_pending_only_with_verify_artifact` is now a compile-time type check (fn-pointer pins of `verify_artifact`, `read_recording_artifact`, `VerifiedRecording::new`, `recording_artifacts`, `summarize_recording`, and the CLI `ProtectedRecording.recording` / `mcp_skill_jobs::summarize` input type); the text scanner `strip_comments_and_strings` and its self-test `hx6r3_tripwire_scan_ignores_comments_and_strings` were removed with the text scan. parse-worker (`--features engine`) `parsers::recording_artifacts::tests::{sfr_trace_archive_summary_is_redacted_and_fenced, sfr_hostile_trace_archive_refused_in_parse_worker, sfr_fence_safe_keeps_only_well_formed_env_placeholders}`, `parsers::tests::sfr_protected_recording_bundle_parses_only_as_archive_input`; - From FD-SEL (Package H tools 22–29, branch `feat/iso181-fdsel-20261109`): production selectors for the last pending protected-mode tools. **Decision — discovery is a selector, not a refusal:** running discovery stays operator-only (CLAUDE.md "operator-only exploration": disposable containers, dedicated account pool, a manager decision receipt before any run starts), but a protected employee already files discovery *requests* through `tasks_create` (kind `discovery`) as `TrustedCaller::from_isolated_agent`, and every such request waits for a manager (an agent principal is never `manager_for`). The five job tools only let it see, download the published checkpoint of, or withdraw/cancel a request it filed itself — they never start, approve or widen a run — so refusing them would only hide the employee's own pending requests. **Boundary** (`mcp-boundary/src/tool_policy_own_reads.rs`, `pub mod tool_policy::own_reads`, re-exported `OWN_READ_TOOLS`/`OWN_DISCOVERY_TOOLS`/`OWN_FORK_READ_TOOLS`, `own_read_selector`, `check_own_read_arguments`): the eight policies are `required: [Employee]` (was `[Employee, Workspace]`, never issued); `eligible` = exactly the record's own Employee grant (no Workspace, Task, scope or resolver output; `authorize` returns that selection without consulting the resolver); exact protected schemas (`run_id`/`fork_id`/branch/file ids opaque ≤ 128 B `[A-Za-z0-9_-]`, `agent_id` label, `limit` 1..=100, optional keys may be `null`; `diff_branches` uses the protected listing's `left_branch_id`/`right_branch_id`; identity/run/root/workspace keys refuse). `workspace_selection::PENDING_WORKSPACE_SELECTOR_TOOLS` = `["skill_from_recording"]` only (H12d tripwire; a pending selector, not a refusal). **Discovery own-jobs binding** (gateway `discovery/service.rs`, `store_isolated_creators.rs`): `TrustedCaller` carries `IsolatedCreator { employee, incarnation }` — the employee id from the verified call (`isolation_egress::CallIdentity::employee`), the incarnation from `isolation_lifecycle::confirmed_row` only while the label's live receipt is that very employee and not disabled (else `discovery requires a verified isolated caller`); `create_inner` writes `discovery_isolated_creators(task_id, run_id, employee, incarnation)` (new table in `discovery.db`, insert-only, never rebound) before the approval card and the task row; `authorize_task` additionally requires an exact binding match for a protected caller, and every protected refusal (foreign, unknown, another incarnation, a recreated label, an ordinary signed/RPC request, a pre-FD-SEL request) is `DISCOVERY_NOT_FOUND` = `discovery not found`; RPC/signed callers keep `permission denied`. CLI `mcp/discovery.rs::protected_refusal`: under an isolated call the exact schema and `own_read_selector` run before the service. **Fork reads** (`forks.rs`): `ForkStore::inspect`/`diff` use the new actor-only `owned_branch` (exact actor = employee id, principal and binding; no workspace grant — the issuer never signs branch handles), `branch()` (launch/cancel/publication) still requires the branch workspace grant, `select`/`request_cancel` unchanged; `ForkHostControl` applies `check_own_read_arguments`; CLI `mcp_fork::protected_fork_error` answers every non-`Unavailable` refusal of a read with `FORK_NOT_FOUND` (`fork_not_found：…`), so a foreign fork reads exactly like an unknown one; mutations keep `ForkNextRelease`.
